# NAS Workspace

A self-hosted, Google Workspace-style office suite built to run on a Synology DS224+ (or any Docker host). One server hosts **several separate companies**, each with its own domains, users and admins.

| App | What it does |
|---|---|
| **Drive** | Folders, uploads up to 4 GB each (drag and drop), whole folders too (**New → Upload folder**, or drop a folder from your computer: its folders and files arrive as they were, into folders of the same name if they are already there), previews (images, PDF, video, audio, text, and Word, Excel and PowerPoint files shown as Docs, Sheets and Slides would show them, without making a copy), rename, move, trash and restore (the trash empties itself after 30 days, or as long as the company chooses), search, sharing with people as viewer, commenter or editor (folder shares apply to everything inside), share links for people without an account, version history, templates (**New → From a template…**: ready-made meeting notes, letter, project brief, monthly budget, task tracker and project update; admins can make any document, spreadsheet or presentation a **company template** from its ⋮ menu, for everyone in the company to start from), and access as a network drive (WebDAV) from Mac, Windows and other apps |
| **Docs** | Rich-text documents (headings, lists, checklists with tick boxes, dropdown chips for a status or priority that anyone editing changes with a click, date chips (@today, @date), file chips (@ and part of a file's name), email drafts (@email) and event drafts (@event), equations (LaTeX) and special characters, citations with an APA references list, page breaks (Ctrl+Enter; a new page when printed, saved as PDF or downloaded for Word), footnotes (**¹ Footnote** puts a number in the text and the cursor in its note at the end of the document; numbers follow the order in the text, clicking one goes to its note, and they are real footnotes in Word files both ways, numbered links in HTML and a list of notes when printed), links, quotes, code, alignment, text colour and highlight, tables, pictures, and a table of contents that lists the headings as links and keeps itself up to date — kept as links in HTML and Word downloads), edited together live with everyone's cursors shown, print/PDF, HTML and Markdown export (⋮ → **Download as Markdown (.md)**: headings, bold/italic/strikethrough, links, lists, task lists, quotes, code, tables and footnotes; a .md file in Drive opens as a document the same way) |
| **Sheets** | Spreadsheets with formulas (lookups `XLOOKUP XMATCH VLOOKUP HLOOKUP INDEX MATCH CHOOSE ROW COLUMN ROWS COLUMNS`; conditions `IF IFS SWITCH IFERROR IFNA COUNTIF(S) SUMIF(S) AVERAGEIF(S) MAXIFS MINIFS`, with `*` and `?` wildcards; `SUM AVERAGE MIN MAX MEDIAN COUNT COUNTA COUNTBLANK COUNTUNIQUE PRODUCT SUMPRODUCT LARGE SMALL RANK STDEV(.S/.P) VAR(.S/.P) MODE PERCENTILE QUARTILE GEOMEAN AVERAGEA`; trends `CORREL RSQ SLOPE INTERCEPT FORECAST(.LINEAR)`; `ROUND ROUNDUP ROUNDDOWN MROUND CEILING FLOOR QUOTIENT GCD LCM FACT COMBIN EVEN ODD ABS SIGN SQRT POWER MOD INT TRUNC LOG LN EXP ISODD ISEVEN RAND RANDBETWEEN PI`; names and formulas of your own `LET LAMBDA MAP BYROW BYCOL REDUCE SCAN MAKEARRAY` (=LET(total, SUM(B:B), B2/total); =MAP(A2:A9, LAMBDA(x, x*1.06)) fills a column; written the way Excel reads them in .xlsx files); loans and cash flows `PMT PV FV RATE NPER NPV IRR IPMT PPMT CUMIPMT`, depreciation `SLN DDB DB`; distributions and tests `NORM.DIST NORM.INV T.DIST T.TEST CHISQ.TEST BINOM.DIST` and more; database functions `DSUM DCOUNT DAVERAGE`; text `CONCAT TEXTJOIN JOIN SPLIT REGEXMATCH REGEXEXTRACT REGEXREPLACE CLEAN FIXED LEN UPPER LOWER PROPER TRIM LEFT RIGHT MID FIND SEARCH SUBSTITUTE REPT EXACT VALUE TEXT CHAR CODE`; dates and times `TODAY NOW DATE TIME YEAR MONTH DAY HOUR MINUTE SECOND WEEKDAY WEEKNUM ISOWEEKNUM DAYS DATEDIF EDATE EOMONTH NETWORKDAYS WORKDAY NETWORKDAYS.INTL WORKDAY.INTL`; `AND OR NOT ISBLANK ISNUMBER ISTEXT ISERROR NA`); while you type a formula, matching function names are suggested (arrow keys, then Tab or Enter) and, inside a function's brackets, what it takes is shown with the current argument in bold, merged cells (⊞ joins the selected cells into one, keeping the top-left value; selections and arrow keys treat a merge as one cell; it moves with inserted or deleted rows and columns, sorting across it is refused, and it's kept in Excel files), find and replace (Ctrl+F / Ctrl+H: by what cells show, match case, whole cell, or inside formulas; formulas are only changed when you search them), charts, sorting, inserting and deleting rows and columns, undo, cell formatting (bold, italic, underline, strikethrough, text and fill colour, alignment, wrapped text, borders — all, outer, bottom or none — and number, currency, percent and date formats — dates as 7 Nov 2026, 07/11/2026, Saturday, 7 November 2026, 2026-11-07 or Nov 2026 — all kept in Excel files, where dates are real dates), live co-editing with everyone's selected cell shown, multiple sheets, copy/paste with reference shifting, and CSV import/export |
| **Slides** | Presentations: slide layouts, text boxes, pictures, shapes, lines and arrows you drag and resize, tables (**Table**, then double-click to type in the cells; add or remove rows and columns, a coloured heading row, text size; kept in PowerPoint files both ways), text size, bold, italic and colour, four themes, live co-editing, full-screen presenting, and printing or saving as PDF |
| **Calendar** | Month, week, day, year and schedule views (the schedule lists four weeks of events, tasks and birthdays day by day; timed events placed by the hour, side by side when they overlap, with a current-time line), all-day and timed events, repeating events, inviting colleagues and people outside by email (Yes / Maybe / No), colours, upcoming list |
| **Contacts** | Personal address book (import and export vCard and CSV, synced with phones), plus a directory of everyone on the NAS; addresses suggested while writing mail |
| **Chat** | Channels and direct messages, delivered live over WebSockets: unread counts, reactions, editing your messages, pictures and files, and an emoji picker |
| **Forms** | Questionnaires, sign-up sheets and polls: over a dozen question types (file uploads and signatures too), a link for your company (signed in, names recorded) or for anyone, a live summary of the answers, CSV download and a copy in Sheets |
| **Notes** | Quick notes and checklists on coloured cards: pin, label, archive, search, and share with colleagues to keep one together |
| **Meet** | Video meetings in the browser for up to 8 people: a link and a code to join by, camera and microphone check before joining, screen sharing, raised hands, reactions and a meeting chat |
| **Mail** | A mailbox for every user on their company's domains: webmail (read, reply, forward, attachments from your computer or Drive, archive, spam, search, stars), rich-text writing (bold, lists, links, quotes), drafts that save automatically, vacation auto-replies, filter rules, signatures, folders, send as aliases you belong to, and IMAP/SMTP for phones and Outlook |
| **Admin** | Create, disable, reset or delete users; per-user storage use; company settings; all companies (platform owner) |

## Companies (multi-tenant)

| Role | Can do |
|---|---|
| **Platform owner** (the account created on first run) | Add and delete companies, assign domains, set storage quotas, manage any company's users |
| **Company admin** | Manage their own company's users (addresses on their company's domains only), rename the company, switch external collaboration on or off |
| **User** | Use the apps; sees only their own company's directory and chat channels |

- **Sign-in:** everyone signs in with their email address, e.g. `anna@company.com`. The domain must belong to their company, and the same address will become their mailbox in the mail phase.
- **Isolation:** companies can't see each other's directory, channels, users or files.
- **Forgot password:** in **Settings → Recovery email**, anyone can add a personal address (not their work one; their password is asked for). Then **Forgot password?** on the sign-in page emails a link to that address, from their own work address; it works once, for an hour, and asks for a new password that follows the company's rules. Using it signs them out everywhere and removes their mail-app password, and is recorded in the activity log. The page says the same whether or not an account or address exists, and only three links an hour are sent for one account. It needs email set up on the server; without a recovery address, an admin resets the password as before.
- **Password rules:** Admin → Company settings → **Password rules** sets the shortest password (8 to 20 characters), and whether it needs **letters and numbers**, **a symbol**, and **nothing easy to guess** (well-known passwords like *Summer2026!*, number runs, or one made of the person's own name or address). They apply whenever a password is chosen: by the person, by an admin creating or resetting an account, or in a CSV import; the rules are shown where people choose one. **Passwords expire** (after 30, 60, 90 or 180 days, or a year) asks people whose password is older than that to choose a new one before going on. Existing passwords keep working until then.
- **Sign-in sessions:** Admin → Company settings → **Sign-in sessions** signs people out of Workspace after a time **without activity** (15 minutes to 8 hours: no typing, clicking or scrolling in Workspace; the app refreshing itself doesn't count, and a tab closed that long ago counts too) and/or after a sign-in has lasted **at most** so long however active (8 or 12 hours, a day, 3 days or a week; otherwise 30 days, renewed while used). Handy for shared or front-desk computers. People signed out for being away see why on the sign-in page. Phones and mail apps aren't affected.
- **Changing someone's address:** Admin → Users → ⋮ → **Change email address**, to another name or another of the company's domains (for example after a marriage or a rebrand). Their mailbox, files, calendar and chats stay as they are. Aliases they're in follow the new address, and the old address can stay as an alias, so mail sent to it still arrives and they can still send as it. They sign in with the new address from then on, also in mail apps (update the account there).
- **Adding many people at once:** Admin → Users → **Import from CSV** takes a spreadsheet saved as CSV, one person per row, with columns **Name** (or First name and Last name) and **Email** (a full address, or just the part before @ on the domain you choose), plus optional **Password** and **Admin** (yes). **Check** shows each row as OK or what is wrong (a taken address, another company's domain, a short password, a repeat). **Create** then adds the good rows. People without a password get an easy-to-type one, shown once with a **Download sign-in details** button so you can pass them on. Up to 500 at a time. Each one is recorded in the activity log.
  - The file can also have **Job title**, **Department**, **Work phone** and **Manager** (their email address, or the part before @) columns, for the Directory and org chart. A row for someone already in the company updates their job details instead of adding them again (an empty cell leaves what's there; their password and role don't change), so a file from **Export** (which has these columns too) can be edited and brought back in. A manager who isn't in the company, or one that would make a loop, is skipped with a note on that row.
- **Last active:** Admin → Users shows when each person last used their account (signing in, using Workspace, or a phone or Outlook syncing their calendar and contacts), kept after they sign out. **Only people inactive for 90 days or more** narrows the list to accounts that may no longer be needed (never used ones too; disabled ones are left out).
**Someone's devices:** Admin → Users → ⋮ → **Signed-in devices** shows where a person is signed in on the web (browser and system, address, when last active) and signs out one device or all of them, for a lost laptop or a shared computer left signed in. Phones and mail apps keep working until their password is reset. Each sign-out is in the activity log.

**Choosing their own password:** when an admin adds someone (or resets a password), **Ask them to choose their own password** (on by default) means the password given only lets them choose a new one: at sign-in they see *Choose your own password*, and nothing else works until they have (it can't be the same one). People added with **Import from CSV** get made-up passwords and always choose their own the same way.

Admin → Users can act on **several people at once**: tick them (or the box at the top for everyone shown, e.g. only the inactive ones) and choose **Suspend** (after confirming: they're signed out everywhere and can't sign in until restored; their mail keeps arriving), **Restore**, **Sign out everywhere**, **Set department…** or **Add to group…**. Your own account is skipped where it doesn't apply, and each change is in the activity log.

- **The list of people:** Admin → Users → **Export as CSV** downloads everyone in the company with their address, whether they're an administrator, active or disabled, two-step verification, Drive storage used, when the account was made and when it was last used. It opens in Excel or Sheets; names that look like formulas stay plain text. Exports show in the activity log.
- **Reused addresses start empty:** when someone is deleted or renamed, their mailbox stays on the server under its old folder, and a new person who later gets that address gets a fresh, empty mailbox. (This applies to deletions from this version on.)
- **Working across companies:** a user can share a file or folder, or start a direct message, with someone at another company by typing their **exact email address**. This works only while **both** companies allow external collaboration. If either admin switches it off, outside access ends immediately; switching it back on restores it. Shares show an "External · Company" badge.
- **Quotas:** set per company by the platform owner (0 = unlimited). Within a company, an admin can also give one person a **Drive storage limit** (Admin → Users → ⋮ → **Drive storage limit**): their files, trash and older versions count, uploads and copies past it are refused with a message saying so (files sent to them through file requests and form file questions too), and they see it under **Storage** in Drive.

When two people edit the same doc or sheet, changes are pushed live. If both edit at once, the second person sees a conflict banner ("load theirs" or "keep mine") instead of silently losing work.

## Architecture

```
Browser (React SPA) ──HTTP/WebSocket──▶ Node.js server (Express) ──▶ SQLite  (data/workspace.db)
                                              │                └──▶ files    (data/files/<uuid>)
                                              │ writes accounts/aliases/DKIM keys to mail-config/
                                              │ webmail over IMAP 993 / SMTP 465
                                              ▼
Internet ◀── SMTP 25 ──▶  mail container: Postfix (SMTP) · Dovecot (IMAP, mailboxes) · Rspamd (spam filtering, DKIM signing)
Phones/Outlook ── IMAP 993 / SMTP 465·587 ──▶ (same container)
```

- **Two containers, about 250 to 300 MB of RAM in total.** The app needs 60 to 120 MB, with no separate database server, which matters on the DS224+'s 2 GB. The mail container measured about 160 MB, most of it Rspamd.
- **Mail server:** `mail/`. Standard, battle-tested programs (Postfix, Dovecot, Rspamd) in an Ubuntu 24.04 image with configuration written for this app. The app owns all accounts and settings: it writes plain tables to `mail-config/`, and the mail container picks up changes within 5 seconds. It reloads Postfix, Dovecot or Rspamd only when what that program reads has actually changed, judged by the copy it reads, so a change the app makes while the container is copying is loaded on the next round rather than missed. Mail the app sends in those few seconds (a password-reset email for someone just added, say) is tried again for up to about 12 seconds if the mail server doesn't know the account or address yet. Users' passwords are never stored in readable form. Webmail opens mailboxes with a server-held secret (a Dovecot "master user"), and phones use the user's own password, which is kept in step automatically.
- **Server:** `server/src`. Node 22 with the built-in `node:sqlite` module, so there are no native modules to compile. Express 5 and `ws` are the only dependencies.
- **Web:** `web/src`. React 18, Vite, TipTap (the Docs editor) and a custom formula engine (`web/src/apps/sheets/formula.js`).
- **Security:**
  - Passwords are hashed with scrypt.
  - Sessions use HttpOnly SameSite cookies, which are Secure automatically when served over HTTPS.
  - Write requests must carry a CSRF header.
  - Sign-in protection covers both the web and mail apps; see [Sign-in protection](#sign-in-protection).
  - Uploaded HTML and SVG files are never rendered inline on the app's origin.
  - Every file access checks permissions up the folder chain.
  - HTML emails are shown in a sandboxed frame with no scripts and no access to the app.
  - Remote images in emails are blocked until the reader clicks "Show images", which stops tracking pixels.
  - Email attachments always download rather than open in the browser.
  - Users can only send from their own address or an alias they belong to; this is enforced by the mail server itself.

## Install on a Synology DS224+

Requires DSM 7.2+ and **Container Manager** (from Package Center).

1. **Create folders.** In File Station, inside the `docker` shared folder, create `workspace/app`, `workspace/data`, `workspace/mail-config`, `workspace/mail-status` and `workspace/mailboxes`.
2. **Copy the code.** Upload this whole project into `docker/workspace/app`, so that `docker-compose.yml` sits directly in it.
3. **Set the user ID.** Enable SSH (Control Panel → Terminal & SNMP), connect with `ssh you@NAS-IP` and run `id`. Put your `uid` and `gid` into the `user:` line of `docker-compose.yml` (the default `1026:100` is the usual first admin account on DSM). At the top of the file, set your **mail hostname** (e.g. `mail.yourcompany.com`) and **time zone**.
4. **Build and start.** In Container Manager, go to **Project → Create**:
   - Name: `workspace`
   - Path: `/volume1/docker/workspace/app`
   - Choose "Use existing docker-compose.yml", then click Next and Done. The first build takes a few minutes.
5. **Create the workspace.** Open `http://NAS-IP:8080`. The first visit asks for your company name, its domain and your account, and that account becomes the **platform owner**. Then use **Admin → All companies** to add other companies (each gets its own first admin), and **Admin → Users** to add people.

On 2 GB of RAM, both containers fit with room to spare. Adding a RAM stick only matters if you run other heavy containers. Don't install Synology's own MailPlus or Mail Server packages alongside this, because they use the same ports (25, 465, 587, 993).

## Publish it securely on your fixed IP

1. **DNS:** at your domain registrar, add an A record, e.g. `workspace.example.com → <your fixed IP>`. One hostname serves every company. If you like, each company can also get its own name (e.g. `office.company.com`), added as an extra reverse-proxy entry and certificate pointing at the same port 8080.
2. **Router:** forward TCP **443** and **80** to the NAS. Port 80 is needed only for Let's Encrypt renewals. Do **not** forward 8080 or the DSM ports (5000/5001).
3. **Certificate:** Control Panel → Security → Certificate → Add → *Get a certificate from Let's Encrypt* for `workspace.example.com`.
4. **Reverse proxy:** Control Panel → Login Portal → Advanced → Reverse Proxy → Create:
   - Source: `HTTPS`, host `workspace.example.com`, port `443`, and enable HSTS.
   - Destination: `HTTP`, `localhost`, port `8080`.
   - Custom Header tab → Create → **WebSocket**. This is required for live chat and live updates.
5. **Assign the certificate:** back in Certificate → Settings, assign the new certificate to that reverse proxy entry.
6. **Firewall (recommended):** Control Panel → Security → Firewall. Allow 80 and 443 from everywhere, and allow DSM ports only from your LAN.

Cookies switch to `Secure` automatically once traffic arrives over HTTPS through the proxy.

## Accessibility

Pictures in documents and presentations can be given **Alt text** for screen readers. Workspace is checked against WCAG 2 (levels A and AA) with axe-core on 65 screens (Mail (inbox, a message, writing, its settings), Drive, Docs, Sheets, Slides, Calendar, Chat, Tasks, Contacts, Notes, Forms, Meet, Home, Settings, Admin, the sign-in page and the main dialogs; also a form's Theme and Import questions windows, a themed form as people fill it in, Admin → Announcements and Reports (chart and table, and its Video meetings figures), an event (also with **Running late** and who is), a task's details and a page with an announcement showing, Home's *Comments for you*, Drive with its 💬 new-comment marks, the **Copy to…** and **Rename** (several) windows, and the comments panel with *New* threads, **For you** and a comment being edited, Drive's **Activity**, Admin → **Share links**, Drive's **Shared by link** and a Share window's link with its 🔑 🔔 ✉ buttons, Home's Chat and note reminder cards, Chat's **Reminders** and note, task and event cards with their tick boxes and answer buttons, Notes' reminder window with **Repeat**, Meet's lobby with its camera, microphone and speaker choices and level meter, and, in a meeting, its controls with the ⚙, **People** (host settings), chat (with a kept-chat note), **Polls**, **Questions** and whiteboard panels, open breakout rooms (the host's view and a room), and a guest without an account asking to join (their page, their waiting screen and the host's *Waiting to join*); in light and dark mode), with no problems found: everything can be reached by keyboard and has a name screen readers read out, and text stands out enough from its background (avatar initials included: each colour is made dark enough for its white letters). The same goes for buttons and badges in a company's own colour: their text is white or near-black, whichever reads on it, and every form theme colour carries white text.

Lists that open as you type or press a key are checked open too: the search box at the top (with its tips, recent searches and results, light and dark), Chat's **/** commands (in a conversation and in a thread), the Calendar's quick add with what it understood and the event it opens (guests and room chosen), Mail's **Move to** and **Label as** pickers (also with nothing matching) and its address suggestions while writing, Chat's *@* suggestions and **Jump to**, Sheets' function suggestions and a dropdown cell's choices, the Docs *@* menu, and a form's file question with its kinds of file and size (in the editor, and on the form with a file just refused). On a highlighted choice, its grey details (an address, what a function does) take the highlight's text colour, so they stay readable in both light and dark. In each, the list holds only its choices; buttons such as *Clear recent searches* or *Hide tips* and notes such as *Nothing found* sit under it, where screen readers don't take them for choices.

## Install as an app (phones and computers)

Once Workspace is published over HTTPS (above), it can be installed like an app, with its own icon and window, without an app store:

- **iPhone / iPad:** open it in Safari → Share → **Add to Home Screen**.
- **Android:** Chrome's menu → **Install app** (or **Add to Home screen**).
- **Windows / Mac:** in Chrome or Edge, the install icon in the address bar (or menu → **Install Workspace**). The installed app's icon offers **Mail**, **Calendar**, **Drive** and **Chat** shortcuts (right-click it, or press and hold on Android).

On a phone the screens fit its width: the menus become strips you swipe along, Mail's folders sit in a strip under **Compose** and writing a message takes the whole screen, a chat message's buttons (react, reply, …) appear when you tap it, and the formatting tools of documents and spreadsheets fold away behind **Formatting ▾** (a presentation's behind **Tools ▾**) so the page has the room. It opens even without a connection, with a note at the bottom saying so; it reconnects by itself when the network is back (documents, spreadsheets and presentations you had open keep your changes meanwhile). Only the app itself is kept on the device: mail, files and everything else always come from the server and are never stored by it. Over plain HTTP on the local network it still works in the browser, just without installing or opening offline.

**The app's icon** shows how many unread emails and notifications you have (Android, iPhone with notifications on, Windows and Mac; it updates when a notification arrives, and fully when Workspace opens), and clears as you read them.

**Share to Workspace:** once installed on an Android phone, Workspace is in the **Share** menu of other apps: share a photo, a PDF from an email, or a link, and Workspace opens with **Save to Drive** (choose My Drive, a folder or a shared drive you can edit). What's shared waits on the phone itself until you save it (or **Cancel**), then it's uploaded like any file; a link or text on its own is saved as a small text file. (iPhone and iPad don't offer installed web apps in the Share menu.)

**Notifications on your phone:** in the bell's **Notification settings**, **Turn on notifications on this device** (and allow them when the browser asks). From then on, what lands in your bell (mentions, tasks, invitations, shared files, bookings and the rest you've left on) also reaches that device, even with Workspace closed, until you **Turn off** there. Not while notifications are paused or in your quiet hours. On iPhone and iPad it needs Workspace added to the Home Screen first (iOS 16.4 or later). They go through the browser maker's push service (Apple, Google, Mozilla, Microsoft), encrypted so that the service can't read them; the NAS needs to reach the internet for that, and the server's signing key is made by itself on first use. A device that stops taking them (the app removed) is forgotten. The same settings list **every device** you've turned them on for (*Chrome on Android · this device*, when it was last notified): **Remove** stops them on one you no longer have. They're also tied to being signed in there: signing that device out (Settings → **Signed-in devices**, or **Sign out all other devices**, or an admin doing it, or the sign-in expiring) stops them too, since notifications show what people wrote; and a turned-off account gets none.

Admins who'd rather keep what's written off lock screens tick **Keep what's written off lock screens** in Admin → Company settings: notifications then say who or what it's from (*HR Team · New email*, *Calendar reminder · In 10 minutes*, *Kim Park · Sent you a message*), not the subject, message, event or task name.

Calendar reminders come too, at the time you set on each event (*Design review — In 10 minutes · Room 4*; for an event with a video meeting, *Video meeting: tap to join*, and tapping it opens the meeting; repeating events every time, not ones you declined), when Workspace isn't open on any of your devices (when it is, it shows them itself). Tasks too: from 9:00 each morning (your phone's time) what's due today and overdue (*2 tasks are due today, and 1 is overdue*), and a task with a due time when that time comes. Direct messages in Chat come too (who from and what they wrote; not for a conversation you muted, and once only when they @mention you); **Direct messages in Chat (on devices with notifications on)** turns them off. New email comes too, the moment it's delivered (sender and subject; tapping it opens the message): for the Inbox and any folders you chose **Notify me about new mail** for (a folder’s ⋮ menu in Mail). **New email (on devices with notifications on)** in the bell's notification settings turns that off. The mail server tells Workspace about each delivery on its internal address, with the same key as the sign-in protection; mail your own mail apps save (sent copies) doesn't count.

## Editing documents together

Everyone who can edit a document, spreadsheet or presentation can work in it at the same time, like in Google Docs, Sheets and Slides.

- **Live:** what others type shows up as they type, and simultaneous changes merge character by character (Yjs), so nobody's work is overwritten. Each person's cursor shows in their colour, with their name, and the people who have the document open are shown at the top.
- **Saving:** there's no Save button and no "someone else changed this" conflict. The server saves a couple of seconds after each change, and when the last person closes the document.
- **Undo** (Ctrl+Z, or the ↶ button; redo with Ctrl+Y or Ctrl+Shift+Z) takes back only your own changes, not your colleagues', in documents, spreadsheets and presentations alike.
- **Offline:** keep typing. Your changes are kept in the browser and merged in when the connection is back, even after the server restarts.
- **Viewers** see changes live but can't type.
- **Version history** keeps a version each time a different person starts editing, or after a 10-minute pause. Restoring a version reloads it for everyone who has the document open.
  - In Docs, Sheets and Slides, **⋮ → Version history** lists them beside a preview of the one you pick (the document as written, the spreadsheet's values, or the slides), with **Restore this version**. What was there before a restore is kept as a version too.
  - For documents, **Show changes since then** marks what has changed from that version to now, paragraph by paragraph: added words in green, removed words struck through, edited paragraphs flagged at the side, and a count of words added and taken out.
  - For spreadsheets, it lists each cell typed in, changed or cleared since then, sheet by sheet (what it was and what it is now, formulas as typed), and sheets added, renamed or deleted. For presentations, it lists each slide added, deleted, moved or changed, with what changed on it: text (then → now), boxes added or removed, speaker notes, and changes to the look.
  - **Name current version** (or name any earlier one, such as "Sent to the client") marks it in the list; named versions are kept for good, however many come after.
- **Sheets** works the same way, cell by cell: two people changing different cells at once both keep their change, and the same cell keeps the later change. Each person's selected cell is outlined in their colour. Formats, column widths and new sheets are shared too.
- **Slides** too: slides and the boxes on them merge one by one, so two people working on different boxes (or slides) both keep their changes; the same box keeps the later change. Each person's selected box is outlined in their colour.

## Zoom and spell check (Docs)

- **Zoom:** the box at the bottom right of a document shows it smaller or bigger (50% to 200%), for a small laptop screen or a big monitor. It's kept with your account, so every document opens at that size for you, on any device. It's only how you see it: others see their own size, and printing and downloads aren't affected. People who can only view a document can zoom too.
- **Spell check:** misspelt words are underlined by your browser, in its own languages (right-click one for suggestions). **⋮ → Preferences → Underline misspelt words** turns it off for you, in every document.

## Tabs (Docs)

- **⋮ → Add tab** splits a document into tabs, each with its own pages (chapters of a handbook, a tab per meeting, *Plan* and *Budget*): what was there becomes *Tab 1*, and a new tab opens to write in. The **Tabs** panel beside the document lists them (▤ in the header shows or hides it); click one to open it, or **＋** to add another (up to 50).
- **A tab's ⋮** renames it, duplicates it (with everything in it), moves it up or down, or deletes it (Undo or version history brings it back). With one tab left, the document goes back to having none. **Copy link to tab** (viewers have it too) gives a link that opens the document on that tab.
- **Outline:** with tabs, the outline lists the open tab's headings; open another tab to see its own. Links to headings work across tabs (they open the heading's tab).
- **One document:** everyone sees the same tabs and edits them together live; which tab is open is each person's own. Comments, suggestions, find and replace, word count, version history and search cover every tab. **Find** opens the tab with the match, clicking a comment opens the tab its text is in, and moving the cursor past the end of a tab (or following a link to a heading in another) opens that tab.
- **Downloads and printing:** Word, Markdown, HTML and printing (or saving as PDF) have every tab, one after the other, each starting on a new page under its name as a heading. Previews and earlier versions show every tab under its name.

## Suggesting (Docs)

Switch **✎ Editing** to **💡 Suggesting** at the top of a document to propose changes instead of making them, like track changes in Word:

- What you type shows **green and underlined**; what you delete stays in the text, **red and struck through**. Typing over a selection suggests a replacement. Hovering shows who suggested it. Everyone with the document open sees suggestions as they're made.
- **💡 (number)** at the top lists the suggestions in order, with who made each and when ("Replace “March” with “April”", "Add …", "Delete …"). Click one to go to it. Anyone who can edit can **Accept** (the change goes in) or **Reject** (it's undone) each one, or **Accept all** / **Reject all**.
- Deleting your own suggested text simply removes it. Backspace at the start of a paragraph still joins paragraphs, and formatting, new paragraphs, pictures and tables go in directly: only text is tracked.
- Until a suggestion is accepted it isn't part of the document: Markdown and HTML downloads and printing show the text without pending suggestions. **Download as Word** keeps them as Word's **tracked changes** (insertions and deletions, by whoever suggested them and when), to accept or reject in Word; and opening a Word file with tracked changes turns them into suggestions here, by the same names, to accept or reject in Docs (a deletion and the insertion right after it, by the same person, come in as one replacement).

## Tables and pictures (Docs)

- **Tables:** **Table** → *Insert table*, then Tab to move from cell to cell. The same menu adds and deletes rows and columns, turns the heading row on or off, and deletes the table.
- **Drawings:** **✎ Drawing** opens a sketch pad for a quick diagram: draw freehand, or drag out lines, arrows, boxes and ellipses, in six colours and four sizes (eraser, **Undo**, **Clear**). **Insert** puts it in as a picture, saved in Drive next to the document like other pictures (so it's in Word downloads and prints too).
- **Pictures:** **Picture** to choose one, or paste or drop it into the document. It's saved in Drive next to the document (or in your Drive), and everyone who can open the document sees it, even without access to the picture file itself. Click a picture to make it smaller (a quarter, half or three quarters of the page width) or put it in the centre or on the right. **Alt text** (with the picture selected) describes it for people who can't see it; screen readers read it out instead of the picture, and Word files carry it both ways (a new picture starts with its file name).
- **Fonts and spacing:** the toolbar's font, size and spacing menus set the font (Arial, Georgia, Times New Roman, Verdana, Trebuchet MS, Courier New) and size of the selected text, and the line spacing (single, 1.15, 1.5, double) of the paragraphs the cursor is in. **Default** takes them off again. They're kept in the Word download.
- **Colour:** the **A** and highlight buttons colour the selected text or mark it with a highlight.

- **@mentions:** type **@** and a few letters of a colleague's name, then Enter (or click) to put them in as a chip. They get a notification linking to the document (once in a while, not for every mention). If they can't open it, a note says so; if it's yours, **Share with them** lets them edit it and tells them. Mentions read as @Name in Word, Markdown and search.
- **Dates:** type **@today**, **@tomorrow** or **@yesterday** (offered above colleagues as you type) to put the date in as a chip, or **@date** to pick one from a calendar; **📅 Date** in the toolbar puts in today. Click a chip (or Enter on it) to change the day, for everyone editing. Dates read the same for everyone (*9 Nov 2026*), past ones show red, and they come out as that text in Word, Markdown and print. Drive search finds them by their words or as *2026-11-09*.
- **Files:** type **@** and at least two letters of a file's name (no spaces; offered after colleagues and dates) to put in a chip for a file or folder from Drive — its icon and name (📄 document, 📊 spreadsheet, 📽 presentation, 📁 folder, 📕 PDF, 🖼 picture, 📎 anything else). Only what you can open is offered, never the document itself. Clicking a chip opens the file in a new tab (a document, spreadsheet or presentation in its editor, a folder in Drive, anything else in Drive's preview). Chips come out as the file's name in Word (underlined, blue), Markdown and print, and Drive search finds the document by it. **Pasting a link** to a file here on its own (a document, spreadsheet, presentation, folder, or a file's preview link copied from the address bar or Share) turns it into the file's chip as well; a link to something you can't open, or to this document, stays a link.
- **Email drafts:** type **@email** (or **@draft**) and pick **Email draft** to put in a block with **To**, **Cc** and **Subject** above the text, for writing an email together: everyone editing sees it change live, and comments and suggestions work on its text. **Open in Mail** opens it as a new message in Mail in a new tab (your signature added), ready to check and send; links in it, file chips included, work from the recipients' inboxes, and Mail offers to share linked files they can't open. In Word and Markdown downloads it reads as *To:* and *Subject:* lines then the text, and Drive search finds it by who it's to and its subject.
- **Event drafts:** type **@event** (or **@meeting**) and pick **Event draft** to plan a meeting together in the document: a block with **Title**, **When** (the day, today to start with, and start and end times; no times makes it all day, no end makes it an hour), **Guests** and **Where**, above notes such as an agenda. **Add to Calendar** opens it in Calendar in a new tab as a new event, filled in: the guests, the place, the notes with a link back to the document, and the document attached (so guests can open it). Check it, add a room or a video meeting if you like, and **Save** sends the invitations. Word and Markdown downloads show *Event*, *When*, *Guests* and *Where* lines then the notes; Drive search finds the document by them.
- **Links to Drive files:** **📄** in the toolbar puts in a link to a document, spreadsheet, presentation, file or folder from Drive, named after it (or links the selected text). If anyone who can open this document can't open what you linked, a note says who; if it's yours, **Share with them** lets them view it.
## Charts from Sheets (Docs)

**Chart** in the toolbar picks a spreadsheet and one of its charts and puts it in the document where the cursor is (drag it to move it). The document keeps a copy of the chart, so everyone who can read it sees the chart, even without access to the spreadsheet. Click the chart to see whether the spreadsheet has changed since: **↻ Update chart** brings in its latest figures, title and type (for people who can open the spreadsheet; checked when you select it, when you come back to the tab and every minute), and **Open spreadsheet** goes there. It prints as it shows. Word and Markdown downloads have the chart's title and a table of its figures, and HTML ones a figure with the same. Presentations can have charts from Sheets too (see Slides).

## Action items to Tasks (Docs)

Write action items as checklist items that mention someone, like *☐ @Kim send the figures by Friday* (the **Meeting notes** a calendar event makes have a list ready). **⋮ → Send action items to Tasks** lists the unticked ones and, after you choose, makes each a task in that person's Tasks (on their first list, under **Assigned to me**), given by you, with 📄 linking back to the document. A date or time in the item (*by Friday*, *tomorrow 3pm*) becomes the due date, as in Tasks' quick add; an item mentioning two people goes to both. Sending again leaves items already sent alone (the list shows them as *already sent* or *done ✓*). If someone can't open the document, you're told, so you can share it.

## Dropdowns (Docs)

Put a status, a review stage or a priority right in the text, like *Website: In progress ▾*, and change it with a click.

- **▾ Dropdown** in the toolbar puts one in where the cursor is: **Status** (Not started, In progress, Blocked, Done), **Review** (Draft, In review, Changes needed, Approved), **Priority** (Low, Medium, High), **Yes or no**, or **Your own choices…** (up to 12, each with one of six colours).
- Click a dropdown (or select it and press Enter) to see its choices and pick one. It takes that choice's colour, for everyone with the document open, as it happens. Arrow keys and Enter work in the list too. Until a choice is picked it shows the first one, uncoloured.
- In the same list, **Clear the choice** empties it, and **Change the choices…** edits, recolours, adds or removes choices. If the picked choice is removed, nothing is picked any more.
- People who can only view or comment see the dropdowns but can't change them.
- The chosen word is kept as text: Search finds it, a Markdown download has it in bold, a Word download has it shaded in its colour, and printing and published pages show the chip.

## Equations and special characters (Docs)

- **Equations:** **∑ Equation** in the toolbar opens a box for the equation in LaTeX (`\frac{a}{b}`, `x^{2}`, `\sqrt{x}`, `\sum_{i=1}^{n}`, `\alpha`…), with buttons for common pieces and a preview that updates as you type; mistakes are pointed out and can't be put in. Or type it straight in the text between double dollars, like `$$E = mc^2$$`. Equations are drawn properly (KaTeX, built in: nothing is fetched from the internet), shared live, and printed.
- Click an equation to change or remove it. People who can only view see it but can't change it.
- Downloads keep them: a Word download has real Word equations, Markdown has `$…$` (and a Markdown file with `$…$` opens with its equations; prices such as *$5 to $10* stay text), and Search finds the LaTeX.
- **Special characters:** **Ω** in the toolbar lists dashes and quotes, signs such as © ™ ° ✓, currencies, maths, fractions and powers, arrows, Greek and accented letters. Type a name to find one (*euro*, *em dash*, *right arrow*) and press Enter, or click any number of them; each goes in where the cursor is.

## Citations and references (Docs)

- **Cite a source:** **❝ Cite → Cite a source…** asks for the author(s) (*Smith, J. & Lee, K.*; separate people with & or ;), year, title, publisher or website, and an optional web address, and puts the citation in the text in APA style: *(Smith, 2020)*, *(Smith & Lee, 2021)*, *(Ng et al., 2019)*, or the title when there's no author. To cite a source again, pick it under **A source already cited**.
- **References list:** **❝ Cite → Insert references list** adds a *References* section listing every source cited in the document once, sorted by author, APA style (title in italics, hanging indent). It updates as citations are added, changed or removed, for everyone editing.
- Click a citation to change it (the list follows) or remove it; pointing at one shows its full reference. People who can only view can't change them.
- Downloads keep both: Word (the list with a hanging indent and italic titles), Markdown and HTML. Search finds the sources' authors and titles.

## Paint format (Docs; Sheets has one too)

- **🖌 Paint format** in the toolbar copies the formatting where the cursor is: bold, italic, underline, strikethrough, colour, highlight, font and size, and the paragraph's style (heading level, alignment, line spacing). Then select text, or just click a word, and it takes that formatting (what was there goes; links and comments stay). The brush turns itself off after one use.
- **Double-click** the brush to keep it on and paint several places in a row; **Esc** or the brush again turns it off. Copying from plain text makes text plain.

## Find and replace, word count, outline (Docs)

- **Ctrl+F** finds text in the document: every match is highlighted, and Enter (Shift+Enter) goes to the next (previous) one. *Match case* and *Whole words* narrow it down. **Ctrl+H** (or *Replace…*) replaces the current match or all of them; replaced text keeps its formatting, and each replace is one step for Undo. Only the person searching sees the highlights; replacements reach everyone as they're made. Viewers can find but not replace.
- **Regular expression** in the find bar searches with a pattern: `INV-\d+` finds every invoice number, `(\d{4})-(\d{2})-(\d{2})` every ISO date. In **Replace with**, `$1`, `$2`… put back the bracketed parts and `$&` the whole match, so replacing that date pattern with `$3/$2/$1` turns 2026-11-09 into 09/11/2026 (formatting kept). A pattern that isn't valid says so instead of finding nothing.
- **Autocorrect:** as you type, straight quotes become curly ones (“…”, it’s) and `--` becomes —, `...` …, `->` →, `<-` ←, `=>` ⇒, `<=` ≤, `>=` ≥, `!=` ≠, `+-` ±, `(c)` ©, `(r)` ®, `(tm)` ™, `1/2` ½, `1/4` ¼ and `3/4` ¾ (not in code). **Backspace** straight after a change puts back what you typed. **⋮ → Preferences** turns it off and holds **your replacements**: a short form and what it becomes (*addr* → your address), applied when you type a space or punctuation after it. Preferences are yours, kept on the server, so they follow you to every device and document.
- **Indent:** **⇥** and **⇤** in the toolbar, or **Ctrl+]** and **Ctrl+[**, move the selected paragraphs and headings in or out by half an inch at a time (up to 8 steps), for a quoted passage or an address block. Lists keep their own indenting (Tab nests them). Everyone editing sees it, paint format copies it, and Word downloads keep it.
- **Superscript and subscript:** **x²** and **x₂** in the toolbar, or **Ctrl+.** and **Ctrl+,**, raise or lower the selected text (or what you type next): mc², H₂O, 1st. Text is one or the other, never both. Word files keep them both ways, and Markdown downloads write them as `<sup>`/`<sub>` (read back too).
- **Sort lines:** select some paragraphs or list items and pick **⇅ → Sort A → Z** (or **Z → A**) in the toolbar: they're put in order by their text, numbers as numbers (*Item 2* before *Item 10*), capitals ignored, blank lines last. Each keeps its formatting, and **Ctrl+Z** puts them back.
- **Capitalisation:** select text and choose **Aa → lowercase, UPPERCASE, Title Case** (small words like *of* and *the* stay small; short all-capital words like *NASA* stay) **or Sentence case** in the toolbar. Bold, links and other formatting stay where they were.
- **Compare documents** (⋮ menu) picks another document, or a Word (.docx) or Markdown file in Drive (one you can open), and shows how its text differs from this one, paragraph by paragraph: words it has that this one doesn't in green, words it leaves out struck through, and paragraphs only one of them has marked down the side, with a count of each. **⇄ Swap** compares the other way round, and **↓ Next change** / **↑ Previous change** step through the differences. It compares the document as it stands, what you've just typed included (suggestions not yet accepted are left out); nothing is changed or saved.
- **Word count** (⋮ menu, or **Ctrl+Shift+C**) gives the words, characters (with and without spaces), paragraphs, sentences, reading time, speaking time (for a talk, at about 130 words a minute) and **readability** (for English text of 30 words or more: *Very easy* to *Very difficult*, with its Flesch reading-ease score; shorter sentences and words read more easily) of the document, and of the selected text beside it when something is selected. Words are counted as Docs and Word count them (*it’s*, *e-mail* and *2026.11* are one word; a dash on its own isn't one). **Display word count while typing** keeps a small counter in the corner (*340 words*, or *12 of 340 words* with a selection) that follows your typing; click it for the full counts. It's remembered in that browser.
- **Word count goal:** in the **Word count** window, **Word count goal** sets a target for the document (*1,500 words*). The counter in the corner then shows progress (*350 / 1,500 words*, with a bar, and ✓ when it's reached) for everyone editing; **Remove goal** takes it away.
- **Page setup** (⋮ menu): paper size (A4, Letter, Legal, A5), portrait or landscape, margins, a header and a footer, and page numbers. The paper on screen takes the size; printing (or saving as PDF) and the Word download use all of it. Everyone editing the document sees the same setup. Printing is always on white paper, whichever theme you use.
- **Pageless:** **Page setup → Pageless** shows the document as one long sheet as wide as the window, without page edges: handy for wide tables, pictures and notes nobody prints. Everyone editing sees it. Printing, PDF and the Word download still use the paper size and margins; **Pages** brings the pages back.
- **Columns:** **Page setup → Columns: Two** (or **Three**) sets the text in columns, like a newsletter: it runs down the first column and on into the next, with paragraphs and tables kept whole. Everyone editing sees it straight away; printing, PDF and the Word download are in columns too, and a Word file in columns opens in columns. On a phone it's shown as one column so it stays readable.
- **Numbered headings:** **Page setup → Number the headings** numbers the document's headings as it goes: *1 Introduction*, *1.1 Scope*, *1.2 Goals*, *2 Costs* (three levels; a heading of a lower level counts under the one above). The numbers follow as headings are added, moved or removed, for everyone editing; they're printed and are in the downloads: Word (in the headings and the table of contents), OpenDocument, Markdown and e-book.
- **Line numbers:** **Page setup → Line numbers in the margin** numbers every line of text down the left margin (1, 2, 3… right through the document), as contracts and reviews use them (*see line 42*). A paragraph that wraps onto three lines gets three numbers; empty lines count, tables don't (as in Word). They follow the text as anyone types, work with columns, and print with the document; the Word download numbers its lines too, and a Word file with numbered lines opens with them on.
- **Page colour:** **Page setup → Page colour** colours the page (cream, light yellow, red, green, blue, purple or grey, charcoal or navy, or a colour of your own; **White** takes it off), for everyone with the document open. On a dark colour the text turns light so it still reads. It's printed (and in PDFs saved from the print window) and kept in Word downloads, where Word shows it as the page colour; a Word file with a page colour keeps it when it's opened as a document.
- **Watermark:** **Page setup → Watermark** puts faint grey text such as *DRAFT* or *CONFIDENTIAL* (up to 40 characters; common ones are suggested) diagonally across every page: on screen, when printed or saved as PDF, and as a real Word watermark in the Word download. Everyone editing sees it at once. A Word file with a watermark keeps it when opened as a document. Clear the box to take it off.
- **Outline:** the list button next to Comments opens the document's headings down the left, indented by level. Click one to go there; the heading you're in is marked, and headings anyone adds show up as they type. It stays open on that computer until you close it.
- **Links to a heading:** hover over a heading in the outline and click 🔗 to copy a link to it (the document's address ending in, say, *#budget-costs*). Whoever opens it (and can open the document) lands on that heading.
- **Links:** **🔗** in the toolbar or **Ctrl+K** opens the link box: type a web address (*example.com* gets *https://*) or an email address, or pick one of the document's **headings and bookmarks** from the list (typing words narrows it). With nothing selected it asks for the link's text too; on a link already there it shows where it goes, to change it or **Remove link**. **Ctrl+click** opens a link while editing (a plain click when reading); a link to a heading or bookmark goes there in the same tab. Word downloads keep links to headings and bookmarks as links inside the file.
- **Bookmarks:** ⋮ → **Bookmarks** → **Add a bookmark where the cursor is** puts a small 🔖 marker there (it doesn't print) and copies a link to it: the document's address ending in *#bm-…*. Whoever opens the link (and can open the document) lands right there; a link to it inside the document goes there too. The same dialog lists the document's bookmarks with the words that follow each, to **Go to**, **Copy link** or **Remove** (viewers can go to and copy them). Word downloads keep them as Word bookmarks, with links to them as links inside the file, and Word files bring their own bookmarks in.

## Comments and @mentions (Docs, Sheets, Slides, Drive files)

- **Comment:** in Docs, select some text and press **Comment** in the toolbar (or Ctrl+Alt+M); in Sheets, select a cell and do the same. The text is highlighted (cells get a small orange corner) and the comment opens in a panel on the right. The highlight moves with the text as people edit.
- **@mention:** type `@` and a few letters of a colleague's name to pick them from a list. They get a chat message with the comment and a link that opens the document at that comment. If the file isn't shared with them, you're told, so you can share it first.
- **Following a conversation:** when someone replies in a thread, everyone in it (who started it, who replied, who it's assigned to) hears about it in the 🔔 bell, with the reply and a link to the thread; a file's owner hears when someone starts a new comment on it. Someone going through a file leaving several (new comments or replies) is one notification counting them, *Kim Park left 3 comments on “Budget”*, with the latest and a link to it, while it's unread and less than 10 minutes old; an @mention is always its own. Not whoever wrote it, not someone already told because they were @mentioned in it, and not anyone who can no longer open the file. Untick **Replies in comment threads you're in, and new comments on your files** in the bell's ⚙ to stop them.
- **What's new:** the Comments button shows **N new** when others have commented, replied or edited a comment since you last looked at that file's comments (a file you've never opened them on counts all of theirs), and those threads are marked **New** with a blue edge while the panel is open. Having the panel open counts as looking, so the count goes as you read, and comes back live when more arrive while it's closed. Your own comments never count. Each person's is their own, kept on the server, so it's the same on every device. In Drive, a file with comments new for you has a small 💬 *2* beside its name (how many threads), which goes once you've looked and comes back as more arrive.
- **For you:** tick **For you** at the top of the comments to see only the threads that @mention you (someone else did) or are assigned to you; the number beside it counts the open ones.
- **Links to comments:** 🔗 on a thread copies its address. Pasted in Chat, an email or a task, it opens the file with that comment showing (a Drive file opens in its preview).
- **Deleting:** 🗑 is on your own comments, and on everyone's for the file's owner; deleting the first comment of a thread removes the whole thread.
- **Editing:** ✎ on a comment of your own changes it (others can't); it then shows *· edited*, everyone with the file open sees the new words, and anyone you @mention for the first time in the edit is told, as for a new comment (people mentioned before aren't told again).
- **Reactions:** ☺ under a comment adds an emoji (👍 ❤️ 🎉 👀 ✅ 😂) instead of a "Thanks!" reply; click one to add yours too or take it back, and hover to see who. Anyone who can comment can react; viewers see them. Everyone with the file open sees them as they happen.
- **Assign:** when a new comment @mentions someone, **Assign to** gives it to one of them: it goes on their task list (top of their first list, linked back to the comment) and they get a notification. The thread shows **☑ Assigned to …**, and resolving it ticks the task off (reopening puts it back); ticking the task off in Tasks resolves the thread too. They need to be able to open the file.
- **Replies, resolve:** anyone who can edit the file can reply, and resolve a thread when it's done (a new reply reopens it). Tick **Show resolved** to see them again. Comments update live for everyone with the file open.
- **Who can do what:** viewers read comments; **commenters** (a sharing level between viewer and editor, for people, groups or everyone in the company: in the Share dialog) read the file and add, reply to and resolve comments, but can't change it; editors do both. A comment can be deleted by its author or by the file's owner (deleting the first comment removes the thread). In Docs, a commenter selects text and presses **Comment** at the top (their comments find the quoted text, as they can't add a highlight to the document); in Sheets, **Comment on B2**; in Slides, **Comment on this slide**.

- **In Slides:** **Comment on this slide** comments on the slide you're on (a 💬 shows on its thumbnail). The comment stays with its slide when slides are moved, and clicking where a comment is takes you to its slide. **💬 Comments** opens the list, with replies, resolving and @mentions as in Docs and Sheets.
- **On uploaded files** (PDFs, pictures, anything in Drive): in the file's preview, **Comments** opens the same panel beside it. **Comment on the file** comments on the whole file; on a picture, **Comment on a spot** and click where you mean, and a numbered pin marks it (click a pin for its comment). @mentions and notifications open Drive with that file's preview and comment.
- **Are they around?** @mentioning colleagues who are away (Mail vacation reply on) or outside their working hours shows a line under the comment box as you write (*🌙 Kim Park: it's 23:10 for them, outside their working hours*), as in Chat.

## Zoom (Sheets)

The box at the bottom right of a spreadsheet, beside the sheet tabs, shows it smaller or bigger (50% to 200%): more rows and columns at once, or larger cells to read. It's kept with your account, so every spreadsheet opens at that size for you on any device (documents have their own zoom). The cells, charts and slicers grow and shrink together, and clicking, selecting, filling, dragging column and row edges and moving or resizing charts and slicers work as usual. It's only how you see it: column widths, printing and downloads aren't affected, and people sharing the spreadsheet keep their own size.

## Printing (Sheets)

- **Print or save as PDF** (⋮ menu) prints the sheet, or just the selected cells (chosen for you when several are selected), on A4 or Letter, portrait or landscape, with or without gridlines. Values print as they're shown (formatted numbers, dates and formula results), with bold, colours, fills, alignment, borders, merged cells and column widths. Hidden and filtered-out rows and columns are left out. Choose "Save as PDF" in the print dialog for a PDF.

## Find and replace (Sheets)

**Ctrl+F** (or ⋮ → **Find and replace**) finds text in the sheet: every match is highlighted, and Enter (Shift+Enter) goes to the next (previous) one. *Match case*, *Whole cell* and *Search formulas* narrow it down. **Ctrl+H** adds **Replace** and **Replace all**, for those who can edit.

## Rows, columns and sorting (Sheets)

- **Select** a whole row or column by clicking its number or letter; Shift-click to select several.
- **Insert or delete:** right-click a row number, a column letter or a cell (or press Shift+F10 or the menu key) and choose *Insert 1 row above*, *Delete column B*, and so on. With several rows or columns selected, that many are inserted or deleted.
- **Everything follows:** formulas point to the same cells after they move, and ranges grow or shrink (`=SUM(B2:B10)` becomes `=SUM(B2:B11)` with a row inserted inside it). A formula that used a deleted cell shows `#REF!`. Formats, column widths, charts and comments move with their cells; a comment whose cell is deleted is marked *(deleted)*.
- **Sort:** right-click a cell and choose *Sort A → Z* or *Z → A* to sort the table around it by that column, or press **Sort** for a range, column and order of your choice. A heading row is spotted and kept at the top. Numbers come before text, and empty cells go last. Formulas move with their row.
- **Cleanup suggestions** (right-click → **Cleanup suggestions…**) looks over the selection, or the table around the cell, and lists what's untidy, each with a **Fix**: **extra spaces** (at the ends of text, or doubled inside), **the same value written differently** in a column (*North*, *north*, *NORTH*: all made like the most common, shown before you fix it) and **duplicate rows** (removed as *Remove duplicates* does). **Fix all** does them all, spaces first. Only text changes: numbers, dates and formulas stay as they are, and a heading row (guessed, and a tick box to change) is left alone. Undo takes a fix back.

## Grouping rows and columns (Sheets)

Select rows (or columns) by their headings, then **Group rows 2–4** in the right-click menu: they're marked at the side, and a **−** on the heading just after them collapses them (**+** brings them back), like the details under a total. Everyone with the spreadsheet sees the same; groups move with inserted and deleted rows, can't overlap, and **Ungroup rows** removes them (collapsed rows show again). Collapsed rows and columns are left out when printing, and Excel downloads and uploads keep groups as outlines.

## Filling and selection totals (Sheets)

- **Paint format:** **🖌** in the toolbar copies the selected cells' formatting (bold, colours, fill, borders, alignment, number format…); then select cells, or click one, and they take it, replacing what they had. Copying a block of cells repeats it across a bigger selection. Double-click the brush to paint several places, then **Esc**. Undo puts it back.
- **Paste special:** after copying cells in a spreadsheet, right-click where they should go → **Paste special: values only** (what the cells showed, so formulas become their results; also **Ctrl+Shift+V**), **formatting only** (the look without the values), or **transposed** (rows become columns, with formulas moved to match). A normal paste (Ctrl+V) still brings formulas and formatting.
- **Fill handle:** the small square at the corner of the selection. Drag it down, up, left or right to carry the selected cells on: numbers with a steady step keep stepping (1, 2 → 3, 4; 10, 20 → 30), text ending in a number counts on (*Item 1* → *Item 2*), weekdays, months and quarters go round (*Mon* → *Tue*, *Nov, Dec* → *Jan*), and formulas move their references as with copy and paste. A single number, or anything else, repeats. Filled cells take the formatting of the cells they continue, and a fill that would reach into a protected range is refused as a whole.
- **Ctrl+D / Ctrl+R** fill the selection down (or right) from its first row (column); on a single cell they copy the one above (or to the left).
- **Totals:** select more than one cell to see their **Sum**, **Average**, **Min**, **Max** and **Count** at the bottom right (hidden rows left out).

## Column widths and frozen rows (Sheets)

- **Width:** drag the right edge of a column letter to make the column wider or narrower; double-click the edge (or right-click the letter → *Fit column to its contents*) to fit what's in it.
- **Row height:** drag the bottom edge of a row number to make the row taller or shorter (handy for a picture in a cell, or wrapped text), or right-click row numbers → **Resize rows…** to type a height in pixels for all the selected rows (16–600; the standard is 24). Double-click the edge, or **Standard row height**, puts them back. Heights move with inserted and deleted rows, show for everyone at once (two people resizing different rows both keep theirs), appear on the published page and are kept in Excel files both ways. A picture set to fit the cell grows with its row.
- **Vertical alignment:** the **↕ Middle** list in the toolbar puts the text of the selected cells at the **Top**, **Middle** (the standard) or **Bottom** of a taller row. Paint format copies it, **Clear formatting** resets it, and it's kept in Excel files both ways.
- **Text rotation:** the **⟲** list in the toolbar tilts the text of the selected cells 45° up or down, turns it 90° up or down, or stacks its letters one under another: handy for long headings over narrow columns. Turned and stacked text makes the row taller to fit. Paint format copies it, **Clear formatting** resets it, and it's kept in Excel files both ways.
- **Custom number formats:** **Number format → Custom…** takes a format code, as in Excel or Google Sheets: `#,##0.00`, `0.0%`, `0.0,,"M"` (millions), `"RM"#,##0.00;[Red]("RM"#,##0.00);"Nil"` (positive; negative in red and brackets; zero), `0 "items"`, `dd/mm/yyyy`, `ddd d mmm, hh:mm`, `h:mm AM/PM`, conditions like `[>=100]`, and `@` for text. It shows what the current cell will look like as you type, with common codes to pick from; **✎** next to the list changes the code later. Only the display changes, never the value. Codes go to Excel as written, and Excel files keep theirs (times included) when opened here.
- **Date arithmetic:** dates work like day numbers in formulas: `=A2+30` is 30 days after the date in A2, `=B2-A2` the days between two dates, `=TODAY()+7` a week from today, and `=A2+0.5` adds half a day (*2026-12-20 12:00*). **MAX** and **MIN** of dates give the latest and earliest date. Comparisons (`=A2>TODAY()`) work as before. In the Excel download, formulas that clearly give a date (a date ± days, `DATE`, `TODAY`, `EDATE`, `EOMONTH`, `WORKDAY`, `NOW`) get a date format so Excel shows a date rather than its day number.
- **Freeze:** right-click a row number → *Freeze the first row* (or *Freeze rows 1–3* with three rows selected) to keep headings in view while scrolling; the same for columns from a column letter. *Unfreeze* puts them back. A darker line shows where the frozen part ends.
- **Hide rows and columns:** right-click a row number or column letter → *Hide row 3* (or *Hide columns B–D* with several selected). Formulas still use what is hidden; arrow keys step over it. A small button on the heading next to hidden ones shows them again, or select across them and choose *Unhide*. Hidden rows and columns move with inserts and deletes and are kept in Excel files.
- **Compare spreadsheets:** ⋮ → **Compare spreadsheets** picks another spreadsheet, or an Excel (.xlsx) or CSV file in Drive (one you can open), and lists how it differs from this one, sheet by sheet and cell by cell, with each value side by side: sheets only in one of them, or named differently, are marked. **Results** (the standard) compares what the cells come to, so a CSV export matches the formulas that made it; **Formulas** compares what's typed. **⇄ Swap** turns it round. It compares the spreadsheet as it stands, what you've just typed included; nothing is changed.
- **Goal seek:** ⋮ → **Goal seek…** finds the number to put in one cell so that a formula comes to the value you want: *Set cell* B4 (profit, `=B1*B2-B3`) *to value* 10000 *by changing* B2 (price) gives **B2 = 28**. Start on the formula's cell and it's filled in. **Solve** shows the answer first; **Keep it** puts the number in the cell for everyone (Ctrl+Z takes it back). It works with curves (a loan's interest rate for a given payment) and formulas that use other sheets, and says plainly when it can't: the cell has no formula, the changing cell holds a formula or text, changing it makes no difference, or no number gets there.
- **Column stats:** right-click a column letter → **Column stats…** for a quick summary of that column: how many cells are filled, empty and different; for numbers the sum, average, median, smallest and largest; and the ten most frequent values with bars. **‹ ›** go to the neighbouring columns, and *The first row is a heading* (guessed for you) leaves the heading out.
- **Alternating colours:** right-click → **Alternating colours…** stripes the table around the cell (or the selected cells) in one of six ready-made styles or colours you pick, with a bold heading row and an optional footer row. A cell's own fill colour still shows, and conditional formatting wins over both. The stripes grow with rows inserted inside them, show for everyone editing, print, and become cell colours in Excel downloads. Open it again to change the colours or **Remove alternating colours**.
- **Remove duplicates, split text:** right-click → **Remove duplicates…** removes rows that repeat an earlier row in the selection (or the table around the cell): rows that are the same in every column, ignoring capitals and spaces at the ends, comparing what cells show. The rest close up. **Split text to columns…** splits a column's text ("Kim Park", "Leeds, UK") into the columns to its right at a comma, semicolon, tab, space, bar or anything you type, or the one it finds. Both can be undone.
- **Randomise rows:** right-click → **Randomise rows** puts the selected rows (or the table around the cell) in a random order, each row's cells moving together (with their formatting; formulas adjust as for moved rows). A header row stays at the top. **Undo** puts them back.
- **Trim whitespace, change case:** right-click → **Trim whitespace** removes spaces at the ends of the text in the selected cells (or the table around the cell) and turns runs of spaces inside into one, tabs and non-breaking spaces included (pasted lists and exports are full of them). **Change case…** makes the text **UPPER CASE**, **lower case**, **Title Case** (*O'Neil-Smith*, not *John'S*) or **Sentence case**. Both change text only: formulas and numbers stay as they are, and **Undo** takes it back.
- Widths and frozen rows and columns are shared with everyone editing, kept per sheet, and carried to and from Excel.

## Filters (Sheets)

- **Filter** in the toolbar turns on a filter over the selected cells, or the table around the cursor; its first row holds the headings, which get a ▾ button.
- The ▾ menu lists the column's values (with how often each comes, and **(Blanks)** for empty cells): untick the ones to hide, or search a long list. Rows with a hidden value disappear for everyone; nothing is deleted, and the arrow keys skip them. The button turns blue on filtered columns. The same menu sorts the table by that column.
- **Filter by condition** in the same menu shows only rows whose value meets a test: empty or not, text that contains, starts with, ends with or is exactly something (ignoring capitals), or a number greater or less than, equal to, or between two values. It works together with the ticked values; the button's tooltip says what it's filtering by, and **None** clears it.
- Rows added below the table are filtered too, and a row whose value changes to a hidden one goes as it changes. **Filter** again removes it, showing every row.
- Downloading as Excel keeps it as an AutoFilter with those rows hidden (conditions included); an Excel file's AutoFilter comes in as a filter.

## Filter views (Sheets)

- **Filter views** in the ⋮ menu lists the sheet's views and makes a **New filter view** (over the sheet's filter range if it has one, else the selection or the table around the cursor). A view filters like a filter (the ▾ buttons, ticked values, conditions), but the rows it hides are hidden **only on your screen**: everyone else, and the sheet's own filter, are untouched. While one is open, a dark bar shows its name and the column headings turn dark.
- Views are saved with the sheet (*Filter 1*, *Filter 2*…; type in the bar to rename one), so colleagues open the same view from **Filter views**, and changes to it are kept for everyone who opens it. **Copy link** gives a link that opens the spreadsheet with the view on; **Delete** removes it; **✕ Close** shows every row again. Views move with inserted and deleted rows and columns, and changing one isn't a change to protected cells.
- People who can only view (or comment) can still filter: they get a **temporary filter view**, only on their screen and not saved, from **New temporary filter view**, from changing a saved view, or from the ▾ buttons of the sheet's own filter.

## Tables (Sheets)

Give alternating colours a name and they become a **table** formulas can use by its columns' headings, as in Excel.

- **Make one:** right-click in the data → **Alternating colours…** → **Table name** (*Sales*: letters, digits, _ and ., not like a cell, and not used by another table or named range).
- **Use it:** `=SUM(Sales[Qty])` (that column's data), `=SUMPRODUCT(Sales[Unit price], Sales[Qty])`, `[@Qty]*[@[Unit price]]` on a row of the table (or in a column of formulas just beside it), `Sales[#All]`, `Sales[#Data]`, `Sales[#Headers]`, `Sales[#Totals]` (the footer row), `Sales[[Unit price]:[Qty]]` for several columns, and `Sales` alone for all its data. Other sheets can use it too.
- **It grows:** rows typed straight under a table belong to it (unless it has a footer row), so `=SUM(Sales[Qty])` takes in new orders without changing the formula.
- **Excel:** an Excel file's tables open as tables here, with their names and their formulas as written (`=SUM(Orders[Qty])` keeps following the table as rows are added); a reference that can't be worked out here is written as a range instead. In an Excel download a table keeps its name, and formulas are written with plain ranges so they work there as they are.

## Slicers (Sheets)

- **Slicer** in the toolbar puts a control on the sheet that filters the table around the cursor by the cursor's column: a chip with the column's heading and what it shows (*Region · All*). It goes beside the table's top right; drag it anywhere.
- **Click it** to tick the values to show, as in a filter's ▾ menu (with how often each comes, a search and **(Blanks)**). The chip then says what's shown (*North*, or *2 of 4*) and turns blue; the other rows are hidden for everyone, and nothing is deleted.
- **Its ⋮ menu** filters by another column of the table instead (**Filter by Rep**), **Show all**, or deletes the slicer. A sheet can have several, on the same table or different ones; a row shows only if every slicer (and the filter) lets it.
- **Charts follow:** a chart leaves out rows that a slicer or filter hides (or that are hidden), so a slicer above a chart makes a small dashboard. Rows added below the table are filtered too, and inserting or deleting columns keeps it on its column.
- **Excel:** Excel's slicers need a table or pivot table behind them, so a download has the rows a slicer hides hidden; the slicer stays in Workspace.

## Dropdowns and tick boxes (Sheets)

- **Validation** in the toolbar (the window is called **Data validation**) has three kinds: **Dropdown**, **Tick box** and **Rule**.
- **Dropdown** turns the selected cells into a list of choices. It starts from the values already in those cells; add or change them one per line. The cells show an arrow: click it, or press **Alt+↓**, then pick with the mouse or the arrow keys and Enter.
- **Only allow these choices** refuses anything else typed into the cells (with a note saying what's allowed); typing a choice in the wrong case is corrected. Turn it off to suggest values without enforcing them.
- **Allow several choices in a cell** lets a dropdown hold more than one (skills, tags, who's going): the list stays open and each click ticks a choice on or off, and the cell shows each one as its own chip (in its colour, if it has one). It's kept as *Design, Sales*, so typing that works too: each part is checked against the choices (and spelt as they are), and with **Only allow these choices** a part that isn't one is refused. Excel's dropdowns take one value, so in an Excel download the list only suggests (with a note saying several are allowed); uploaded again, it's a several-choices dropdown once more.
- **Tick box** shows a box in each cell: click it or press Space. Ticked is TRUE, so formulas such as `=COUNTIF(C2:C20, TRUE)` count them.
- **A range of cells** takes a dropdown's choices from cells instead, on the same tab (`D2:D10`) or another one (`Lists!A2:A20`). The dropdown follows the list: add, change or remove a choice there and every dropdown using it changes too, including what typing allows. Blank cells and repeats are left out. The range moves when rows or columns are added or removed around it, and follows a renamed tab.
- **Rule** says what the cells may hold: a **number** or **whole number** (between, not between, greater or less than, at least, at most, equal to or not equal to some limits), a **date** (before, after, on, or between dates), a **text length** (say, at most 10 characters), an **email address**, a **web address**, or a **custom formula** that must be TRUE (written for the first cell and moved along like a copied formula, e.g. `=B2<=C2` for a start date that's not after its end date). Pick whether something else is **refused** as it's typed (the cell stays open to fix; Esc gives up) or **kept with a warning**, and write your own message if you like (*A quantity from 1 to 99*). Either way, a cell that breaks its rule (from before the rule, a formula, pasting, or the custom formula) gets a red corner, and hovering over it says why. Blank cells always pass.
- **Colours** gives each choice a colour, so cells holding it show it as a coloured chip (for example, Done in green and Blocked in red).
- They move with inserted and deleted rows and columns, are shared live, and are kept when downloading as Excel (rules as Excel's own number, date, text length and custom formula rules, with their message and whether they refuse or warn, and back again on upload; tick boxes become a TRUE/FALSE list there; a dropdown from a range is written as that range, so Excel's dropdown follows it too, though the choice colours stay here). Dropdowns in an uploaded Excel file come in as dropdowns, and lists taken from cells, on the same sheet or another, stay linked to those cells.

## Cell notes (Sheets)

- **A note on a cell:** right-click → **Insert note** (or **Shift+F2**), write it, and press Ctrl+Enter or click away (Esc leaves it as it was). The cell gets a small dark corner; pointing at it, or selecting it, shows the note. Unlike comments, notes have no replies or mentions: they're for a remark that belongs with the number, such as *Estimate* or *Up 5% from March*.
