- **Notify me of changes:** in a document's, spreadsheet's or presentation's **⋮** menu (or **Change notifications on/off** on a file or folder in Drive), turn on a notification for when someone else changes it or uploads a new version; for a folder, when someone adds or changes something in it or its subfolders (file-request uploads too). At most one an hour for each thing you follow, and only while you can still open it. Your own changes never notify you.
- **Storage:** the space line at the bottom of the Drive sidebar opens **Storage**: how much your files, their older versions and the trash take, your company's total (and its limit, if set), and your 25 largest items with what their older versions add. From there, **Delete older versions** frees a file's history (the file stays), the bin moves an item to the trash, and **Empty trash** clears it for good.
- **Duplicate files:** in **Storage**, **Find duplicate files** lists your files that have exactly the same content as another of yours, whatever they're called and wherever they are (the trash doesn't count), biggest waste first, with where each is and when it last changed. **Keep the newest, trash N** clears a group's extra copies, or trash one at a time. Files are compared by a fingerprint of their content, worked out once; with very many large files it says to look again to check the rest.
- **Transfer ownership:** **⋮ → Transfer ownership…** on something you own makes a colleague the owner (of a folder: with everything in it you own). They decide who has it, it counts as theirs, and you keep edit access. They get a notification, and its activity says who made whom the owner.
- **When someone leaves:** deleting a user (Admin → Users → ⋮ → **Delete user and their files**) can **give their Drive files to** a colleague instead of deleting them. The files go into a **Files from {name}** folder in that colleague's My Drive, with their folders and sharing as they were. The same window can also **send their email to** a colleague from then on (their address becomes an alias to that colleague's inbox, listed under Admin → Mail aliases, where it can be removed later) and make a colleague the **organiser of the upcoming meetings** they organised (repeating ones included; the guests keep their invitations; past ones go with the account).

## File details (Drive)

**Details** in a file's or folder's ⋮ menu shows what it is, its size (for a folder: everything in it, sub-folders too, with how many files and folders that is and its biggest files, not counting the trash), owner, when it was made and last changed (and by whom), and who has access; for a document also its length (*1,240 words · 6 min read*). Anyone who can edit it can add a **description** there, which search also finds, and give a folder a **colour**, shown on its icon in the list.

**Who has seen it:** for files, the owner and editors also see in **Details** who has opened it (in its editor, or the file itself in Drive) and when they last did, with how many times (openings within ten minutes count once). Only people who can still open it are listed, and not you. Viewers and commenters don't see the list. Anyone can hide their own openings in **Settings → Who has seen it** (which also forgets the ones kept so far), and an admin can turn it off for the whole company in **Admin → Company settings → Who has seen files**.

**Shared in Chat:** for a file, **Details** also lists the Chat conversations you're in where it was attached or linked (a channel, a private one with 🔒, or the person in a direct message), the latest first, with who shared it last, when, and how many times. Clicking one opens Chat at that message (in its thread, for a reply). Deleted messages don't count; nothing shows when it hasn't been shared in Chat.

**Activity** in the same window lists what happened to it, newest first, for anyone who can open it: who created, uploaded or copied it, edited it (one line per person per hour of editing, including from Docs, Sheets, Slides and the network drive), renamed it, moved it, shared or stopped sharing it, put it in the trash or restored it, or brought back an earlier version. The latest 200 entries are kept for each file. A **folder's** Activity also covers everything inside it, at any depth (what's in the trash too): *Kim Park renamed “Budget” from Draft to Budget*, *Ann created “Q3”*, so you can see what changed in a shared folder without opening each file. Click an item's name there to open it. **Activity** in Drive's left bar is the same for everything you can open: what others did in the last 30 days (made, edited, renamed, moved, shared…), grouped by day, newest first, updating as it happens; your own doings aren't in it, nor files in the trash or that you can't open. The number beside **Activity** says how many entries are new since you last looked (opening it counts as looking), and those are marked *New*. Narrow it with **Who** (one colleague) and **What** (*Made or added*, *Edited*, *Shared*, *Moved, renamed or trashed*, *Locked or approvals*). Activity loads 100 entries at a time: after **Show all**, **Show older** brings the next ones.

**Groups:** admins make groups of colleagues in **Admin → Groups** (*Marketing*, *Managers*…) and tick who's in each. In a file's or folder's **Share** window, **Share with a group…** gives the whole group view or edit access in one step. Whoever is in the group has it: people added later get it (with a notification), and people taken off lose it, unless it was also shared with them directly (they keep the better of the two). The Share window lists groups apart from the people shared with one by one. Deleting a group takes away what it gave. Groups also save typing elsewhere: **Invite a group…** in an event's window adds everyone in it as guests, and **+ (group)** when choosing who's in a private Chat channel ticks its members.

**A group's email address:** when the server has email, each group in Admin → Groups can have an **Email address** (e.g. `support@`). Mail to it reaches everyone in the group, and they can send from it too (it's in their **From** list). It follows the group: people added get it, people taken off, disabled or deleted stop getting it, and deleting the group frees the address.

**Access until a date:** in the Share dialog, the date beside someone ends their access after that day (up to a year ahead; empty means no end). They're told the date when it's shared. When it comes, they can no longer open it, you get a notification that their access has ended, and the file's activity says so. A group they're in can still give them access.

**View only (no downloading, printing or copying):** in a file's **Share** window its owner can untick **Viewers and commenters can download, print and copy**. People who can only view or comment then read it in Workspace but get no **Download**, **Print**, **Download as…** or **Make a copy** (printing shows a note instead, and text can't be selected), and the server refuses those for them too: downloads and Word/Excel/PowerPoint/Markdown exports, copies, zip downloads (it's left out), the network drive, share links, and attaching it to an email or a chat message. Editors and the owner aren't limited. It keeps honest people honest; anyone can still take a screenshot.

**Everyone in the company:** in a file's or folder's **Share** window, its owner (or an editor of a [shared drive](#shared-drives-drive)) can give *Everyone at (your company)* view or edit access in one step, including people who join later (for a handbook, templates, shared forms). It shows in their **Shared with me**, covers everything inside a folder, and stops at once when set back to *No access*. People in other companies never get it this way.

**Asking for access:** opening a link to a document, spreadsheet or presentation that isn't shared with you shows **You need access**: choose view or edit, add a note, and **Request access**. The owner gets a notification and **Access requests** in Drive, where they can **Share** (as asked, or view only) or **Decline**. Either way you're told. The page says nothing about the file, not even whether it exists, until the owner shares it.

## Shared drives (Drive)

A shared drive belongs to a team (Marketing, Finance, a client project) rather than to one person: everyone in it sees the same files, and what people add stays when they leave the company.

- **Make one:** **Shared drives** in the Drive sidebar → **New shared drive**, give it a name, then add its members (people, groups, or everyone in the company) as viewers, commenters or editors.
- **Members:** **Members** at the top of a shared drive (or **⋮ → Manage members** in the list). Editors can add files and folders, rename the drive and add or remove members too; viewers and commenters only read. A drive shows under **Shared drives** for its members, not in My Drive or Shared with me.
- **When someone leaves:** deleting their account keeps what they added to shared drives, in place: it becomes the drive owner's. A drive whose owner leaves goes, with everything of theirs in it, to one of its editors, or to the person their files are given to, or else (while anyone else is still in it) to the admin deleting the account; the delete window says so, and how many items stayed. Only the drive's owner can delete the drive itself, and a shared drive can't be moved into a folder.
- **Recent:** Drive's **Recent** (and Home's recent files) list the 50 latest-changed files you can open, including colleagues' new work in shared drives and in folders shared with you, not only files shared with you one by one.
- **For admins:** **Admin → Shared drives** lists every shared drive in the company with its owner, how many people are in it, how many files and how much space. Admins see inside only the drives they're in; **Add me** makes an admin an editor of one (to look after a drive whose people have moved on), tells its owner, and is in the activity log.
- **Trash:** what anyone moves to the trash in a shared drive shows in the **Trash** of everyone who can edit the drive (marked *· in Marketing*), not only its owner's: any of them can **Restore** it or **Delete forever**. **Empty trash** still empties only your own things.
- **As a network drive:** Finder, File Explorer and phone file apps show a **Shared drives** folder beside *Shared with me*, with the drives you're in; members who can edit add, change and move files there as anywhere else. New drives are made, renamed and deleted in Drive.

## Home

**Home** (at the top of the left bar) is the day at a glance: today's **events** (with **Now** on the one that's on, and **Join** for those with a video meeting), **tasks for today** (what you put in ☀ My Day first, then what's due today or overdue, with a box to tick each off right there, and **Undo**), **unread** email and chat conversations, your **recent files** and **pinned notes**, and **🏖 Who's out**: colleagues on approved time off today with the day they're back (not what kind of time off), and those whose Mail vacation reply is on today, time-off requests waiting for your answer, and the next company holiday. Everything opens where it lives, and the page keeps itself up to date as things change. To open Workspace on Home instead of Drive, choose it under **Settings → Appearance → Open Workspace on** (kept with your account, on every device).

- **Birthdays and dates:** your contacts' birthdays (with the age, when the year is known) and other dates such as anniversaries in the next two weeks, soonest first (*Today*, *Tomorrow*, the weekday, then the date), up to six. **✉ Email** opens a new message to them when they have an address and the server has email. The card only shows when something is coming up.
- **Not answered yet:** email from people in your Inbox (not newsletters or notifications: what's in the Updates tab) that came 3 to 14 days ago and that you haven't answered, newest first, up to five, with who sent it and when. The subject opens it; answering it (here, on your phone or in Outlook) takes it off; **Not now** stops suggesting that one. The card only shows when there's something in it. In Mail, those emails say *Received 5 days ago. Reply?* under their subject in the Inbox, and in **Sent**, mail you sent 3 to 14 days ago that nobody has answered (a reply in any folder but Junk and Trash counts) says *Sent 4 days ago. Follow up?*; **Not now** beside either stops suggesting that one. **Settings → Nudges** turns all of these off.
- **Waiting for a reply:** emails you asked to be reminded about (Mail's ⏰ *No reply in…*), with when the reminder is, and those that have had **No reply yet** (in red, first). They stay until a reply comes (checked hourly), you mark them **✓ Done**, or 30 days pass; **Cancel** stops waiting for one still to come. The subject opens the email. The card only shows when there's something in it.
- **Comments for you:** open comment threads in any document, spreadsheet, presentation or Drive file you can open, where someone @mentioned you or that are assigned to you (*… assigned you*), newest first, up to six. Each shows the file, who wrote it and the start of the comment, and opens the file at that comment. Resolving a thread (or losing access to the file, or it going to the trash) takes it off; new ones appear as they're written. The card only shows when there's something in it.
- **Keep in touch:** the contacts you're due to be in touch with (see Contacts → Keep in touch), most overdue first, up to five, with **✉ Email** and **✓** (you've been in touch another way). Its title opens the full list. It only shows when someone is due.
- **Saved in Chat:** the chat messages you saved for later (🔖 in Chat), latest saved first, up to five, with who wrote them and where. Each opens its message in Chat (in its thread for a reply); **✓** is done with it (takes it off Saved). Its title opens Chat with **Saved** showing (also at `/chat?saved=1`). It only shows when something is saved.
- **Chat reminders:** messages you asked Chat to remind you about (the clock on a message), soonest first, up to five, with when. Each opens its message; **✕** cancels the reminder. Like Saved in Chat, it changes by itself when you save, set or cancel something in Chat.
- **Note reminders:** your upcoming note reminders (yours and those on notes shared with you), soonest first, up to five, with when and 🔁 on repeating ones; **✕** removes a reminder, and the title opens Notes on **Reminders** (also at `/notes?reminders=1`).
- **Recent video meetings:** the Meet meetings you started or were in over the last week (and any with people in it now: *● 2 in it*), newest first, up to four: **Join** opens it, **👥** shows who joined and **💬** (when its host kept the chat) the chat.
- **Customize** (top right of Home) lists Home's cards: untick the ones you don't want (at least one stays) and move the others up or down with the arrows. Your choice is kept with your account, so it's the same on every computer; **Reset** goes back to the usual. Cards that only show when there's something in them (approvals, signatures, who's out, company apps, pinned notes) keep their place for when they do.

## Search everything

The search box at the top looks everywhere at once as you type (two letters or more): **files** (names and what's inside), **mail** (Inbox and Sent, when email is on), **chat** messages, chat **conversations** by name (channels by their name or topic, including ones you've left or that are archived, which come last, and your direct messages by the other person's name; *#* in front is fine), **tasks**, **notes**, calendar **events**, **forms** (yours and those you edit, by title, description, questions or options, with how many responses), **comments** on files you can open (by their words or the start of them, accents ignored; replies too, with who wrote them and on which file; picking one opens the file at that comment), lines of **meeting chats** kept from meetings you started or were in (accents ignored; picking one opens that meeting's chat), your **contacts** and **people** in the company, a few of each with how many there are. Click one, or pick it with the arrow keys and Enter, to open it where it lives: a document opens, a chat message is shown in its conversation, a task, note or event opens in its app. Enter with nothing picked (or **Search Drive for …**) runs the full Drive search with its filters, as before. Escape closes the list.

- **Quick actions:** type what you want to do and it's offered first, under **Actions**: *new doc*, *sheet* or *slides* makes one and opens it (with a name after it, *doc Q3 plan*, *sheet Budget 2027* or *slides Board update*, it's already called that); *compose* or *email* starts a message; *meeting* or *event* opens a new event; *tasks*, *notes*, *meet*, *settings* (and *admin* for admins) go there.
- **Events and tasks with details:** start with *event* (or *meeting*) and add a day or time, and the first action is that very event, read as the Calendar's quick add: *event Lunch with @kim tomorrow 1pm in the Boardroom* offers **New event: Lunch with Kim** (*Sat 14 Nov, 1:00 PM – 2:00 PM*), and Enter opens it in Calendar with Kim invited and the room chosen, to Save. Start with *task*, *todo* or *remind me to* and it's that task, read as Tasks' quick add: *task Send invoice friday 3pm !high #billing* offers **Add task: Send invoice** (*due Fri 20 Nov, 15:00 · high priority · #billing*), and Enter adds it to your own list straight away (**Open** in the note that says so).
- **Notes and email the same way:** *note Call the bank about the card* adds that note to Notes at once, and *list milk, eggs, bread* a checklist (its items split at the commas); *#words* become its labels (**Open** shows it). *email @kim Budget figures* (also *mail*, *message*, *write to*; colleagues by *@name*, anyone by address, with *about* if you like) offers **Email Kim Park**, and Enter opens a new message to them with that subject. With nobody named, it's the plain **Write an email**.
- **Chat the same way:** *chat @kim Are we still on for 3?* (or *dm @kim …*) opens your direct messages with Kim (started if need be) with those words in the box, to finish and send; *chat #launch Shipped it!* does the same in a channel. Nothing is sent: the words wait as that conversation's draft (on every device), under anything you'd already typed there. Two colleagues with the same first name: use the full name (*@Sam Ortiz*) or the address part (*@sortiz*).
- **Tips and the / key:** press **/** anywhere outside a text field to jump into the search box (in Mail, **/** still searches mail; with single-key shortcuts turned off in Settings it does nothing). Clicking into the empty box lists **Type to make things**: *event Lunch tomorrow 1pm*, *task Send invoice friday !high*, *note …*, *email @…* and *chat @…*; picking one (or ↓ and Enter) puts it in the box to change and use. **Hide tips** hides them for good (kept with your account, so on every device); **Settings → Search tips** brings them back.
- **Recent searches:** clicking into the empty box lists your last eight searches (kept with your account, so they're on every computer); click one to search again, × to forget it, or **Clear** for all. A search is remembered when you open one of its results or press Enter.

## Publish to the web (Docs, Sheets, Slides)

**⋮ → Publish to the web…** in a document, spreadsheet or presentation (for its owner) makes a read-only page anyone with its address can see, with no account: a price list, a policy, a timetable, a deck to send round. **Publish** gives the address (**Copy** or **Open** it); the page always shows the latest version and checks for a newer one every minute. Documents show as they read (pictures included, suggestions that weren't accepted left out), spreadsheets as tables of their worked-out values with their formatting (one tab per sheet), presentations slide after slide (skipped slides and speaker notes left out). The dialog says how often it was opened; **Stop publishing** ends it at once. It follows the company's *Links for people without an account* switch, and stops when the file goes to the trash or the owner's account is turned off. Published pages aren't indexed by search engines.

**Embedding:** the dialog's **Embed in a website** gives a piece of HTML (an iframe) to paste into your own website's page, showing the published page there without its heading. Forms have the same, under **⋮ → Embed in a website** (for forms *Anyone with the link* can fill in), and answers arrive as usual. Only these pages can be shown inside other websites; the rest of Workspace can't.

**Admins:** Admin → Company settings → *Links for people without an account* lists everything the company has published (with its owner and how often it was opened), each with **Stop**, and has its own switch, **Let people publish … to the web**: turning it off stops every published page at once and publishing until it's back on (pages come back then). Publishing and stopping are in the activity log.

## Approvals (Drive)

Ask colleagues to sign off a file (a contract, a press release, a budget): **Request approval…** in its ⋮ menu (in Drive, or in a document, spreadsheet or presentation), pick the reviewers (they need to be able to open it), and add a message and a date it's needed by if you like.

- Reviewers get a notification. At the top of the file a banner says who asked and what for, with **✓ Approve** and **✕ Reject** (and a comment). They can also decide from **Drive → Approvals** (with a count of what's waiting beside it), which lists what's **waiting for you** and what **you asked** for. **Home** shows what's waiting for your approval too.
- It's **Approved** when every reviewer approves, and **Rejected** as soon as anyone rejects it. Everyone involved is told, and the banner shows the outcome (with each reviewer's decision and comment under **Details**). If the file is changed after it was approved, the banner says so.
- Whoever asked can **Cancel request** while it's waiting; asking again starts a new request. Requests and decisions also appear in the file's activity (**Details**).

## Signatures (Docs)

Get a document or a PDF signed (a contract, a lease, a consent form): **⋮ → Send for signature…** in the document (or on the PDF in Drive), add the signers (colleagues, or anyone by email when this server has email and your company allows links for people outside it) and a message.

- Each signer gets a private link (colleagues also a notification; everyone an email from you, when email is on). It opens the document **exactly as it was sent**, with nothing else of yours, and no account is needed. They type their name, then **draw** their signature (mouse, pen or finger) or **type** it, tick that they agree to sign electronically, and **Sign**.
- **In order:** tick **One after another, in this order** to have them sign in turn: only the first is asked at first, and each next person is asked (notification and email) once the one before has signed. Someone who opens their link early is told whose turn it is; **Remind** only reminds whoever's turn it is.
- While it's out the document is **locked**, so the text can't change under the signers. Its banner shows **Out for signature · 1 of 2 signed**, with **Sign now** when it's your turn, and **Cancel** for whoever sent it (which unlocks it).
- When the last person signs, you're told (by email too), and the banner says **Signed by everyone**. **Signature record** lists each signer with their signature, name, email, the time and the address they signed from, and a fingerprint (SHA-256) of the text that was signed; **Print** it to keep with the document. The document stays locked; if it's unlocked and changed afterwards, the banner says it has **changed since it was signed**.
- **Signed copies:** once everyone has signed, **Print / Save as PDF** and the Word download end with a **Signatures** page: each signature (drawn or typed) with the signer's name, email, time and address, and the document's fingerprint. If the document is changed after it was signed, that page is left out.
- **Declining:** a signer who won't sign chooses **Decline to sign…** and can say why. The request stops there, the document is unlocked, and whoever sent it is told (notification and email) with the reason, which the banner shows too; fix the document and send it again.
- **Drive → Signatures** lists what's **waiting for your signature** (with **Sign**; the count shows beside it, and **Home** shows them too) and what you've **sent for signature**: how many have signed, who it's waiting for, and **Remind**, which sends those still to sign a reminder (notification and email; at most once an hour).
- Pictures in the document only show on the signing page for signers the document is also shared with.
- **PDFs too:** a PDF's ⋮ menu in Drive has **Send for signature…**, the same way (signers, a message, in order or not, reminders, declining). Signers see the PDF itself on their signing page, exactly as it was sent (a frozen copy is kept, with its fingerprint). **Place where they sign…** in the sending window (optional) shows the PDF: pick a signer and **Signature** or **Date signed**, click where it goes, drag to adjust; each signer sees their boxes highlighted on the signing page, and their signature (drawn, or their name written in italics when typed) and the date they signed are put in them. When everyone has signed, **… (signed).pdf** appears beside it in the sender's Drive: the PDF with a **Signatures** page at the end (each signature, drawn or typed, with name, email, time, address and the fingerprint), and **Drive → Signatures** links to it.

## Locking a file (Drive)

When a file is final (a signed contract, a published report), lock it so nobody changes it by accident: **Lock (stop changes)** in its ⋮ menu, in Drive or in the document, spreadsheet or presentation. Anyone who can edit it can lock it.

- While it's locked, nobody can change its content, the owner included: editors open read-only with a 🔒 **Locked by …** banner, and Drive shows a lock beside its name. People already editing switch to read-only straight away. Uploaded files can't take a new version (in Drive or through the network drive) and earlier versions can't be brought back. Comments still work.
- Whoever locked it, or the owner, can **Unlock** it (from the banner or the menu). Locking and unlocking show in the file's activity.

## Searching Drive

- The search box at the top finds files by **name** and by the **words inside** them: documents, spreadsheets (cell text and sheet names), presentations (with speaker notes, chart titles and picture descriptions), and uploaded text, Word, Excel and PowerPoint files. Each match shows the sentence it was found in, with the words marked. It looks through your files, those shared with you or your company, and everything inside folders shared that way.
- Words can be typed in any order, the start of a word is enough (`penan` finds *Penang*), and accents don't matter (`cafe` finds *café*). Name matches come first.
- **Filters** under the results narrow them by **type** (documents, spreadsheets, presentations, PDFs, pictures, videos, audio, archives, folders; Word, Excel and PowerPoint uploads count with their kind), **owner** (you or someone else), and **when they last changed**. They work without any words too: press Enter in an empty search box, then pick, say, PDFs. The filters are in the page address, so Back and bookmarks keep them.
- **Label** (once you have labels of your own) narrows the results to files with one of them.
- Only files you can open are found, and files in the bin aren't. Changes are searchable within a few seconds, however they were made (in the editor, by upload, or over the network drive). PDFs and pictures are found by name only.

## Drive as a network drive

Drive can also appear on a computer as an ordinary drive (WebDAV), so files open and save straight from Finder, File Explorer or any app's Open and Save dialogs. The address is `https://workspace.example.com/dav/files/`. Settings shows it for each person.

- **Mac:** Finder → Go → **Connect to Server…** → enter the address → sign in.
- **Windows:** File Explorer → This PC → **Map network drive** → *Connect to a website that you can use to store your documents and pictures*. Then enter the address.
- **Linux, phones and other apps:** any WebDAV client (for example the Files app with a WebDAV app, rclone or Cyberduck).
- **What you see:** *My Drive* at the top, plus **Shared with me** and **Shared drives** folders. Documents, spreadsheets and presentations stay in the browser, so they aren't listed.
- **Sign-in:** the same as for calendar sync: your email address and password, or your mail-app password with two-step verification.
- **Safe by design:**
  - Saving over a file keeps the previous one in its version history.
  - Deleting moves to Drive's trash.
  - Viewers can only open files.
  - Quotas and the upload size limit apply.
  - A file that someone has open (locked) can't be overwritten or deleted by anyone else meanwhile.
  - Mac and Windows clutter (`.DS_Store`, `._…`, `Thumbs.db`) isn't stored.
- **Checked** with *litmus*, the standard WebDAV test suite: all 104 tests pass (basic operations, copy and move, properties, locks).
- **Windows note:** Windows connects to WebDAV only over HTTPS, which is how the Synology setup above serves it.

## Version history (Drive)

Drive keeps earlier versions of files, so a wrong edit or an overwritten upload can be undone.

- **Uploaded files:** in a file's ⋮ menu, **Upload new version** replaces its contents (and takes the new file's name). Links, shares and its place in Drive stay the same, and the previous contents are kept.
- **Documents and spreadsheets** keep a version from each editing session: when someone else starts editing, or after a 10-minute pause.
- **Version history** (in the ⋮ menu) lists the versions with when and who. You can download earlier uploads, and **Restore** any version. What's there now is kept as a version too, so you can switch back. Open editors load the restored content straight away.
- Viewers can see and download versions. Editors can also upload new versions and restore. Owners can delete versions they no longer need.
- The most recent 50 versions of each file are kept (`KEEP_VERSIONS` changes this). They count towards the company's storage quota, and are deleted with the file.

## Share links (Drive)

Send a file or a whole folder to someone who has no account, such as a client. In Drive, open **Share** on a file or folder; under **Link sharing**, pick how long the link lasts (1, 7 or 30 days, or no expiry), optionally add a password, and press **Create link**. The link is copied straight away and looks like `https://workspace.example.com/s/…`.

- **What they see:** a simple page with the name, who shared it and from which company, a preview (pictures, PDF, video, audio, text) and a **Download** button. A folder link lists what's inside; they can open sub-folders and view or download any file. Docs and Sheets inside a folder aren't included yet.
- **Password:** they type it once per browser tab. Wrong passwords count towards sign-in protection, so guessing gets the address blocked. Send the password separately, for example by phone.
- **File requests:** for a folder, tick **File request (upload only)** before **Create file request** to collect files from people without an account (receipts, signed forms, photos). The page they open asks for their name and takes files dropped on it; they see only what they sent from that page, never what's in the folder. Each file lands in the folder as yours, counts towards your company's storage, says who sent it (in its details), and you get a notification: several files from the same person in a row are one notification counting them (*Bob Client sent 3 files*, with the latest one's name) while it's unread and less than 10 minutes old, so a batch of receipts doesn't fill the bell (or buzz your phone each time). Expiry, password and removing the link work as for other links.
  - **What it takes:** ticking **File request (upload only)** shows **What it takes**: tick kinds of file (PDFs, pictures, documents, spreadsheets, presentations, videos, zip files; none ticked takes anything), set the **largest file** in MB, and leave **a note for the people sending** (*Your receipts for October, as PDFs or photos, please.*). Their page shows the note and *Takes PDFs or pictures, up to 10 MB each*; its file picker offers only those kinds, and a file of another kind or too large is refused there and then, saying why (the server checks too: by the file's name, and by what's in it, so a renamed file such as a program called *receipt.pdf*, or a JPEG called *.png*, is refused with *doesn't look like the kind of file its name says* and not kept; requests that take any file aren't checked this way). The request's line says what it takes; **⚙** on it changes all three later, keeping the same link.
- **Email a link:** with this server's email on, ✉ next to a link opens a new email with it: the file's or folder's name, the link, what they can do with it (or, for a file request, how to send files), until when it works, and, for a link with a password, that you'll send the password separately (it's never put in the email).
- **Change expiry:** the menu next to a link makes it last 1, 7 or 30 days from now, or never expire, keeping the same address (so people you've sent it to can still use it). An expired link can be brought back the same way. 🔑 adds or changes a link's password (whoever used the old one needs the new one) and 🔓 removes it, also keeping the address.
- **Shared by link:** Drive's left list has **Shared by link**: every link you've made, on any file or folder (tick *Show expired links too* for the rest), with what kind it is (🔑 password, 🔔 you're told when it's opened), until when it works and how often it was opened. Copy one, open its **Share…** window to change it, or remove it.
- **Keeping track:** the Share dialog lists each link with its expiry, whether it has a password, and how often it was opened. **Remove** stops a link at once.
- **Notices about your links** (opened, ending soon, files sent through a request, stopped by an admin) have their own switch in the bell's settings (⚙): *Your share links*, apart from *Files shared with you*. (Anyone who had *Files shared with you* off before this switch existed has it off too, so nothing new starts reaching them.)
- **Know when it's opened:** tick **Tell me when it’s opened** when making a link (or 🔕 → 🔔 next to an existing one) for a notification the first time someone opens it, then at most once an hour with how many times it has been opened. A password-protected link counts once the password is typed.
- **Before a link ends:** about a day before one of your links stops working you get a notification (*Your link to brief.pdf stops working in 12 hours*); it opens the file's Share window, where **Change expiry** gives it longer with the same address, or you let it end. Not for links made to last two days or less, and once per expiry date.
- **Links stop working by themselves** when they expire, when the file or folder goes to the trash (or is deleted), or when the owner's account is disabled. A file moved out of a shared folder is no longer reachable through that folder's link.
- **Company switch:** Admin → Company settings → *Links for people without an account*. Turning it off immediately stops every link in the company; turning it back on restores the ones that haven't expired. Under it, admins can make links **expire within** a number of days (1–365; *Never expires* and longer choices then aren't offered) and **require a password on every link**. These apply to links made or changed from then on; existing ones stay as they are until an admin uses **Apply the rules to existing links…** in Admin → Share links (offered when some don't follow them): links without a required password stop (one can't be added for people who already have them), and links that last too long, or never expire, then expire that many days from now. Their makers get a notification saying which. Mail's large files sent as links last no longer than allowed, and with passwords required Mail can't send them as links at all (it says so).
- **For admins:** **Admin → Share links** lists every working link people in the company have made (tick *Show expired links too* for the rest): the file or folder, who made it, whether it's a view link or a file request and has a password, until when it works and how often it was opened. **Stop** ends one at once, and whoever made it gets a notification. **File or folder** finds links by part of a name, **Made by** shows one person's links; with someone picked, **Stop all of their links…** ends every one still working (someone moving on, a project closing), and they're told. **Download (CSV)** gives every link (expired ones too) as a spreadsheet for an audit: the file, who made it, the link, password, when made, until when, how often and when last opened. (A company admin can also remove a link from a file they don't own this way.)
- Only the owner of a file can create or remove its links. Link pages aren't indexed by search engines.

## Calendar

- **Views:** Month, Week, Day, Year and Schedule (the choice is remembered in each browser).
  - In week and day views, events sit at their times, overlapping ones side by side, with a red line for the current time.
  - **Year** shows all twelve months at once; days with events are shaded (darker with more of them), and clicking a day opens it. The arrows move a year at a time.
  - **Show weekends** (untick it in the Calendar sidebar) leaves Saturdays and Sundays out of the week and month views, for a Monday-to-Friday calendar. **Show week numbers** adds the week of the year (ISO, as used in Europe: W46) to each row of the month and to the week and day titles. Both are remembered in each browser.
  - **Week starts on** (Calendar sidebar): Sunday, Monday or Saturday. The week, month and year views, the week's title and **Print or save as PDF → This week** follow it; remembered in each browser.
  - Click an empty half hour to create an event there, or a date to open that day. The arrow keys move between periods.
- **Quick add:** the box under **Create** takes an event as you'd say it: *Lunch with Kim tomorrow 1pm*, *Dentist friday 10am for 45 minutes*, *Workshop 24 Nov 3-5pm*, *1:1 with Ana every 2 weeks on tue 10am*. Days, times and repeats are read as in Tasks' quick add; a range (*3-5pm*, *10:30 to 11:15*, *from 9 to 11am*) or a length (*for 30m*, *for 1.5 hours*, *for an hour*) sets the end, otherwise it lasts your default event length. A day without a time is all day. What's understood shows under the box as you type; **Enter** opens the event filled in, to add guests or a room and **Save**. Words with no day or time open a new event with that title. Short day names need *on* (*on tue*), so *sat with Kim* stays words.
  - **Guests:** *@kim* or *@Kim Park* (a colleague) and email addresses invite them: *Lunch with @kim tomorrow 1pm* invites Kim Park and is called *Lunch with Kim*; *Kickoff with jo@client.com friday 9am* invites Jo by email and is called *Kickoff*. The preview says who (*· with Kim Park*). When two colleagues fit (*@sam*, with Sam Lee and Sam Ortiz), or nobody does, it stays as typed: write the full name (*@Sam Ortiz*) or the address part (*@sam.ortiz*).
  - **Rooms:** *in* or *at* one of the company's rooms picks it: *Budget review in the Boardroom tomorrow 2pm* opens with the Boardroom chosen (*Free: booked when you save*, or *Taken then*), and the room's name leaves the title. The longest name wins (*at Room 12* isn't Room 1); a place that isn't a room (*Trip to Lisbon*) stays words.
- **Dragging with the mouse:** drag an event to another time or day (in 15-minute steps in week and day views; to another day in the month view and the all-day row), or drag its bottom edge to change when it ends. Esc cancels a drag. For a repeating event you choose "This event", "This and following events" or "All events"; a weekly series on set weekdays moves its weekdays along with it. Invitations from other people can't be dragged. On phones and tablets, open the event and change the times instead.
- **Changing the start** of an event in its window moves the end with it, keeping the length (a one-hour event moved to 16:00 now ends at 17:00), so a late-evening draft moved to the afternoon doesn't end up lasting into the next day.
- **Reminders:** each event can remind you (at the start, or minutes, hours, days or a week before; up to five). New events remind you 10 minutes before. While Workspace is open in a browser, a reminder pops up at the bottom of the page, and as a desktop notification when the tab is in the background and notifications are on (Settings). It shows once, even with several tabs open. Reminders are your own: a guest sets theirs in the invitation, and the organiser's don't apply to them. Phones and calendar apps that sync over CalDAV get them too, and reminders set on the phone show on the web.
- **Repeating events:** in an event, choose **Repeat**: daily, weekly (pick the weekdays), monthly on the same date, monthly on a weekday (**Monthly on the second Monday**, from the event's date; in a month's last week also **Monthly on the last Friday**, which suits month-end meetings: dragging the event to another day makes it that day's place in the month), or yearly; every 1, 2, 3… of those. It ends never, on a date, or after a number of times. A line such as "Every 2 weeks on Mon, Thu" confirms the choice.
  - Dates are worked out in the event's own time zone, so a 9:00 meeting stays at 9:00 when the clocks change, also for colleagues looking at it from another time zone.
  - A monthly event on the 31st skips months without a 31st, and a yearly event on 29 February appears only in leap years.
  - Changing or deleting one occurrence asks **This event / This and following events / All events**.
  - Repeating events show ↻.
- **Inviting colleagues:** add people under **Guests** (type an address; suggestions come from your company directory). You can invite anyone you can work with: your own company, and other companies when both allow collaboration.
  - Guests see the event in their calendar, outlined until they answer, and answer **Yes / Maybe / No**. For a repeating event, the answer covers the whole series.
  - When email is set up, they also get an invitation email from you (with the time in your time zone), an "Updated:" email when you change the time, place or details, and a cancellation if you delete the event or remove them.
  - **A note with the answer:** guests can add a few words when answering (*I'll join 10 minutes late*), on the event or on the answer page for people outside Workspace. The organizer gets a notification with it and sees it beside the guest's answer.
  - You see each answer in the event, for example "3 guests: 2 yes, 1 not answered". Calendars that are open update straight away.
  - Only the organiser can change or delete the event. A guest who says No still sees it, faded.
- **Inviting people outside Workspace** (clients, suppliers): type their email address under **Guests**; they show as "by email". This needs email to be set up.
  - They get an invitation email with a calendar file, so Outlook, Gmail, Apple Mail and phones show it as an invitation they can add to their own calendar. Repeating events keep their time across clock changes.
  - The email has a personal link to a simple page (no account needed) where they answer **Yes / Maybe / No**, see the time in their own time zone, and can download the event again. Their answer shows in your calendar straight away.
  - If you change the time, place or details, they get an updated invitation that replaces the old one in their calendar. Removing them or deleting the event sends a cancellation, and their link stops working.
  - They can also answer with their own mail app's buttons (Outlook's *Accept*, Gmail's *Yes*). That reply arrives in your inbox as usual, and Workspace reads the answer from it within a couple of minutes. Only a reply from the guest's own address counts.
  - An address on one of this server's own domains must belong to someone here, so a typo can't email a stranger.

- **Booking pages:** **Booking pages** in the Calendar sidebar makes a link people can use to book time with you, with or without an account (customers, candidates, suppliers). Choose the length, the days and hours, how much notice you need and how far ahead people can book. Visitors see only the free times, in their own time zone: your events (repeating ones too) and invitations you haven't declined count as busy, all-day events don't. A booking goes on your calendar with the visitor as a guest and their note in the details; they get an invitation by email, and you get a notice. **Group sessions:** set **People at a time** above 1 (a class, a tour, a webinar) and several people can book the same time, up to that many: they join one event (named after the page), each as a guest; the page shows how many places are left at each time (*2 places left*) and stops offering a time once it's full. Visitors don't see each other: their invitations list only themselves and leave out the event's notes (where you see who's coming and what they answered). Cancelling takes just that person off; the last one out removes the event. A place in a group session can't be moved (cancel and book another time). Turn a page off or delete it to stop bookings; Workspace's address must be reachable from the internet for outside visitors (set `PUBLIC_URL`).
  - **Questions:** up to five of your own (a phone number, their company, what it's about), each optional or **Required**; visitors answer them under their name and email, and the answers go in the event's notes.
  - **Where:** **📹 A video meeting** makes a Meet link for each booking: it's on the event (the host starts it from there), on the visitor's confirmation and in their invitation, and they join as a guest without an account (their link has their name in it, so it's already filled in). **📍 A place…** puts an address (or a phone number) on each booking. Visitors see which before they book.
  - **Gap and daily limit:** **Gap before and after** keeps 5 minutes to an hour free around your events and other bookings (no back-to-back calls), and **Bookings a day** stops offering a day once it has that many from this page.
  - **Changing or cancelling:** the visitor's confirmation (and the invitation email) has a link to their booking. **Pick another time** shows the page's free times and moves it there (the event moves on your calendar, they get the updated invitation, and you're told); **Cancel booking** takes it off your calendar, tells you, and offers the time again. Not once it has started.
  - **Reminders** (when this server has email): visitors get an email from you about a day before (in the 24 hours before, not in the last hour), with the time and their link to change or cancel. Not for a booking made less than 12 hours ahead; each is sent once. **Email visitors a reminder the day before** in the page's settings turns it off.
  - **Shared by a team (co-hosts):** under **Also take bookings**, add colleagues. The page then offers a time when any of you is free (the page shows all your names), and each booking goes to someone free then, the one with the fewest bookings from the page so far: it's on their calendar, they're told, the reminder comes from them, and if the visitor moves it, it stays with them.
- **Other people's calendars:** **See a colleague's calendar…** in the Calendar sidebar adds a colleague (from your own company, up to ten at a time); their busy times show as hatched blocks in the week and day views, in their own colour, so you can find a time that suits everyone. Only when they're busy shows, never what or where. The list is kept on that device; **×** hides someone again.
- **Share my calendar:** in the Calendar sidebar, choose colleagues who may see what your events are, not only when you're busy (an assistant, your team). When they add your calendar beside theirs, each block shows the event's title and place. They get a notification, and their view changes at once, also when you remove them. The window also lists who shares theirs with you. Only people in your company. **Can make and change events** (tick it beside someone, for an assistant) lets them also pick your calendar under **Calendar** at the top of their Calendar sidebar and add, change and delete events on it as you (you organise them; invitations go out from you). You get a notification each time, and it's in the activity log; your own settings, booking pages, sharing and subscriptions stay yours.
- **One event as a file:** **Download (.ics)** in an event's window (or **Download this event (.ics)** in an invitation) saves just that event as a calendar file, with its time and time zone, place, notes, organiser and guests (and its repeats), for Outlook, Apple or Google Calendar, or to send to someone. The organiser, the guests and people who can see that calendar can download it.
- **Links to events:** **Copy link** in an event's window copies its address (for a repeating event, of the time you opened). Opening the link opens the event in Calendar: the event itself for whoever organises it, the invitation (with Yes / Maybe / No) for guests. Pasted in Chat, it shows as a card under the message with the title, when and where, and your part in it (*You organise it*, *You're going*, *You haven't answered*…) with how many guests are going. A guest can answer right on the card (**Yes**, **Maybe** or **No**; their answer is marked, and the organiser is told as usual), except for a repeating event or one that's over, which are answered in Calendar. Only people who could open the event see the card (its organiser, its guests and people who can see that calendar, and for a private event only those who could change it or are in it). Anyone else sees *An event you can't see*, and the link tells them so. **Send in Chat…** next to **Copy link** (in an event's window, or an invitation) puts the link in the message box of the conversation you choose and opens it there, to add a word and send.
- **Chat with guests:** in an event's window (or a guest's invitation), **Chat with guests** opens a private Chat channel for the event: named after it, with the organiser and the colleagues invited (people outside the company aren't in Chat), its card posted first. It's made the first time; after that it's the same channel, and anyone invited since is added. For the organiser and the guests.
- **Copy this week:** in the week view, **⧉ Copy this week…** in the Calendar sidebar copies your own one-off events of the week shown to the later weeks you tick (next week, or up to 8 weeks on, each getting them all), on the same days and at the same times, also across a clock change (9:00 stays 9:00), with their details, colour, calendar and your reminders. Repeating events and ones you're invited to stay as they are. **Invite their guests too** sends the guests invitations to the copies; otherwise they're yours alone. Rooms aren't booked again. **Undo** in the note that says so takes the copies away.
- **Duplicate:** **Duplicate** in an event you organise opens a copy with the same title, time, place, notes, guests, colour and reminders (as they are in the window, so you can change the time first); change what you need and **Save**. The copy doesn't repeat, and guests are invited to it as a new event.
- **Undo a delete:** deleting an event of your own with nobody else in it (no guests and no rooms) shows **Undo** for a moment, which puts it back as it was, with its reminders and any changed occurrences of a repeating one (for a day). Deleting an event with guests cancels it for them, as before, so there's no Undo.
- **Templates:** **📋 Template → Save as template…** in an event's window keeps its title, length, place, notes, guests, reminders, colour and calendar under a name (*Weekly 1:1*, *Client call*; the same name replaces it). **📋 From a template…** on the left then opens a new event with those details, starting at the next full hour (or today, for an all-day one), to change and **Save**. Templates are your own; **📋 Template** also deletes them.
- **Moving templates:** **From a template… → Export templates** saves them as *Calendar templates.json* (Download your data has the same file); **Import templates…** (under **Templates…** when you have none) reads one back here or on another Workspace server: ones already here are skipped, a name taken by a different template gets a number, and a calendar that isn't one of yours here is left out.
- **Holidays in …:** **Holidays in** on the left of Calendar picks a country (the same ones as *Add public holidays*: the US, the UK, Ireland, Germany, France, the Netherlands, Canada, Australia) to show its public holidays on your own calendar (🎉), worked out for whichever weeks you look at. It's yours alone, for when you work with people in another country or the company hasn't set its holidays; a company holiday on the same day shows instead (🏳). **(none)** turns it off. On phones and calendar apps syncing with Workspace (CalDAV) it's a calendar of its own, *Holidays in …*, read-only, with last year's to the year after next
- **Time off:** **🏖 Time off** in the Calendar sidebar asks for days off (vacation, sick leave, personal or other, with a note): the first and last day, and it counts the working days. Whoever you report to (set in Admin → Users) is asked, or the company's admins if you report to nobody; they get a notification, and 🏖 shows how many wait for their answer. They **Approve** or **Decline**, with an answer if they like, and you're told. Approved time off goes on your calendar as an all-day out-of-office event (so invitations for those days are declined for you). **Cancel** takes back a request, or approved time off (its event goes, and whoever approved it is told). In a company of one, time off is added straight away.
  - **Half days:** for a single day, **Part of the day** asks for just the **morning** or the **afternoon**: it counts as half a working day, goes on the calendar as out of office until or from noon, and the other half of the same day can be asked for separately.
  - **Vacation allowance:** in **Time off → Team**, admins set the company's vacation days a year (empty: no limit) and, if someone has a different number, theirs. Everyone then sees how many days they have left this year (approved and waiting both count; sick leave and other kinds don't), a request for more than that is refused with what's left, and whoever decides sees the person's balance next to each request. Days are counted per year, so time off over New Year uses some of each.
  - **Everyone's balance:** **Everyone's 2026 balance (.csv)** beside the time off report (admins) downloads a row per person for the year: when they started, their days (with any pro-rating and carrying over), how many carried over, taken, waiting for an answer, and left, for HR or payroll.
  - **Start dates and the first year:** each person's **Started** day in **Time off → Team** (admins; the day their account was made until it's set) is when their vacation begins: nothing before it. Tick **Give people who start during the year that year's days in proportion** and their first year is the share of the year left from that day, to the nearest half day: 24 days a year, starting 1 October, is 6 that year and 24 after. Carrying over counts from the start date too.
  - **Carrying over:** **Carry over up to (days)** in **Time off → Team** (admins) moves unused vacation days into the next year, up to that many: with 20 a year and 5 carrying over, someone who took 12 last year has 25 this year, and their balance says so (*25 of 25 vacation days left in 2027 (5 carried over from 2026)*). Days carried in are used first, so they don't carry on again; only approved days count, and only from the year the account was made. 0 turns it off.
  - **Company holidays:** admins list them in **Time off → Team** (a day and a name), or **Add public holidays…** to add a country's for a year in one go: the United States (federal), the United Kingdom (England and Wales), Ireland, Germany, France, the Netherlands, Canada (federal) and Australia (national). They're worked out for any year (Easter and the *first Monday in May* kind included, and the days off given in their place when one falls at a weekend, where the country does that); the list shows them first, with any already there marked and kept. Regional and one-off days are added by hand. They show on everyone's calendar (🏳), aren't counted as working days in time off, aren't offered on anyone's booking pages or suggested by **Find a time**, and a request for nothing but holidays and weekends is refused.
  - **Team:** whoever decides for others (admins: everyone) sees each person's vacation this year and their time off in the next three months.
  - **Report for payroll:** admins download a year's approved time off from **Team** as a spreadsheet (.csv): name, email, kind, first and last day, all day or half, the working days it took in that year (requests over New Year split by year), who approved it and the note. **with every request** adds waiting, declined and cancelled ones.
- **Busy or free, and private:** in an event's window, **Show me as Free** keeps it on your calendar without blocking the time: colleagues looking at your calendar, booking pages, **Find a time** and your *In a meeting* status don't count it. **Private** (🔒 on the event) keeps what it is to yourself and your guests: people you've shared your calendar with to look at, or one of your calendars, see only **Busy** at that time (no title, place, notes or files, and no calendar file to download), on their phones too. People who may make changes to it still see everything. Both go to phones and calendar files as TRANSP and CLASS, and come back from them.
- **Out of office:** **🌴 Out of office…** in the Calendar sidebar (or ticking *Out of office* on any event) marks time off, shown striped. While it lasts, new invitations for that time are declined for you, and whoever invited you gets a notification with your message (the event's description). You can still say Yes yourself. Colleagues looking at your calendar see *out of office* for the whole time, even all-day, and booking pages don't offer it. Time off has no guests. When the server has email, **Also turn on my email auto-reply for these days** sets your vacation reply (Mail → Vacation responder) to the same first and last day (in the event's time zone), with the event's message (or one saying the day you're back), saved together with the event. The subject is *Out of office*, or *Out of office: (its title)* when you've named it.
- **Calendars of your own:** **+** next to *My calendars* in the Calendar sidebar adds one besides your main calendar (Personal, Family, a project…), with a name and a colour. In an event's window, **Calendar** puts the event in one (it takes that calendar's colour, unless you gave it one of its own); moving an event there later works the same way. Untick a calendar to hide its events (remembered on every computer); its **⋮** menu changes its **name and colour** (its events in the old colour follow), **imports** an .ics file straight into it, **downloads** it on its own, or **deletes** it, either with its events (guests are told they're cancelled) or keeping them in your main calendar. Colleagues see these events as part of your calendar (free/busy, sharing), and on phones and other calendar apps (CalDAV) each is a calendar of its own, in its colour: events added to one there go in it, moving an event to another calendar there moves it here, and renaming or recolouring one there is kept (new calendars are made here).
- **Sharing one of your calendars:** its **⋮ → Share…** adds colleagues, each to **See events** or **Make changes** (only that calendar: your main one and the others stay yours, unlike *Share my calendar*). They're told, and find it under **Shared with me** in their Calendar sidebar, with its events in its colour; they can hide it or **Remove from my list**. Those who can make changes add events to it (pick it under **Calendar** in a new event) and change or delete its events; the events stay yours and you get a notification of each change. Everyone sharing it sees changes as they're made. Others open its events read-only. Shared calendars sync to phones too (see *Calendar and contacts on your phone*).
- **Calendars by address:** **+** next to *Other calendars* in the Calendar sidebar subscribes to a calendar published on the web (an address ending in .ics, or a webcal:// link): public holidays, a sports fixture list, a supplier's or another team's published calendar. Its events show in your calendar in their own colour, read-only (click one for its details), and are fetched again every few hours; ⋮ next to it updates it now or unsubscribes. Up to 20, 5 MB each. Addresses on the server's own network aren't fetched.
- **Import and export:** **Import (.ics or .csv)** in the Calendar sidebar adds the events from a calendar file exported from Google Calendar, Outlook or Apple Calendar: times in their time zones, all-day events, repeats (with changed or cancelled occurrences) and reminders. Importing the same file again skips what's already there. It also takes a calendar **exported as CSV** (Outlook's *File → Export*, or the spreadsheet layout Google Calendar imports): columns **Subject**, **Start Date**, **Start Time**, **End Date**, **End Time**, **All day event**, **Description** and **Location**, dates month first (12/25/2026) or 2026-12-25, times like *9:30 AM* or *14:30*, read in your time zone; a row with no time is an all-day event and one with no end lasts an hour. **Export (.ics)** downloads all your own events as one file for another calendar app. **Export as CSV** downloads them as a spreadsheet in the same columns (times in your time zone, plus a **Repeats** column saying Daily, Weekly, Monthly or Yearly), for Excel or another calendar's CSV import, and it imports back here; a calendar's **⋮ → Download as CSV** does the same for just that calendar.
- **Calendar settings:** in the Calendar sidebar: the **default event length** for new events (15 minutes to 2 hours), **Speedy meetings** (30-minute meetings end 5 minutes early and those of an hour or more 10 minutes early, so there's a gap before the next one), and the **reminders** a new event starts with (none, or up to five). Each event can still be changed; kept with your account. **Show declined events** under the calendars in the sidebar: untick it to hide invitations you said No to (they're shown faded otherwise); kept with your account too, so it's the same on every device.
- **Colours by title:** **Calendar settings → Colours by title** takes a word or phrase and a colour (*1:1* purple, *Lunch* green, *Interview* orange; up to 20): events whose title has it as a whole word (any case), and no colour of their own (the usual blue), show in that colour, yours, invitations and shared calendars' alike. It's only how they look to you: the events themselves don't change. The first matching rule wins, and **×** takes one off.
- **Working hours:** **Working hours** in the Calendar sidebar sets the days and times you work, in your time zone (say Tuesday and Thursday, 13:00 to 17:00). **Find a time** then only offers times inside the hours of everyone it checks, each in their own time zone, and names whose own hours it kept to. Anyone who hasn't set theirs counts as Monday to Friday, 9 to 5, in the organizer's time zone. **Use the usual** goes back to that. While you plan an event with colleagues, the window names any invited colleague who's **busy then**, or for whom the time is **outside the working hours they've set**, with what time it would be for them (*🌙 Kim Park 23:00–00:00 for them, outside their working hours*; *📅 Cy busy then*), or away that day with their vacation reply on (*🌴 Lee away until 7 Mar*). It updates as you change the time or the guests; nothing about their other events is shown.
- **Attachments:** **Attach from Drive** in an event's window adds documents, spreadsheets, presentations or files (the agenda, last month's figures). Guests on the server are given view access to the ones you own when you save, so they open them straight from the invitation. Others' files they can already open show as links; any they can't open are listed as "no access". **×** takes one off (nobody loses access they were given).
- **Meeting notes:** **📝 Take meeting notes** in an event you organise makes a document in your Drive called *Notes – (event) (date)*, with the title, when and where it is, who's coming (colleagues as @mentions, people outside by address), the description as the agenda, and places for notes and action items, and opens it in a new tab. It's attached to the event, and colleagues invited (also ones added later) can edit it, so everyone writes in the same notes. Once made, the button reads **Open meeting notes**; if it's taken off the event, asking again puts the same document back.
- **Focus time:** **🎯 Focus time…** in the Calendar sidebar (or ticking *Focus time* on an event) blocks time to work without interruptions. While it lasts, new invitations for that time are declined for you (the organizer is told you're in focus time) and notifications are paused (🔕 in the top bar), though they still arrive in the bell. It's just you: no guests, rooms or video meeting. It can repeat like any event.
- **Status from your calendar:** when you haven't set a status yourself, colleagues see one from your calendar (in the Directory, Chat and elsewhere): **📅 In a meeting until 15:00** during an event with other people, **🎯 Focusing** during focus time, **🌴 Out of office** while away. Event titles are never shown, and a status you set always wins. With nothing on your calendar but your Mail **Vacation reply** on, it's **🌴 Away until** its last day (or just *Away* without an end date).
- **World clock:** **+** next to *World clock* in the Calendar sidebar adds a place (up to eight): each shows the time there now, how far ahead or behind you it is (*+9 h*, *−5:30 h*), and *tomorrow* or *yesterday* when the day differs; × takes one off. Your places are kept with your account, so they're there on every computer.
- **Second time zone:** **Settings → Second time zone** adds another place's time beside the hours in the week and day views (in purple, with both zones' names at the top), for working with colleagues or clients elsewhere. Kept with your account.
- **Time insights:** **📊 Time insights** in the Calendar sidebar shows how a week went: hours in meetings (events with other people), focus time and other events, a bar for each day, and who you meet with most. ‹ › moves between weeks. Only you see it; all-day events, out of office and invitations you declined aren't counted.
- **Working location:** set where you usually work each weekday in **Settings → Working location** (🏢 office, 🏠 home or 📍 elsewhere). In the week and day views a row above the events shows it for each day; click it to change one day (*Elsewhere…* asks where, e.g. *Client site*), or **Back to my usual week**. Colleagues see where you are today in **Contacts → Directory**, and **Home** shows yours. To plan office days, the Directory has a strip of **today and the next four working days**, each with how many are in the office (*Thu 12 · 🏢 5*; hover for home and elsewhere): pick a day to see where everyone is then, and **Where they are** shows only those *In the office*, *At home*, *Elsewhere* or who haven't said. **Home** has an **In the office today** card: the colleagues who'll be in (*You'll see 3 colleagues there* when you're in too); click its title for the Directory.
- **Running late:** from half an hour before an event until it ends, **⏱ Running late** in its window (for its organiser and guests coming) says by how much: 5, 10, 15 or 30 minutes. Everyone else on it gets a note on screen (with **Open**) and in the bell (guests outside Workspace, invited by email, get an email from you, when this server has email), and the event shows *⏱ Kim Park: 10 min late* for that time of it (a repeating event's other times aren't affected). If the event has its own channel (**Chat with guests**), *⏱ Running 10 minutes late* is said there too, as a message from you. Saying it again changes it. It's also a click away where you are: **⏱ Late** next to the event in Home's **Today**, and **5 min late** / **10 min late** on the reminder that pops up before it (also on the desktop notification, when Workspace is in a background tab, in browsers that allow buttons there such as Chrome and Edge: with a video meeting, **Join** and **5 min late**).
- **In the room or on video:** when you're invited, **How?** next to your answer lets you say **🏢 In the room** or **💻 On video** (optional; it goes with Yes or Maybe, and changing it later saves at once). The organizer sees it by each guest (*Yes, in the room*) and in the count (*2 guests: 1 yes, 1 maybe (1 in the room, 1 on video)*), handy for picking a room size. Answering No clears it.
- **Email guests:** with this server's email on, **✉ Email guests** on an event (in the organizer's window, and in a guest's invitation) opens a new message in Mail to everyone on it but you: the organizer and the guests (a guest's leaves out those who said No). The subject is the event's title, and the message starts with when and where it is and a link to it in Calendar; write the rest and send.
- **Propose a new time:** a guest can answer an invitation with **Propose a new time** (a day, times and a note); they're counted as Maybe, and the organizer gets a notification. In the event's window the organizer sees each suggestion with **Use this time**, which fills in the times to save (guests then get the usual update). Changing the time clears the suggestions. Not for repeating events.
- **Find a time:** in an event's window, **Find a time** suggests the first free times in the next two weeks, for the event's length, within everyone's working hours (every half hour), when you and every colleague you've invited are free (their own events and invitations they haven't declined; out-of-office counts too). Click one to use it. Guests outside your company can't be checked, and it says so.
- **Rooms:** admins list the company's meeting rooms (or anything else people book, like a projector) in Admin → Company settings → **Rooms**, with how many each seats, where it is (*2nd floor*) and what's in it (*Video, whiteboard*, typed with commas). In an event, **+ Add a room…** shows each room as free or taken at that time, with where it is and its equipment, best first: free rooms, then ones big enough for you and your guests (the smallest that fits first, leaving big rooms for big meetings); a room with fewer seats than people coming is marked **⚠ Too small**. **🔒 Admins only** on a room (in Admin) keeps it for the company's admins (a boardroom, the company car): others see it greyed out, can't **Book now**, and if it's added anyway it's declined, saying why. **Needs:** above the list has a button for each thing some room has (*Video*, *Whiteboard*…): pick what the meeting needs and only rooms with all of it are offered. **🚪 Rooms now** has the same buttons. A free room is booked when you save; a taken one declines (for a repeating event, if it's taken at any of its times in the next six months). Moving an event checks its rooms again. Guests see the room in the invitation. **+ See a room's bookings…** under *Other calendars* shows what a room is booked for, and by whom, in the week and day views.
- **Rooms from a spreadsheet:** under the rooms in Admin → Company settings, **Import from CSV…** takes a file with a **Name** column and any of **Seats**, **Where**, **Equipment** (several, with commas), **Admins only** and **Check-in** (yes or no): new rooms are added, ones with the same name (ignoring case) updated, and a column the file doesn't have leaves what's there. Lines it can't use are listed. **Export as CSV** gives the same columns back, to edit and import again.
- **Room usage:** under the rooms in Admin → Company settings, **Usage** shows for the last 7, 30 or 90 days how many bookings each room had, the hours booked (overlaps counted once), how much of the working time that is (weekdays, 8 hours a day, with a bar) and who booked it most, busiest first, and **No-shows**: bookings released because nobody checked in (rooms with ✅ Check-in), which shows how much booked time goes to waste. Declined bookings don't count. A room that's rarely used could become something else; one that's always taken may mean you need another.
- **Rooms now:** **🚪 Rooms now** in the Calendar sidebar (when your company has rooms) is a board of every room: a green dot and *Free until 4:15 PM* (or *Free for the next 12 hours*), or a red dot and *In use until 3:30 PM: Standup (who booked it)*, counting back-to-back bookings as one. It keeps itself up to date. **Book now** on a free room puts a meeting in your calendar with that room in it, from now for the time chosen in **Book for** (15 minutes to 2 hours; cut short if the room is booked sooner, and refused if that's in under 10 minutes). **Only free rooms** hides the ones in use. **✅ Check-in** on a room (in Admin) asks someone from each meeting there (its organizer or a guest) to press **Check in** on this board within 10 minutes of the start (*⏳ Check in by 10:10 AM or it's released*); when the meeting starts, its organizer and the guests coming get **Check in to Huddle** in the bell, which opens this board; if nobody does, the room is released for that time (one occurrence of a repeating meeting at a time), the organizer is told in the bell, and others can book it. When a room is in use for a meeting of yours (a one-off one you organise), it has **End now** (finished early: the meeting ends now in everyone's calendar and the room is free for others) and **+15 min** (run on, if nobody has the room then; otherwise it says who's after you).
- **Search:** the **Search events** box in the Calendar sidebar finds your events and invitations with all the words you type, in the title, place or notes. Coming events are listed first (a repeating one by its next time), then past ones, newest first; click one to go to its day and open it. **Clear** brings back Upcoming.
- **Print or save as PDF:** in the Calendar sidebar, pick **This week**, **This month**, **The next four weeks** or your own dates (up to three months) and see the agenda day by day: times (or *All day*), titles and places, your events and the invitations you haven't declined. **Print** prints just the agenda, on portrait pages; choose *Save as PDF* in the print window to keep a copy.
## Email

Your business fixed IP is what makes this work. Do these once:

1. **Ask your ISP** for two things:
   - Make sure port **25** is open in both directions.
   - Set **reverse DNS (PTR)** for your fixed IP to your mail hostname, e.g. `mail.yourcompany.com`. Gmail and Outlook reject mail from IPs without a matching PTR record.
2. **Mail hostname:** at your registrar, add an **A record** `mail.yourcompany.com → <fixed IP>`. This one hostname serves every company and domain.
3. **Router:** forward TCP **25, 465, 587 and 993** to the NAS.
4. **Certificate:** in DSM, go to Control Panel → Security → Certificate → Add → Let's Encrypt for `mail.yourcompany.com`. To use it in the mail container:
   - SSH in and run `sudo cat /usr/syno/etc/certificate/_archive/DEFAULT` to see the certificate folder ID. If the mail certificate isn't the default, run `sudo ls -l /usr/syno/etc/certificate/_archive/` and find the folder whose `cert.pem` shows `CN=mail.yourcompany.com` (`sudo openssl x509 -noout -subject -in …/cert.pem`).
   - Uncomment the `/etc/nw-tls` line in `docker-compose.yml`, replace `REPLACE_ME` with that ID, and rebuild.
   - DSM renews the certificate in place, and the mail container reloads it automatically.
   - Until this is done, the container uses a self-signed certificate. Webmail works, but phones will warn.
5. **Every domain:** open **Admin → Email**. Each domain shows the exact **MX, SPF, DKIM and DMARC** records to add at its DNS provider, with copy buttons. Use **Check DNS** to confirm them, and as the platform owner use **Mail server → Check** to verify the A and PTR records.
6. **Test:** send a message from webmail to a Gmail address, then in Gmail use "Show original". SPF, DKIM and DMARC should all say `PASS`.

**What's built in:**
- **Mailboxes:** every user automatically has one at their sign-in address, with its standard folders: Inbox, Drafts, Sent, Archive, Junk and Trash.
- **Aliases** (Admin → Email): one address delivers to several people or forwards outside, e.g. `sales@` → three people. Members can also send as the alias.
- **postmaster@ and abuse@** go to the company's admins automatically, as email standards require.
- **Catch-all** (Admin → Email, per domain): mail to any address on the domain that doesn't exist (typos, old addresses, `anything@`) goes to one mailbox you choose.
- **Encrypted delivery (MTA-STS)** (Admin → Email, per domain): tells other mail servers that mail to the domain must reach this server over an encrypted connection with a valid certificate, so it can't be read or quietly redirected on the way (Gmail, Outlook.com and others honour it). **Testing** first: senders still deliver if something's wrong, and send daily TLS reports to *postmaster@*; then **Required**. Turning it on adds three DNS records to the list (a CNAME for `mta-sts.<domain>` pointing at this Workspace, `_mta-sts` with an id that changes whenever the setting does, and `_smtp._tls` for the reports); Workspace serves the policy file itself at `https://mta-sts.<domain>/.well-known/mta-sts.txt`, which **Check DNS** fetches along with the records. On a Synology, add `mta-sts.<domain>` to the reverse proxy rule and the certificate used for Workspace.
- **Reports from other mail servers** (Admin → Email → **Read reports**): Google, Microsoft, Yahoo and others send postmaster@ (which reaches the admins) a daily report for each domain, as zipped attachments nobody reads. Workspace reads the last 30 days of them from your own Inbox and Archive and adds them up per domain: **DMARC** (how many messages were sent as the domain, from which servers, how many passed SPF and DKIM, and whether receivers delivered them, sent them to spam or rejected them, so you can spot a newsletter service that isn't set up, or someone pretending to be you) and **TLS** (how many connections to you were encrypted, and why any failed: an expired certificate, a name mismatch…). The same report counted once even if it arrived twice; nothing is stored.
- **Spam blocklists** (platform owner): Admin → Email → **Check blocklists** looks up the mail server's public address on the main spam blocklists (Spamhaus ZEN, SpamCop, Barracuda, PSBL, Mailspike, UCEPROTECT). Mail from a listed address is refused or sent to Junk at many companies, after a hacked account sent spam or because someone used the address before you.
