It names the domain and the records (*acme.com: SPF, DMARC not right*), and the platform owners get a notification (*Needs attention: Mail DNS records*), as for the other checks, so a DNS change at your registrar that breaks mail doesn't go unnoticed.
- **Outgoing mail queue** lists mail the other side hasn't taken yet: to whom, from whom, how long it has waited and why (*Connection timed out*, a refusal from the other server, …). The mail server keeps trying for five days before returning it to the sender; **Retry** tries one again now (after fixing what was wrong), **Retry all** the whole queue, and **Delete** drops a message without telling the sender (in the activity log). An **Outgoing mail** check warns when something has waited over an hour, and needs attention after a day, which is how you'd notice that, say, your internet provider blocks port 25 or a big provider refuses this server's mail. The mail container reports its queue every few seconds to `mail-status/`, and carries out the requests the app leaves in `mail-config/queue/`.

**Database snapshots** lists the daily snapshots to take one now or download one (see [Backups](#backups)).

## Announcements (admins)

**Admin → Announcements** puts a short notice at the top of every page for everyone in the company: an office closure, a maintenance window, a new policy. Give it the text (up to 300 characters), an optional link (a page in the suite like `/drive`, or an `https://` address), a style (**Info** or **Warning**, which is amber) and, if you like, an end date after which it disappears by itself. It shows up straight away for everyone signed in. Each person can dismiss it with the × and it stays gone for them. **Showing now** lists what's up, with how many people have dismissed each one, and **Take down** removes one for everyone. Posting and taking down are recorded in the activity log.

- **For one group:** **Who sees it** picks one of the company's groups (Admin → Groups) instead of everyone: only its members see the notice (*Night shift: new rota*). The list says who it's for and how many people that is.
- **Starting later:** **Starts** sets a day and time to show it from (within a year), such as the evening before a maintenance window, and the button becomes **Schedule**. Until then it waits under **Scheduled** (with **Cancel**), and nobody sees it; when the time comes it appears for everyone with Workspace open, without reloading, and moves to **Showing now**. *Ends* counts from the start.
- **By email too:** with this server's email on, **Also email it to them** sends the announcement as an email from you to the people it's for (everyone, or the group's members), as well as showing it in Workspace: the subject starts *Announcement:* (or *Important:*), with the link and who it went to. A scheduled one is emailed when it starts. Recipients are hidden from each other (Bcc), and a copy comes to you. The list shows *✉ emailed to 12*; if the mail server couldn't take it at all, it tries again a minute later.

## Data protection (admins)

**Admin → Data protection** sets rules for what may not leave the company, by email or from Drive, so card numbers, bank details or a confidential project's name aren't sent or shared out by mistake.

- **What a rule looks for:** **credit card numbers** or **bank account numbers (IBAN)** (only ones with valid check digits, so order numbers, dates and phone numbers don't trip them), **words or phrases** (whole words, any capitals: *Project Falcon, payroll*), or **a pattern of your own** (a regular expression, such as a national ID number's shape; patterns that would be slow to check are refused).
- **When:** sent to **people outside the company** (the usual choice: colleagues can still email each other) or to **anyone**.
- **Then:** **Warn** asks the sender to check (*Check before sending*, with what was found, the numbers shown only by their last four characters); they can **Edit message** or **Send anyway**. **Block** stops the message, says why, and leaves it open to change. Blocks and sends after a warning are recorded in **Activity**.
- It looks at the subject and the message of email sent from Workspace: replies, forwards, scheduled and confidential messages and mail merges (with the values filled in). **Try** in a rule's window checks it on some text first. Rules can be switched off without deleting them.
- **Files:** the same rules look through a file before it leaves the company from Drive: making a **link for anyone** (a folder: every file in it, up to 200), **Publish to the web**, or sharing with someone at **another company**. They read Docs, Sheets and Slides, Word, Excel and PowerPoint files and text files (the file's name too). A block says which file and what was found; a warning asks *Check before sharing* (**Don't share** / **Share anyway**, which is recorded). The same question comes up when Mail, Chat or Docs offer to share a linked file with people who can't open it (Mail sends the message only once the file is shared). File requests (links that only take files in) aren't checked, as they show nothing.
- Mail sent from phone and computer mail apps (IMAP/SMTP), pictures and PDFs aren't checked.

## Legal hold (admins)

When the company has to keep someone's data (a dispute, an audit, an investigation), **Admin → Users → ⋮ → Put on legal hold…** keeps everything of theirs from being deleted for good until the hold is lifted. Say why (*Supplier dispute, case 2026-14*); it's recorded, with who did it and when, and shown when you point at the **On hold** label beside their name. They aren't told.

- **What it stops:** the company's clean-ups skip them (Drive's trash after so many days, **Keeping chat**, and the automatic emptying of their Mail Trash and Junk), and deleting for good is refused, with the reason given: *Delete forever* and *Empty trash* in Drive, deleting a file's versions, deleting their Chat messages (or a thread they replied in), and deleting mail for good from Trash or Junk in Mail. Their account can't be deleted. In the Drive trash their files say **Kept** instead of when they go.
- **What it doesn't stop:** moving things to the trash (which keeps them), editing, or mail apps on phones and Outlook deleting mail themselves; download their data with **Download their data (.zip)** at the start if that matters. Files in shared drives are kept when the person on hold created them.
- **Lift legal hold** (same menu) puts everything back to normal; both are in the activity log.

## Security checkup (admins)

**Admin → Security checkup** lists the company's open security points, worst first (⚠ needs attention, 💡 worth doing, ✓ done), each with what to do and a button to the Admin tab that fixes it: admins who sign in with just a password; people without two-step verification or a passkey (or how many still have to set it up, when it's required); accounts nobody has used for 90 days; mail forwarded outside the company by itself; share links that never expire and have no password; phishing reports nobody has dealt with; and settings worth having (marking mail from outside the company, signing people out when they're away).

## Activity log

**Admin → Activity** shows each company's admins what happened in their company, newest first:

- **Sign-ins:** successful and failed sign-ins, sign-outs, devices signed out, password changes, two-step verification turned on or off, and mail-app passwords.
- **People and settings:** people added, changed or deleted, company settings, mail domains and aliases.
- **Files:** sharing changes, public links made or removed, files deleted forever, and the trash being emptied.

Each entry shows who did it, what it was done to, the details and the IP address. Filter by kind, search for a person, file or address, or pick dates. **Download CSV** saves what's shown, for a spreadsheet or your records. Admins see only their own company; the platform owner can pick any company. Entries are kept for a year (set `AUDIT_DAYS` to change this).

**Security alerts:** a company's admins also get a notification right away when someone is made an admin or loses admin rights, two-step verification is reset or turned off, one account has five failed sign-ins within 15 minutes (once an hour at most), or an admin downloads someone else's data. Clicking it opens **Admin → Activity**. Whoever made the change isn't told about their own action. Each admin can turn these off in the bell's settings (*Security alerts*).

**New sign-ins:** when your account signs in on a kind of device it hasn't used before (another browser or system, e.g. *Safari on iPhone*), you get a notification at once on the devices you're already using, with the address it came from and a link to Settings to change your password or sign that device out. Your very first sign-in isn't flagged. Turn it off in the bell's settings if you like.

## Tasks

- **Lists:** everyone starts with **My tasks**; **+** next to Lists makes more. Type in **Add a task** and press Enter: new tasks go on top. Drag tasks to put them in order.
- **Colour and emoji:** a list's **⋮ → Colour and emoji…** (for its owner) gives it one of nine colours and an emoji (🚀, 🏠, 🛒… or any you type). They show beside its name in the list on the left and in its heading, with its tasks in My Day, Planned, Important and Assigned to me, and on the Calendar; everyone the list is shared with sees them.
- **Quick add:** type the details along with the task: a day (*today*, *tomorrow*, *Friday*, *on fri*, *next week*, *in 3 days*, *24 Dec*, *Dec 24*, *24/12*, *2026-12-24*), a time (*3pm*, *at 9:30am*, *15:00*), a repeat (*daily*, *every weekday*, *every week*, *monthly*, *yearly*), **!** for important, and on a shared list **@name** for who it's for. "Send invoice tomorrow 3pm every month !" adds *Send invoice*, due tomorrow at 15:00, repeating monthly, starred. What's understood shows under the box before you press Enter; **Keep as typed** adds the words as they are. A time or repeat without a day means today.
- **Comments:** a task's details have a **Comments** box for talking it over; everyone on the list sees them as they're posted, and the list shows 💬 with how many. The person it's for, whoever made it and anyone who has commented get a notification. You can delete your own comments.
- **Board:** **List | Board** at the top of a list shows it as a board with **To do**, **In progress** and **Done** columns. Drag a card to another column (or use its ‹ › arrows); moving one to Done ticks it off, and back takes it off again. In progress shows on the task in the list too, and everyone sharing the list sees the same columns. Each list remembers which view you chose (in this browser).
- **Timeline:** **List | Board | Timeline** also shows a list as bars on a strip of days, from each task's start to its due date (just the due day if it has no start), with today marked and weekends shaded; tasks without a due date are listed underneath. Drag a bar to move it, or its left or right end to change the start or due date; or select it and use ← → (a day at a time), Shift+← → (the due date) and Alt+← → (the start). Click a bar to open the task. **Starts** in a task's details sets the start (it can't be after the due date, and goes when the due date is cleared); a repeating task's next one starts as long before its due date. Everyone on a shared list sees changes as they happen.
- **Priority:** **Priority** in a task's details sets it to **High** (!!!), **Medium** (!!) or **Low** (!), shown on the task in red, amber or blue (everyone on the list sees it); **None** takes it off. Quick add understands **!high**, **!medium** (or **!med**) and **!low** (also **!p1** to **!p3**; a lone **!** still means important). **⇅ → Priority** sorts the list high first, then medium, low and none. The board's cards show it too, and **Select**'s bar sets (or takes off) the priority of several tasks at once. Duplicates, the next time of a repeating task and copies of a list keep it; the CSV and Excel downloads have a **Priority** column, and an import reads one (High, Medium, Low or 1 to 3).
- **Estimates:** **Estimate** in a task's details says how long you expect it to take (15 minutes to 8 hours). It shows on the task (*~1 h 30 min*, or *⏱ 20 min of ~1 h 30 min* once time has been spent), and the open tasks' estimates are added up beside the list's name (*~4 h to do*), in My Day too. Duplicates and the next time of a repeating task keep it. **+ Book time on my calendar** starts as long as the estimate, and the list's CSV and Excel downloads have an **Estimate (min)** column (read back by **Import from CSV…**).
- **Time spent:** a task's details have a **Time** section. **▶ Start timer** starts yours (starting one on another task stops the first; one left running counts at most 12 hours) and **■ Stop timer** stops it; **Add time** records time by hand (*45m*, *1h 30m*, *2:15*; a day, and what it was for). The task shows ⏱ the total in the list, and ● with the name of anyone timing it, for everyone on the list as it happens. Everyone on the list sees the entries and each person's total; you can remove only your own. **⋮ → Time report…** shows who spent how long on which task between two dates, with **Download CSV** for Excel or Sheets.
- **Sections:** **+ Add section** under a list splits it into parts with headings (*This week*, *Later*). Drag a task onto another section's tasks (or into an empty one) to move it there, or pick its **Section** in its details; a section's ⋯ has **Add a task here…**, **Rename section…**, **Move section up / down** and **Delete section…** (its tasks stay on the list under no section, or go with it if you choose). Tasks under no section come first. Click a section's name (▾) to fold it away and see only its heading and count (▸ opens it again); that's your own choice, kept on every device, and others on a shared list keep theirs. Everyone on the list sees the same sections; a task moved to another list leaves its section behind, and **Make a copy…** copies the sections too. A list's CSV and Excel downloads have a **Section** column (tasks in order, section by section), and **Import from CSV…** reads one back: tasks go into the section named (one already on the list, whatever the capitals, or a new one).
- **Sorting:** **⇅** at the top of a list sorts it your way: **My order** (drag to arrange), **Due date** (timed ones first on a day, undated at the end), **Title**, **Recently added** or **Starred first**. It's your own choice for each list, remembered on every device; others on a shared list keep theirs.
- **Copying a list:** **⋮ → Make a copy…** makes a new list from one you use again and again (onboarding, month-end, an event): its open tasks with their notes, steps (unticked), tags, stars, links, files and what each waits on. Not who they're for, comments or time. Give a **Start on** day and the due dates move so the earliest falls on it, keeping the same gaps; without one, the copy has no due dates.
- **Download or print a list:** **⋮ → Download as CSV** (or **Download as Excel (.xlsx)**, with due dates as real dates and the heading row frozen) saves the list as a spreadsheet (Excel, Sheets or Numbers): each task with its status, due date and time, start, who it's for, importance, tags, steps (how many done, and each one) and notes, open tasks first. **⋮ → Print list** prints it (or saves it as PDF) with tick boxes to tick by hand, open tasks first and then the completed ones.
- **Send by email:** with this server's email on, **⋮ → Send by email** opens a new message in Mail with the list in it: its name as the subject, the open tasks as a checklist (★ for important ones, due dates, who they're for, steps) and a link back to the list. **Send by email, with completed** adds the done ones under *Done*, struck through. Add who it's for and send: handy for a shopping list or a handover to someone without Workspace.
- **Import from CSV:** **⋮ → Import from CSV…** adds tasks from a spreadsheet saved as CSV (from Excel, Sheets, another to-do app, or a list downloaded here) to the end of the list. Only a **Task** (or Title or Name) column is needed; **Due** (2026-12-01, 1/12/2026 or Dec 1 2026), **Time**, **Notes**, **Tags**, **Important**, **Steps** (one per line, *[x]* for done) and **Status** (*Done*) are used when they're there. Up to 1,000 tasks at a time. A **Todoist** export (a project's CSV) works too: its sections become sections, subtasks become the task's steps, comments go into its notes, *@labels* become tags and priorities 1–3 become high, medium and low. An **Asana** export works the same way: **Section/Column** gives the section, **Completed At** marks a task done, and subtasks (rows with a **Parent task**) become their parent's steps, as they do in any file with that column.
- **Several lists from one spreadsheet:** **Import lists from CSV…** in the Tasks sidebar takes a CSV with a **List** column (such as *Tasks.csv* from **Settings → Download your data**, or one you make) and makes a new list for each name in it (*Home (2)* if you have a Home already), its tasks read the same way as **Import from CSV** (a date in a **Done** column means done).
- **From Google Tasks:** **Import from Google Tasks…** (at the bottom of the left side) takes *Tasks.json* from the *Tasks* folder of a Google Takeout download. Each of its lists comes in as a new list: tasks in their order with their notes and due dates, the done ones ticked off (with when), and subtasks as steps of their task. Deleted tasks and empty lists are left out.
- **From Trello:** **Import a Trello board…** takes a board's JSON (in Trello: board menu → *Print, export and share → Export as JSON*) and makes it a list: Trello's lists become its sections, in order, and each card a task with its description as notes, its due date, its checklists as steps (ticks kept) and its labels as tags (a label with no name by its colour). Cards marked complete come in done; archived cards and lists are left out. Its Board view then looks much as it did in Trello.
- **Several at once:** **Select** above a list (or in Planned, Important, Assigned to me or a tag) puts a box by each open task; tick some (or click them, or **Select all**) and the bar changes them together: **✓ Mark done**, a **Due** date or **No date**, **★ Important**, **Section…** (on a list with sections: put them all in one, or in none), **Move to…** another list, **Download (CSV)** (just those tasks, from one list or several, in the same columns as a list's download), **Print** (just those, printed as a list prints), **Send in Chat…** (links to them in a conversation's message box; they show as task cards), **Email** (a new message with just those, as a list's email has them), or **Delete** (asked first). Every task is checked first, so if one can't be changed (it's on a list you've left, say), none is; a repeating task ticked off brings its next one, as one at a time does. **Done selecting** goes back.
- **History:** **History** at the bottom of a task's details lists who changed what on it, newest first, down to who added it: *Kim Park took it on*, *changed the due date from 1 Dec to 3 Dec*, *set the priority to High*, *added #venue*, *moved it from To do to In progress*, *ticked it off*, *moved it from Launch to Later*, *added the steps “Call”, “Pay”*, *ticked off the step “Call”*, *attached “Plan.pdf”*, *made it wait on “Design”*, *changed the notes* (what they said isn't kept again). **Skip this time** shows as the due date it moved; moving steps up and down isn't a change. Quick changes in a row by the same person to the same thing are one entry (typing a new title, a date changed twice), and ticking off and **Undo** leave nothing. It updates while it's open as people change the task, only people on the list can read it, it comes back with a task restored from **Deleted tasks…**, and the last 200 changes are kept.
- **Activity:** a list's **⋮ → Activity…** shows what's happened on it lately, newest first under each day: every change to its tasks (as in a task's History), tasks added, and tasks deleted (*Kim Park deleted it · Order cake (deleted)*). Click a task's name to open it; **Show older** goes further back. Everyone on the list can see it; a task moved to another list takes its history with it.
- **Duplicate:** **Duplicate** at the bottom of a task's details puts a copy just below it, with its notes, steps (unticked), dates, repeat, who it's for, star, tags, link and files (not its comments or time).
- **Ticking off:** the box marks a task done (with **Undo**); done tasks move to **Completed** at the bottom, and **⋮ → Delete completed tasks** clears them.
- **Deleted tasks:** deleting a task (one, several at once, the completed ones, or a section with its tasks) moves it to the list's trash, with **Undo** right after. **⋮ → Deleted tasks…** lists what was deleted in the last 30 days, by whom, and **Restore** puts a task back as it was: notes, steps, dates, comments and time spent. Everyone on the list sees the same trash.
- **Details:** click a task for notes, a due date (shown as Today, Tomorrow or the date; overdue in red) and, if you like, a time on it ("Today 15:30"; red once it's passed; it shows on the calendar too, and a note pops up at that time while Workspace is open, with a desktop notification when those are on), who it's for, and which list it's on. Changes save as you make them.
- **Chat with the list's people:** on a shared list, **⋮ → Chat with the list's people** opens a private Chat channel for it, with its owner and everyone it's shared with (from your company), its link posted first. It's made the first time; after that it's the same channel, with anyone the list was shared with since added.
- **Links to tasks:** 🔗 **Copy link to task** at the top of a task's details copies its address. Pasted in Chat, it shows as a card under the message: ☐ or ✅ and the task, whether it's to do, in progress or done, its list, who it's for, steps done, comments and when it's due (red when overdue), as it is when the message is shown. Clicking it opens the task. People who aren't on the list see *A task you can't see* instead, never its name. Under the card, **✓ Mark done** (or **↺ Not done yet**) ticks it off right there, for anyone on its list. The chat button next to it, in a task's details, puts the task's link in a conversation's message box.
- **Search:** the **Search tasks** box finds tasks with all the words you type in their title, notes or steps, on your own lists and those shared with you (open ones first, soonest due first, then done ones). Click one to open it in its list.
- **Schedule time:** **+ Book time on my calendar** in a task's details puts a block on your calendar for working on it: pick when and for how long (15 minutes to 4 hours). Before you book, it warns if that time clashes with something on your calendar (meetings you're going to or haven't answered; events you've marked *free* don't count). The event is called *✓ (the task)* and links back to the task (**Open task** in its window), and the task lists the time you've booked (📅 Thu 12 Nov, 9:00 – 10:00), each opening the event in Calendar. It's only on your own calendar, even on a shared list.
- **Steps:** a task can hold a checklist. Type in **Add a step** in its details; tick steps off, click one to rename it, or **×** to remove it. The list shows progress on the task (☑ 2/5, green when all are done), and everyone on a shared list sees the same steps. A repeating task's next one comes with the same steps, none ticked.
- **Files:** **From Drive** in a task's details attaches a document, spreadsheet, presentation or any file you can open; **Upload** (or dropping files on *Files*) puts files from your computer in a **Task files** folder in your Drive and attaches them. Everyone on the list can then view the files that someone on the list owns, including people added to the list later; files they still can't open show as "no access". The list shows 📎 with how many. **×** takes one off (nobody loses access they were given). A repeating task's next one keeps the files.
- **Waiting on other tasks:** **Waiting on** in a task's details picks tasks on the same list that need doing first (*Print flyers* waits on *Design* and *Write copy*). The list and the board show ⏳ **Waiting on …** until they're all ticked off; then whoever the task is for (or whoever added it) gets a notification that it can start. Tasks can't wait on each other in a loop. Deleting a task, or moving one to another list, takes it out of what others wait on.
- **Repeating:** **Repeat** in a task's details (every day, weekday, week, month or year, or **Custom…**: every N days, weeks, months or years, and for weeks on the days you pick, such as every 2 weeks on Monday and Thursday). Quick add understands *every 2 weeks*, *every other month*, *every tue and thu* and *every 2 weeks on mon, wed* too (a repeat on chosen days starts on the first of them). Ticking it off adds the next one, due on the same schedule from today on (missed ones are skipped, and the 31st becomes the month's last day); **Undo** takes it away again unless someone has already changed it. Clearing the due date stops it repeating. **⏭ Skip this time** (at the bottom of its details) moves a repeating task on to its next date without ticking it off (days already missed are skipped too). **Stop repeating after** (under Repeat) picks the last day it may come due: ticking off the last one before then adds no next one, and Skip says it is the last time. Duplicates and copied lists keep the end day; unticking it repeats for good again.
- **Sharing:** **Share** on a list lets colleagues see it, add and tick off tasks, and be given tasks. Everyone sees changes as they happen. **Assigned to me** gathers the tasks given to you on any list, soonest due first. Leaving a list (or being taken off it) leaves the tasks you were given with nobody.
- **Are they around?** A task's details say when the person it's for is away (their Mail vacation reply is on: *🌴 Kim is away until 16 Nov*) or outside the working hours they've set (*🌙 It's 23:10 for Kim, outside their working hours*), so a task given today isn't expected back tonight.
- **Tags:** type **#client** (any word starting with a letter) in **Add a task**, or add tags in a task's details (× takes one off). They show on the task, **Tags** on the left lists every tag on your open tasks with how many, and picking one shows its open tasks from every list you're on (soonest due first), so *#client* gathers work spread over several lists. Search finds tags too. Anyone on a list can tag its tasks; a repeating task keeps its tags.
- **My Day:** **☀ My Day** at the top of Tasks is your list for today, picked from any of your lists: **☀** in a task's details adds it (or takes it off), and **Suggestions** underneath offers what's due today or overdue, each with **+**. Tick them off there (they stay under *Completed* for the day); the count beside My Day is what's left. It's yours alone (others on a shared list don't see what you picked), and it starts empty each morning: the tasks themselves stay on their lists as they were.
- **Planned:** **Planned** on the left gathers the open tasks with a due date from all your lists (those given to you or to nobody), in **Overdue**, **Today**, **Tomorrow**, **Next 7 days** and **Later**, soonest first, each with the list it's on. Its count is what's due today or overdue. Tick one off right there, or click it to open it.
- **Completed:** **Completed** on the left shows what you ticked off in the last 30 days, from all your lists, under **Today**, **Yesterday** and each earlier day, newest first, each with the list it's on, for a look back over your week. **Last 7 days** or **Last 30 days** at the top chooses how far back (remembered on that device). Only what you did yourself is there (not what others on a shared list finished). Untick one to put it back on its list.
- **Important:** the ☆ on a task marks it important (anyone on the list can, and everyone sees it). **Important** at the top of Tasks gathers the open important tasks from all your lists, soonest due first, with the list each is on. Ticking one off or taking its star off removes it; a repeating task's next one stays important.
- **From an email or a chat message:** the ✓ button on an open email, or on a chat message (hover over it), adds it to **My tasks**: an email's subject or the message's first line becomes the task, and **Open** in the note goes to it. The task shows ✉ or 💬, and its details link back to the email or message. The email is found even after it's been archived or moved to another folder. **Remove** next to the link takes it off.
- **Reminders:** each morning (from 9:00, or when you first open Workspace after that) a note lists what's due today and overdue, with a button to the tasks. When someone gives you a task you get a note straight away. Both show once, in one tab, and as desktop notifications too when those are on.
- **On the calendar:** open tasks with a due date (given to you, or to nobody on your own lists) show in Calendar as all-day items. Tick the box to complete one there, or click it to open it in Tasks. **Show tasks with due dates** in the Calendar sidebar hides them.

## Forms

**Forms** in the left bar makes questionnaires, sign-up sheets and quick polls.

- **Templates:** above your forms, **Start a new form** offers a blank form or one to start from: **Event sign-up** (sessions with limited places, dietary needs), **Feedback survey** (a star rating and a 0–10 recommend scale), **RSVP**, **Contact details** (the email address checked), **Job application** (with a CV upload), **Quiz** (right answers and points set, scored), **Time off request** and **Order form**. Each comes with its questions, a description, a message after sending and the settings it needs (one response each for sign-ups, scoring for the quiz); change anything afterwards.
- **Questions:** **New form**, give it a title and a description, and add questions: short answer, paragraph, multiple choice, tick boxes, dropdown, a 1–5 or 1–10 scale (with optional labels for each end), a **rating** (3 to 10 stars, hearts or thumbs; the Summary shows the average), a date, a **time**, a **file upload**, a multiple-choice or tick-box **grid**, a **ranking**, or a **signature**. Each can have help text and be *Required*. Move questions up and down, duplicate or delete them. Everything saves as you type.
- **Import questions:** **⋮ → Import questions…** copies questions from another form you own or edit: pick the form, tick the questions (all at first), and they're added at the end of this one; the other form doesn't change. Sections and the jumps between them come along when you bring both (a jump to a section you left behind is dropped), and so do answer keys, validation and option limits. Pictures come too when they belong to someone on this form; others are left off, and you're told how many.
- **Theme:** **🎨 Theme** at the top of a form gives it a look of its own: a **colour** (nine to choose from) for the bar across the top of the form, the buttons and the ticked options; a **page** that's plain or tinted with that colour; a **font** (Basic, Decorative, Formal or Playful); and a **header picture** across the top (a wide one fits best, about 4:1), kept in your Drive and shown to everyone who fills the form in, like question pictures. Changes save as you pick them, with a small preview in the window; **Reset to plain** takes them all off. **Make a copy** keeps the theme.
- **Pictures:** **🖼 Add picture** under a question's help text puts a picture with it (a chart to read, a product, two logo designs to choose between), with a description for screen readers. It's kept in your Drive, and everyone who can fill the form in sees it (people outside the company too, for a form for anyone with the link), even though the picture file itself isn't shared with them. **Change** or **Remove picture** below it. On a multiple-choice or tick-box question, **🖼** beside an option gives that option a picture of its own, shown with it (choose between logos, products or rooms); it follows the option if you rename it.
- **Grids:** a *Multiple choice grid* asks about several things on one scale (rows such as *Talks*, *Food*; columns such as *Poor*, *Good*, *Great*), one choice per row; a *Tick box grid* allows several per row. Up to 30 rows and 20 columns. A required grid needs every row answered. The **Summary** shows how many chose each column on each row, each response lists them row by row, and the CSV, the linked spreadsheet and emailed copies have one column (or line) per row, like *Rate the day [Food]*.
- **Rankings:** a *Ranking* question lists 2 to 10 options for people to put in order, first choice at the top: **↑ ↓** beside each (or drag them). An untouched ranking isn't an answer yet (a required one asks for it); **Keep this order** answers as it stands. *Shuffle option order* gives each person their own starting order. The **Summary** lists the options by their average place, with how many put each first; each response, the CSV and the linked spreadsheet show the order (*1. Faro; 2. Lisbon; 3. Porto*). The server checks every option is there once.
- **Finish later:** when you're signed in, what you've answered on a form is saved as you go (*Your answers are saved: come back to finish any time*), along with the page you're on. Close the page, come back on any device, and your answers are back where you left them, with **Start over** to begin again. It's yours alone, nobody else sees it, and it isn't a response until you send it. Sending the form, **Clear form** or **Start over** deletes it, and so does leaving it untouched for 30 days. File uploads aren't kept (pick the files again), and answers to questions that have since changed are left out. Forms for anyone with the link keep nothing for people who aren't signed in.
- **Signatures:** a *Signature* question has people sign in a box on the form with a mouse, pen or finger (**Clear** starts again), or choose **Type it instead** and type their name, which is set in a handwriting style (for keyboard and screen reader users). Either way it's kept as a small picture with the response: a required one can't be sent unsigned, and the server takes only a PNG picture (up to 100 KB). The **Summary** shows the signatures side by side, each response shows its own, and printing (responses or the summary) includes them; in the CSV, the linked spreadsheet and emailed copies the answer reads *Signed*. Someone changing their response sees the signature they gave, with **Sign again**. Printed blank for paper, it's a line to sign on. Good for consent forms, permission slips and time-off requests.
- **File uploads:** a *File upload* question takes up to 1–5 files (you choose), 10 MB each. People pick them on the form (each is sent straight away, with × to take one back) and they arrive with the response, in a **{form name} (File uploads)** folder in the form owner's Drive (named after the person for company forms). The responses link to them, for the owner and editors; the CSV and Sheets copy list the file names. Uploads that never become part of a response are thrown away after a day. **Only some kinds, and smaller:** under the question, tick kinds of file (PDFs, pictures, documents, spreadsheets, presentations, videos, zip files; none ticked takes anything) and pick a smaller **MB each** (1, 2 or 5). The form then says *PDFs or pictures: up to 2 files, 1 MB each*, its file picker offers only those, and anything else is refused as it's picked, saying why; the server checks again, by the file's name and by what's in it (a renamed program called *receipt.pdf* isn't taken), and a file counts only for the question it was sent for.
- **Response validation:** tick **Response validation** under a short answer, paragraph or tick-box question to check answers before they're sent. Short answers can be a **number** (any number, a whole number, greater or less than, equal to, between or not between), **text** (contains, doesn't contain, an email address, a web address), a **length** (at most or at least so many characters) or a **pattern** (a regular expression, such as `^[A-Z]{2}[0-9]{4}$` for a member number); paragraphs a length or a pattern; tick boxes **at least**, **at most** or **exactly** so many choices. Add your own message if you like ("Members must be adults"). People see what's wrong when they leave the box (tick boxes straight away), and can't go on or submit until it fits; the server checks again. An empty answer is left to *Required*.
- **“Other”:** under a multiple-choice or tick-box question's options, **add “Other”** gives people an *Other:* choice with a box to type their own answer (typing chooses it). The **Summary** counts *Other* with the options and lists what people typed; downloads and the linked spreadsheet show the typed answer.
- **Shuffle option order:** tick it under a multiple-choice, tick-box or dropdown question and each person sees the options in their own random order (so the first one isn't favoured); results and downloads keep your order.
- **Limit places per option:** for sign-ups, tick it under a multiple-choice, tick-box or dropdown question and give an option a number of places (*Morning session: 12*; empty means no limit). People see how many places are left, and a full option can't be chosen any more (it shows **Full**). Two people taking the last place at once can't both get it, and changing your own response keeps your place.
- **Pre-filled links:** **⋮ → Get pre-filled link** shows the questions: answer the ones you want filled in for people (a department, an event, a name) and **Copy link**. The form opens with those answers, which people can still change before sending. Answers that don't fit a question are ignored.
- **Sections:** **Add section** starts a new page (with a title and description); people go through the pages with **Next** and **Back**, and each page's required questions are checked before moving on. A multiple-choice or dropdown question can send people to a later section depending on their answer, or straight to the end (**Go to a section depending on the answer**). Each section can also say where to go **After this section** (a later section, or **Submit the form**), so different answers can lead down separate paths that each end on their own. Questions on pages someone skipped aren't required, and anything typed there isn't kept. Sections don't appear in the responses, CSV or summary.
- **Send:** **Send** at the top sends the form by email from you: the people (addresses, or a group's address), an optional message, and the link. Below, **Sent to … · … answered** lists them with **Answered** or **Waiting** (on a company form by their account; on a form for anyone with the link, by the email address they give when *Collect email addresses* is on) and when each was sent and reminded; **Remind … who haven't answered** sends those still waiting a reminder (saying when the form closes, if it does). A company group's address (or another of the company's aliases) sends it to each person in it, counted one by one. With a closing date set, tick **Remind those who haven't answered a day before it closes** and the reminder goes by itself, once (again if you change the closing date). **Link and QR code** shows the link with a QR code to copy, download and print on a poster or show on a screen: people point their phone's camera at it.
- **Sharing:** **Copy link** gives the form's address (`/f/…`). Under **Settings → Who can fill it in**, choose **People at your company** (they sign in, on the form's page if they aren't already, and their name and address are recorded) or **Anyone with the link** (no sign-in, anonymous; not offered if your company has turned off links for people outside it). Company forms can be limited to **One response per person**. **Preview** opens the form as others see it. **Get a new link** stops the old link working.
- **In Chat:** **Send → Link and QR code → Send in Chat…** puts the form's link in the message box of the conversation you choose and opens it there. A form's link pasted in a conversation shows as a card under the message: the title, the start of the description, how many questions, whether it's open (or why not), and **Fill in** (it opens in a new tab). Once you've sent a response it says **✓ You've responded** (with **Respond again** if more than one is allowed). The form's owner and editors also see how many responses have come in. A form for another company, or one that has been deleted, shows as *A form you can't open*.
- **Email addresses and copies:** on a form for anyone with the link, **Settings → Collect email addresses** asks people for theirs before the questions (required); it's kept with their response, shown in the responses and in the CSV and Sheets copy. **Send people a copy of their answers by email** (when this server has email) mails each person what they sent, from the form owner's address, with their score on a quiz and their *change your response* link when that's on. On company forms the copy goes to the address they're signed in with. **Only addresses at** (under *Collect email addresses*: *acme.com, partner.org*) takes addresses at those domains only (subdomains such as *eu.acme.com* too): others are refused, saying which are taken, and the form says so above the address box. **One response per email address** (under *Collect email addresses*) lets each address answer once: a second response from it (in any capitals) is refused, saying so, and the form says so above the address box.
- **Changing a response:** **Settings → Let people change their response after sending it** gives everyone who sends one a private **Change your response** link (on a company form with one response each, it's also offered when they open the form again). It brings their answers back to change and send again, checked like a new response; files already sent stay as they were. The owner sees one response, marked *changed* with when, and editors are notified if notifications are on. Changes stop when the form stops taking responses, or when the setting is turned off.
- **After submitting:** people see your **Message after submitting** (or "Your response has been recorded"), and can send another response unless it's one each.
- **Filtering responses:** with two or more responses, **Show responses where** picks a question and then an answer: an option (or *Other*), a number on a scale or rating, the option ranked first, or words in a written answer; or simply *Answered* or *Left blank*. The count says *2 of 4 responses*, and the Summary, **Individual**, **Print**, **Print summary**, **Download CSV** and **Download Excel** all follow the filter (the server filters the downloads by the same rules). Change the question or the answer, or **Clear filter**, at any time; nothing is deleted.
- **What others answered:** tick **Settings → Show people who've answered what others answered** (good for polls and sign-ups) and, once they've sent a response, people get **See what others answered**: how many responded, and for choices, tick boxes, dropdowns, scales, ratings, grids and rankings how the answers went (bars with counts and percentages, averages, average places). Questions people type into, upload to or sign only show how many answered: who said what, and anything written, stays private, and the server never sends it. Someone on a company form who has responded can see it again from the form (*You have already filled in this form · See what others answered*); on a form for anyone, the browser it was sent from keeps a private link to it. Untick the setting and it's closed again to everyone but the form's owner and editors.
- **On paper:** the form's **⋮ → Print a blank copy** prints it to fill in by hand: its title and description, then each question numbered with room to answer (circles for one choice, boxes for several, writing lines, a grid's rows and columns, a scale's numbers between its labels, a blank to number each option of a ranking), required ones marked *, and each section starting a new page.
- **Responses:** the **Responses** tab updates live as answers arrive: a **Summary** per question (how many chose each option, with bars and percentages, the average on a scale, and the written answers) or each response on its own (**Individual**, with who sent it, and a button to delete it). **Download CSV** saves them all (Excel-safe), **Download Excel** as an .xlsx workbook (the heading row frozen and filterable, dates and numbers as such, answers kept as text, never formulas), and **Open in Sheets** puts a copy of the responses so far in a new spreadsheet in your Drive. **Print** puts every response on its own page (when it came, who sent it, a quiz's score with ✓ and ✗, then each question with its answer: grids row by row, rankings numbered, uploaded files by name), for paper records or a PDF. On **Individual**, the 🖨 button prints just the response shown. **Print summary** prints the Summary as a report to hand round: how many answered each question, the bars with counts and percentages, grids as tables of counts, averages, rankings and the written answers. **Delete all** clears them.
- **Linked spreadsheet:** **Link to Sheets** on the Responses tab makes a spreadsheet in your Drive with the responses so far and keeps it linked: each new response is added as a row as it arrives (live, if someone has the spreadsheet open), and a response changed afterwards is updated in its row. Columns are found by their headings, so you can move them, add your own columns or notes beside them, and a question added later gets a new column at the end. A *Response ID* column ties each row to its response. **Linked spreadsheet** opens it; **Unlink** stops adding to it (it stays in your Drive). **Open in Sheets** still makes a one-off copy.
- **Finding and archiving forms:** above your forms, **Search your forms** finds them by title, and the menu beside it shows **All forms**, those **Taking responses**, **Closed**, **Shared with me** or **Archived**. A form's ⋮ → **Archive** (for its owner) takes a form you're done with out of the list and stops it taking responses (its link says so); its responses stay. **Bring back from the archive** in the same menu restores it as it was.
- **Closing:** untick **Accepting responses** and the link shows that the form is closed. Or let it close by itself: **Settings → Close automatically on** a date and time, and/or **Stop after this many responses** (good for sign-ups with limited places). People opening the link are told why it's closed; change or clear the setting to open it again. **⋮ → Make a copy** starts a new form with the same questions; **Delete form** removes it, its responses and its link.
- **Opening later:** **Settings → Open automatically on** sets when the form starts taking answers (a course sign-up that opens Monday at 9:00, say). Until then the link shows *This form opens on Monday, 16 November at 9:00. Come back then.* in the visitor's own time zone, sending is refused, and your forms list says **Opens later**. A page left open opens the form by itself when the time comes. It has to open before it closes; clear the date to open it now.
- **Quizzes:** **Settings → Make this a quiz**, then set the right answer under each short-answer or choice question (several for tick boxes; short answers ignore capitals and extra spaces) and how many points it's worth. Each response is scored: the **Responses** tab shows the average and how many got each score, and each response its score with ✓ and ✗ per question; the CSV and Sheets copy have a **Score** column. With **Show people their score after submitting**, people see their score and which questions they got right. **Answer feedback** under a question's answer key adds a few words for a right answer and for a wrong one (*Have another look at chapter 2*), shown with the score under each question, for the answer they gave (and in the emailed copy of their answers, when the form sends one). The answers are never sent to people filling the form in.
- **Working together:** **Settings → People who can edit** lets the owner add colleagues by email. Editors see the form in their Forms list (*Shared by …*), can change its questions and settings and read and download the responses, and changes reach everyone who has it open. Only the owner adds or removes editors or deletes the form; an editor can **⋮ → Leave this form**.
- **Notifications:** the owner and editors get a bell notification for each response (turn it off per form in Settings, or for all forms in the bell's settings). Many in a row are one notification counting them (*3 new responses to “Lunch order”*, *The latest from Ana Silva*) while it's unread and less than 10 minutes old, and the same for changed responses, so a form sent to everyone doesn't fill the bell. When this server has email, each of them can also tick **Email me each response** in Settings: every new response then arrives in their own inbox, from *Workspace Forms*, with the answers (and the score on a quiz) and a link to all the responses. It's a choice per person, so an editor turning it on doesn't send mail to the owner; nobody gets an email for a response they sent themselves, and someone taken off the form stops getting them.

## Notes

**Notes** in the left bar is for quick notes and lists, kept as coloured cards:

- **Take a note…** at the top, or ✓ for a checklist (Enter adds the next item; Backspace on an empty one removes it). Clicking outside or **Close** keeps it; an empty note isn't kept.
- On a card: 📌 **pin** it to the top, 🎨 give it a colour, **archive** it (with **Undo**), tick checklist items off right there (ticked ones are counted underneath), and **⋮ → Labels…** (then pick a label on the left to see just those), **Make a copy**, **Copy to a document** (a new document in My Drive: the title as a heading, a checklist as tick boxes, then the note's pictures, each copied as *(note) – picture 1.png* and so on into a **Pictures from notes** folder in My Drive), **Copy to Tasks** (each unticked checklist item, or the note itself, becomes a task in *My tasks*) or **Delete**. Click a card to edit it; it saves as you go, and can switch between text and a checklist.
- **Pictures:** 🖼 on a card (or in an open note) adds pictures (PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP, up to 10 MB each, ten per note). They show at the top of the card, and everyone the note is shared with sees them; × on one in the open note takes it off. **Make a copy** copies the pictures too.
- **Voice recordings:** 🎙 in an open note records with your microphone. The time shows while it records (up to ten minutes); ■ adds the recording to the note and × throws it away. Each plays right there in the note (skip through it), and the card shows 🎙 *Recording 0:42* (or how many there are). A note holds up to five recordings of up to 10 MB each, and × beside one takes it off. Everyone the note is shared with can listen and add their own. **Make a copy** copies them, and **Download (.zip)** (and Download your data) puts each beside the note as *recording-1.webm*, linked from its Markdown. The server keeps only real audio (WebM, Ogg, MP4 or MP3, checked by what's in the file), and the recordings go with the note when it's deleted for good.
- **Drawing:** ✏️ on a card (or in an open note) opens a sketch pad: draw with the mouse, a pen or a finger (or drag out lines, arrows, boxes and ellipses), in six colours and four pen sizes, with an eraser, **Undo** and **Clear**. **Add to note** puts the drawing on the note as a picture, for everyone it's shared with.
- **Reminders:** 🔔 on a card sets one (later today, tomorrow morning, next Monday, or **Pick a day and time…**). It shows on the card (× takes it off), **Reminders** on the left lists the notes that have one, soonest first, and when it's time a notification (and a desktop one, if those are on) reaches you and everyone the note is shared with, listing what's still unticked on a checklist. Once a note has a reminder, the same 🔔 menu can make it **repeat daily, on weekdays, weekly or monthly** (🔁 on the card): after each one goes off the next is set, at the same time of day in your time zone (also when the clocks change; a monthly one on the 31st comes on the last day of shorter months, and stays on that day after). **Pick a day and time…** has the same **Repeat** choice (and, for a checklist, **Untick the list each time**), to set it all at once. Removing the reminder ends the repeat. On a checklist, **Untick the list each time** (in the same menu, once it repeats) starts it again whenever the reminder goes off: everything is unticked (how it was is kept in its version history) and the reminder lists the whole list. You can turn note reminders off in the bell's settings.
- **Checklists:** in an open checklist, **Tab** makes an item a sub-item of the one above (**Shift+Tab**, or Backspace on an empty one, undoes it), **Alt+↑ / Alt+↓** move an item (a main item takes its sub-items along), or drag it by its ⠿ handle. Ticking a main item ticks its sub-items too. On the card, **⋮ → Untick all items** starts the list again (a packing list for the next trip) and **Delete ticked items** clears them out. **Copy to a document** keeps sub-items nested, and **Copy unticked items to Tasks** makes each main item a task with its unticked sub-items as steps.
- **Show / hide tick boxes:** a card's **⋮ → Show tick boxes** turns a note's lines into a checklist (bullets and numbers are dropped, *[x]* or *✓* lines start ticked, and indented lines become sub-items); **Hide tick boxes** turns a checklist back into lines of text, ticked ones written *✓ …*. The note as it was is kept in its version history.
- **Your own order:** drag a card onto another to put it there (pinned ones among the pinned, the others among the others), or use **⋮ → Move earlier / Move later**. The order is yours: someone a note is shared with keeps theirs, and it's the same on all your devices. New notes go first. Cards stay put while you search or look at a label or reminders.
- **Several at once:** the ✓ at a card's corner picks it (then clicking other cards picks them too), and a bar changes the picked notes together: **📌 Pin** / **Unpin**, a **Colour**, a **Label** (one you have, or **New label…**), **Archive** (or **Unarchive** in the archive), **Print** (the picked notes on one printout, each starting a new page), **Send in Chat…** (links to them in a conversation's message box, to add a word and send; they show as note cards), **Email** (a new message with the picked notes in it, each under its title in bold with a link back to it), **Download (.zip)** (each picked note as a Markdown file named by its title, the same as a card's *Download (.md)*, in one .zip, with its pictures in a folder beside it (*Trip files/picture-1.jpg*) shown in the Markdown: to keep, or to import again later) and **Delete** (your own go to the trash; one shared with you, you leave). **Clear** stops picking. Every note is checked first: if one can't be changed, none is.
- **Version history:** a card's **⋮ → Version history** lists earlier versions of the note, newest first, each with when it was and who wrote it; pick one to see it, and **Restore this version** puts its title and text (or checklist) back. A version is kept whenever someone else changes the note (useful when it's shared), or when you come back to it after ten minutes or more, so a burst of typing is one version. Restoring keeps what was there as a version too. Up to 50 per note; colours, pins and labels don't make versions.
- **Chat with people it's shared with:** a shared note's **⋮ → Chat with people it's shared with** opens a private Chat channel for it, with its owner and everyone it's shared with, its link posted first. It's made the first time; after that it's the same channel, with anyone it was shared with since added.
- **Links to notes:** a card's **⋮ → Copy link** copies the note's address, and opening it opens the note (an archived one too). Pasted in Chat, it shows as a small card in the note's colour under the message: its title, the start of its text or the first checklist items (ticked ones crossed out) with how many are done, and whose note it is. Only the owner and the people it's shared with see it. Anyone else sees *A note you can't see*, and a note in the trash counts as gone. On a checklist's card, the first items can be ticked right there (an item with its sub-items, as in Notes), by anyone the note is shared with. A card's **⋮ → Send in Chat…** puts the note's link in a conversation's message box.
- **Send by email:** with this server's email on, a card's **⋮ → Send by email** opens a new message in Mail with the note in it: its title as the subject (or its first words), its text or checklist (☑ / ☐, sub-items indented), and a link back to the note. Add who it's for, change anything, and send.
- **Download or print a note:** a card's **⋮ → Download (.md)** saves the note as a Markdown file (its title as a heading, a checklist as `- [x]` / `- [ ]` lines with sub-items indented, then its labels), for any text editor or notes app; **⋮ → Print** prints it on its own, with its pictures. **Import notes…** on the left does the reverse for any number of Markdown (.md) or text (.txt) files, one note each: a first `# heading` becomes the title (otherwise the file's name), a file of only `- [ ]` / `- [x]` lines becomes a checklist (indented ones as sub-items), and a last line of `#tags` becomes labels, so notes downloaded here (or exported from other notes apps) come back as they were. Empty files are skipped.
- **From Google Keep:** **Import notes…** also takes the *.json* files in the *Keep* folder of a Google Takeout download (pick them all at once, up to 2,000): each becomes a note with its title, text or checklist (ticks kept), colour, pin, labels and dates; archived ones go to the Archive, and ones in Keep's trash are left out. Markdown and text files can be picked in the same go.
- **From Evernote:** **Import notes…** also takes an Evernote export (*.enex*: in Evernote, select notes or a notebook and *Export* as ENEX), with all its notes at once: each keeps its title, text (a line per paragraph, lists as bullets), tags as labels and its created and edited dates; a note made of to-dos becomes a checklist with its ticks. Pictures and attachments aren't brought over (they're marked *[attachment]*). Pictures in a note (PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP, up to 10) come in as the note's own pictures; other attachments (PDFs and the like) are marked *[attachment]* in its text.
- **From HTML (Apple Notes, OneNote, Bear…):** **Import notes…** also takes .html files, one note each, as note exporters for Apple Notes, OneNote and Bear save them (or a saved web page): the title comes from the page's title or first heading (else the file name), the text keeps a line per paragraph with lists as bullets, pictures come in as the note's own (ones written into the page, as Apple Notes exporters do, or picture files beside it in a .zip; up to 10), and a picture on the web is left marked *[picture]* rather than fetched, and a note made mostly of tick boxes becomes a checklist with the ticked ones done. Scripts and styles are left out. Google Keep's Takeout has an .html beside each .json; choose both and only the .json is used, so nothing comes in twice.
- **A .zip at once:** **Import notes…** also takes a .zip of any of these (up to 18 MB): a .zip made by **Download (.zip)**, or Google Keep's whole Takeout .zip as it comes (no need to unzip it; Takeout's own index page and Keep's label list are left out, and each note's .html copy is skipped for its .json). Files in folders inside it are found too, and a whole **Download your data** .zip works as it is: only its `Notes/` folder is read (not its chat, forms or the one-file *Notes.md*). A picture a Markdown note shows from the same .zip (`![](Trip%20files/picture-1.jpg)`) comes in as one of that note's pictures (PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP, up to 10 a note), so a downloaded .zip comes back whole; in Keep's Takeout, each note's photos (listed in its .json) come with it the same way, and a note that is only a photo becomes a note with that picture. Other files are ignored.
- **Label colours**: the ⋯ next to a label on the left picks a colour for it (red, orange, yellow, green, teal, blue, purple, pink or grey). A coloured dot shows by the label, and the label chips on its notes take the colour. Colours are your own: the people you share a note with see theirs. The same menu has **Rename label…** (on every note with it, yours and those shared with you; a name you already use joins the two, and the colour goes with it) and **Delete label** (it comes off those notes; the notes stay).
- **Grid or list:** the button beside **Search notes** switches between cards in columns (☰ for one column) and a single, wider column (▦ back to the grid). It's your own choice, kept on every device.
- **Sorting:** the menu beside **Search notes** shows notes in **Your order** (dragged), **Last edited**, **Newest first** or by **Title** (a note without one by its text), pinned ones still first. Your choice, kept on every device; dragging works in *Your order*.
- **Search notes** finds words in titles, text, checklist items and labels. **Archive** on the left has the archived ones.
- **Trash:** deleting your note moves it to **Trash** (with **Undo**), where it stays for 7 days: **Restore** puts it back as it was, **Delete forever** (or **Empty trash**) removes it for good. While it's there, people it's shared with don't see it and its reminder doesn't go off.
- **Share** (the person icon) gives colleagues in your company the note: they see it among their notes and can change it, and everyone sees changes as they're made. Only the owner can delete it or change who has it; the others can take it off their own list.

## Meet

**Meet** in the left bar starts a video meeting: **New meeting** (give it a name if you like) opens it and gives it a code like `abc-defg-hij`. Share the link (🔗, or **Copy link** before joining) or read out the code; colleagues join with **Join with a code** (typing it with or without dashes, or pasting the link). **Recent meetings** lists the ones you started or joined, with how many people are in each now, so a regular meeting can keep its link.

- **Before joining** you see your camera, can turn the microphone and camera off, and see who's already there. If there's no camera or microphone (or the browser isn't allowed to use them) you can still join to watch, listen and chat.
- **Meeting notes:** in a meeting on a calendar event, **📝** opens the event's meeting notes in a new tab, the same document as Calendar's **Take meeting notes** (everyone invited can write in it). Its organiser can start them from there if there are none yet; guests see 📝 once there are (straight away, while in the meeting).
- **After you leave:** the screen says how long you were in it, with **👥 Who joined** and, if its host kept the chat, **💬 Meeting chat** (for people with an account), beside **Rejoin**.
- **Camera, microphone and speaker:** **Camera** and **Microphone** under *Ready to join?* pick which ones to use (a headset, a second webcam), and the preview switches straight away; **Speaker** picks where you hear the others (in browsers that can choose, such as Chrome and Edge); a bar under **Microphone** moves as it hears you (to check it before joining), and **🔊 Test speaker** plays a short tone on the chosen speaker; **⚙** in the meeting has the same choices and switches without leaving (everyone keeps seeing and hearing you, muted or camera-off as you were). The choice is remembered in this browser for next time; if that device isn't plugged in, another is used.
- **In the meeting:** everyone's video in a grid (a picture of them when their camera is off), 🔇 on whoever is muted, and controls for the microphone, camera, **presenting your screen** (a window, a tab or the whole screen), **raising your hand** (outlined in yellow for everyone, and shown in **People**), quick reactions that float up over your video, the meeting **chat** (seen only by those in the meeting, and gone when it ends) and **Leave** (with **Rejoin**).
- **Pinning:** 📌 on someone's video (hover over it) shows them big, with everyone else in a strip beside them; 📌 again goes back to the grid. It's only for you. When someone presents their screen it's shown big for everyone who hasn't pinned someone else.
- **Spotlight for everyone:** whoever started the meeting can 🔦 someone's video (hover over it; themselves too) to show it big for everyone, marked **Spotlight**: a speaker, a demo. Your own 📌 pin still wins for you, and unpinning goes back to the spotlight. 🔦 again stops it; it also stops when that person leaves.
- **Who's talking:** a green ring goes round the video of whoever is speaking (yours too), and stays a moment between words. The sound is measured in your own browser; nothing more is sent. Muted people never get one.
- **Raised hands in order:** ✋ raises your hand; everyone sees the same queue, numbered in the order hands went up (✋1, ✋2 on the videos, and **Raised hands** at the top of **People**), so the host can call on people fairly. Lowering yours and raising it again puts you at the back. Whoever started the meeting can **Lower** someone's hand once they've spoken (they're told) or **Lower all**.
- **Timer:** whoever started the meeting can pick **⏱ Timer…** at the top (1 to 30 minutes) to time-box an agenda item or a break. Everyone sees the same countdown, people who join later too; it turns amber in the last 30 seconds and red with **Time's up** at the end. **Stop timer** (or **Clear timer**) takes it away for everyone.
- **Polls:** 📊 opens **Polls**: ask a question with 2–10 options and everyone in the meeting is told (the 📊 button counts polls you haven't answered). Each person votes once and can change their vote; counts and percentages update live for everyone, with your choice ticked. The asker or the host can **Close poll** to stop voting. Polls last as long as the meeting.
- **Whiteboard:** 🖍 opens a board everyone in the meeting (guests too) draws on together: a pen, lines, arrows, boxes and ellipses in six colours and four sizes, and an eraser. Each stroke appears for everyone as it's finished; people who join later see the board as it is, and anyone with it closed is told when someone starts drawing (🖍 counts the new strokes). **Undo** takes back your own last stroke, **Clear** empties the board for everyone, and **Save to Drive** keeps it as a picture in your Drive. The board lasts as long as the meeting (each breakout room has its own).
- **Q&A:** ❓ opens **Questions**: anyone in the meeting (guests too) can ask one, and everyone sees it with who asked (the ❓ button counts new ones). Others **👍 upvote** the questions they want answered (once each, not their own; click again to take it back), and the most upvoted come first. Whoever asked, or the host, can **Mark answered** (it moves to the bottom) or take it away. Each breakout room has its own, and they last as long as the meeting.
- **Breakout rooms:** whoever started the meeting can, in **People** (👥), choose how many rooms (2 to 8) and **Open rooms**: everyone else is shared out between them and moved there (they're told, and keep their camera and microphone; a blue bar says which room they're in). Each room has its own video, chat and polls. The host sees who is in each room, can **Move** someone to another room (or back to the main room, for someone who came late, say), **Go** into any room to drop in, and **Bring everyone back** to end them. Guests without an account stay in the main room.
- **End for all:** whoever started the meeting (or a co-host) has **End for all** beside **Leave**: after confirming, everyone in it (breakout rooms too, and guests still waiting) leaves and sees **The meeting has ended** and who ended it. The meeting's link still works to start it again.
- **Ask someone to join:** in **People**, type a colleague's name in **Ask someone to join…** and **📞 Ring**: their screen rings wherever they are in Workspace (*Olivia is asking you to join “Sync”*, with **Join** and **Can't talk**, unless they've turned calls popping up off) and the bell says so too. **Can't talk** tells you. Anyone in the meeting with an account can ask; people already in it aren't offered. For a meeting on a calendar event, **📞 Ring everyone invited who isn't here** (with how many) rings the organiser and the guests who haven't joined yet, except those who said no (all of them at once, however many; the count keeps up with people's answers while **People** is open). If something goes wrong partway, it says who was rung and that the others weren't.
- **Co-hosts:** whoever started the meeting can **Make co-host** next to someone in **People** (not a guest without an account) to give them the host's controls for this meeting: mute and remove people, lock the meeting, settings, the timer, the spotlight, lowering hands and breakout rooms. They're told, and marked **Co-host** for everyone. A co-host can't mute or remove the organiser, or make other co-hosts. **Remove co-host** takes the controls back.
- **Meeting settings:** whoever started the meeting has switches at the top of **People**: **Everyone joins muted** (people coming in have their microphone off, and are told so, however they set it before joining) and **Only hosts can present** (only the organiser and co-hosts can present: everyone else's 🖥 is greyed out, and anyone presenting is stopped and told why). A third, **Keep the meeting chat**, saves the chat from then on (it's off at first, and a line in everyone's chat says when it's turned on or off, with *💾 This chat is being kept* above it). Afterwards, **💬 Chat** next to the meeting in *Recent meetings* (for whoever started it and anyone who was in it, like *Who joined*) shows what was kept, with the breakout room it was said in, and **Download (.txt)**. Whoever started the meeting can **Delete chat** there (for everyone; lines written by someone on legal hold stay, and it's in the activity log). Kept chats follow the company's **Keeping chat** setting (old lines go, except those written by someone on legal hold) and come with **Download your data** (*Meet chats/*). They cover breakout rooms too, and go back to the usual when everyone has left.
- **Host controls:** whoever started the meeting can, in **People** (👥), **Mute** someone (🔇; they're told, and unmute themselves when they want to speak), **Mute everyone** else, or **Remove** someone (×, after confirming): they're told who removed them and can't join that meeting again. **🔒 Lock meeting** (at the top of **People**) stops anyone new from joining: they're told the meeting is locked, guests can't ask to join (any waiting are turned away), and everyone in it is told. Those who were in it when it was locked can come back if they drop out or move between breakout rooms. **🔓 Unlock meeting** opens it again; a locked meeting opens again by itself once everyone has left.
- **Recording:** ⏺ in the meeting controls records it in your browser: everyone's video as your grid shows it (with names), and everyone's sound. A red **… is recording this meeting** bar shows for everyone while it runs. ⏺ again (or leaving) stops it, and the video is saved as a WebM file (named after the meeting and the time) in a **Meet recordings** folder in your Drive, where it plays, and can be shared like any file. Recording uses your computer, so keep the tab open until it says it's saved.
- **From Calendar:** in an event's window, **Add video meeting** gives it a meeting (**Remove** takes it off). Guests see **Join video meeting** when they open the event, the invitation email has the link, the reminder before it starts has a **Join** button, and phones get the link with the event (when `PUBLIC_URL` is set). While people are in an event's video meeting, its window says who (*Kim Park is in it now*, beside **Join video meeting**) and Home's **Today** card shows how many (*● 2 in it*), so you can see whether others are waiting. Once it has happened, the same line has **Who joined (2)** and, if its host kept the chat, **💬 Meeting chat (5)**, for the organiser and anyone who was in it (the same windows as in Meet's *Recent meetings*).
- **From Chat:** the video button beside the message box starts a meeting, posts its link in the conversation (shown with a **Join video meeting** button for everyone) and opens it for you. While people are in it, the top of the conversation says so (*🎥 In a call: Kim, Lee* with **Join**), for any meeting link shared there in the last 12 hours; it goes when the call is over. Conversations with a call going on also get a 🎥 in the Chat list, so you see it from anywhere in Chat. In a direct message (or a private channel of up to 9 people), starting a meeting also **rings** the others: wherever they are in Workspace they see *📞 Olivia is calling you* with **Join** for 30 seconds, and it's in their bell and on their phone (tapping it opens the call). **Can't talk** instead tells the caller (*Kim Park can't talk right now*, in the meeting while they wait) and leaves *📵 Can't talk right now: I'll get back to you.* in the conversation. Don't want calls popping up? **Settings → Calls** turns that off: calls then only reach your notifications (and phone). When someone you call has notifications off (paused, quiet hours or focus time), you're told straight away (*Kim Park has notifications off until 4:30 PM: they may not see your call*). If nobody joins within about 35 seconds (and nobody said they can't talk), it's a **missed call**: *📞 Missed call from Olivia Owner* goes in the conversation and in their bell (it opens the conversation), with **Call back** on it: one click calls them back the same way. When a call rung from a conversation and answered is over (the last person leaves), *📞 Call ended · 12 min* is logged there (not for a missed call).
- **Who joined:** **Who joined** next to a meeting in *Recent meetings* (for whoever started it, or anyone who was in it) lists everyone who came: when they first joined, when they last left (or *still there*), how long they were in it and how many times they joined. **Download CSV** saves it, for a register or timesheet. With this server's email on, **Follow-up email** there opens a new message in Mail to everyone who came (*Follow-up: Weekly*), with links to the meeting notes when its calendar event has some, and to the meeting chat when its host kept it (it opens for those who were there).
- **Who can join:** people in your company, and in companies yours works with (when both allow working with other companies). Up to 8 at once.
- **Guests without an account:** anyone you send the meeting's link to (a client, say, or a guest on a calendar invitation) can open it without signing in, type their name and **Ask to join**. Everyone in the meeting from your company sees **… (guest) wants to join** with **Let in** or **Don't let in**; until then the guest only sees *Asking to join…*. Once in, they show as *Name (guest)* and can talk, present, chat, react and vote in polls, but can't record or let others in. They're listed in *Who joined*. People with an account can **Sign in** from the same page instead. Admins can turn guests off in Admin → Company settings (*Links for people without an account*). If a guest asks to join while nobody from the company is in the meeting (a booking-page call before you've opened it, say), whoever started the meeting gets **Gina Client is waiting to join “Client call”** in the bell (and on their phone), which opens the meeting. The guest sees that they've been told (*Nobody's in the meeting yet: Olivia Owner has been told you're here*); asking again within 10 minutes doesn't ring again.
- **How it works:** sound and video go directly between the people in the meeting; the server only introduces them. Browsers only allow cameras on secure pages, so use Meet through HTTPS (see *Publish it securely*). People on the same network, or on most home and office connections, connect directly. For people behind strict firewalls, run a TURN server (for example coturn) and list it in `MEET_ICE_SERVERS`.

## Chat

- **Unread:** Chat in the app bar and each conversation show how many messages you haven't read; opening the conversation clears them, on your other devices too. To come back to something later, hover over someone else's message and choose ✉ **Mark unread from here**: the conversation counts as unread from that message on (you're taken back to the list so it stays that way). Opening a conversation with unread messages shows a **New messages** line (with how many) above the first one, and starts there with a little of what came before, rather than at the very end; it stays put while you read and goes once you've opened the conversation again. When you've scrolled up to read earlier messages, what arrives meanwhile doesn't pull you down: a **↓ 2 new messages** button appears instead (your own messages don't count), which takes you to them; scrolling to the end yourself takes it away too.
- **Back online:** if the connection drops for a while (a laptop lid closed, the Wi-Fi gone), the conversation you have open brings in the messages sent meanwhile as soon as Workspace reconnects (and edits made in the meantime), without reopening it; **📥 Unreads**, if open, refreshes too. Calendar (events, tasks, colleagues' calendars, where people work), Contacts' directory, an open form's responses, a file's comments and approvals, a conversation's bookmarks, Mail's scheduled messages and notes, your status and task reminders catch up the same way.
- **Unreads:** **📥 Unreads** at the top of the conversation list (with how many unread messages there are, muted conversations aside) shows everything you haven't read on one page, by conversation, latest first and muted ones last: each with how many are new and its last 10 unread messages (thread replies too, in threads you're in). Click a message to open it where it is, or **Mark read** to clear a conversation without opening it. It follows new messages as they arrive.
- **Jump to:** **Ctrl+K** (⌘K on a Mac) in Chat, or **↪ Jump to…** at the top of the list, opens a box: type part of a channel's or person's name (or their address), choose with **↑ ↓** and press **Enter**. With nothing typed it offers your conversations with unread messages first. A colleague you haven't messaged yet is offered too, and picking them starts the direct message.
- **Keyboard:** **Alt+↑ / Alt+↓** (⌥ on a Mac) opens the conversation above or below in the list, and with **Shift** the next one with unread messages (round past the end). **↑** in an empty message box changes your last message, and **Shift+Esc** marks every conversation as read. They work while typing too; **Ctrl+/** lists them.
- **Starred:** ☆ at the top of a conversation stars it (★): starred channels and direct messages move to a **Starred** section at the top of the list, in the order you starred them, until you unstar them. It's your own list, the same on every device; nobody else's changes.
- **Drafts:** what you've typed in a conversation and not sent stays with it: switch to another and back, close the page, or open Chat on your phone, and it's still there (saved on the server as you type, for you alone). Conversations with a draft show ✏️ in the list (hover for the start of it). If you change it on one device, your others pick that up, unless you're typing in it there.
