Sending clears it everywhere; replies in a thread don't touch the conversation's draft.
- **Typing:** while someone writes in the conversation you have open, **Kim Park is typing…** shows above the message box (or above a thread's reply box, for a reply), for everyone who can read it. It goes when they send, clear the box, or stop for a few seconds. Nothing is stored.
- **Seen:** in a direct message, **Seen** appears under the latest message you sent once the other person has read it (hover for when), as it happens. In a **private channel of up to 25 people**, the latest message you sent says who has read it, as they do: *Seen by Kim Park*, *Seen by Ana and Ben*, *Seen by Ana, Ben and 3 others*, or *Seen by everyone* (hover for each person and when). **Settings → Read receipts** turns it off; then neither of you sees it for the other, and in a private channel you're left out of everyone's *Seen by* and see nobody's. Public channels and bigger private ones don't show it.
- **Reactions:** hover over a message and pick 👍, ❤️, 😂 or any emoji; click a reaction to add yours or take it back. Hover over it to see who reacted.
- **Company emoji:** your company's own emoji (a logo, a team mascot, *:ship-it:*). In the emoji button's **🏢 Company** tab, **＋ Add emoji…** takes a name and a small picture (PNG, GIF, JPEG or WebP, 128 KB at most). Then type `:ship-it:` in a message (or pick it, or search for it) and everyone in the company sees the picture; react with it like any other emoji. The same window lists them with who added each; whoever added one, or an admin, can **Remove** it (messages then show the words again). Up to 500 per company, seen only by people in it.
- **Editing:** hover over your own message and choose the pencil to change it (Enter saves, Esc cancels). Changed messages say "(edited)".
- **Mark all as read:** when anything is unread, **✓ Mark all as read** at the top of the conversation list clears every count at once (channels, private channels and direct messages), on all your devices. Messages that arrive afterwards count as usual.
- **Sections of your own:** the **Section** menu at the top of a conversation puts it in a section of your conversation list (**New section…** makes one: *Clients*, *Projects*), shown between Starred and Channels. Channels and direct messages can share a section, and each is in one at most. A section's ⋯ renames it, moves it up or down, or deletes it (its conversations go back to Channels and Direct messages). Click a section's name (▾) to fold it away: only conversations with something unread (and the one open) still show, with *N more* to open it again (▸). Sections are yours alone, on every device; starred conversations stay in Starred.
- **Download a conversation:** the ⬇ button at the top of a channel or direct message saves the whole conversation **as a web page** (reads well and prints) or **as plain text**: every message in order with who wrote it and when (in your time zone), replies set in under the message they answer, attached files by name, polls with their results, and *(edited)* where something was changed. **With its files (.zip)** gives the web page and, in a *Files* folder, every file shared in the conversation that's still there (two with the same name are numbered), up to 500 MB: to keep a project's chat with its documents, or hand it over. Anyone in the conversation can; messages still waiting to be sent aren't included.
- **Search:** the search box above the channels finds messages with all the words you type, in every channel and direct conversation you can see (thread replies too), newest first, with the words marked. Click one to jump to it, however far back it is (a reply opens its thread).
- **Channels:** **+** next to Channels makes one, open to the whole company or **private** to the people you choose (🔒). The ⚙ button in a channel sets its **topic** (anyone in it), adds people to a private channel or lets you leave it, and, for whoever made the channel or an admin, renames it, makes it private or open, takes people off, or **archives** it. An archived channel stays readable under **Show archived** but can't be written in until it's unarchived. The company-wide #general always stays open.
- **Schedule a meeting:** 📅 at the top of a direct message or a private channel opens a new event with everyone in it invited (the channel's name as its title), at the first half hour you're all free within working hours, to change and save. Once saved, it's posted in the conversation as an event card (*📅 Coffee*), so everyone there can answer from it.
- **Closing a direct message:** **Close** at the top of a direct message takes it off your list (Starred and your sections too). It comes back by itself when someone writes in it, or when you message that person again; nothing is deleted, and it's your list alone.
- **Leaving a channel:** **Leave** at the top of a public channel takes it off your list, and its new messages stop counting as unread for you (it's still there for everyone else). **Browse channels** under the channel list shows the ones you've left, to look at or **Join** again (writing in one joins it again too). Everyone stays in #general; private channels are left from their members list.
- **Announcement channels:** in a channel's ⚙, whoever made it (or an admin, also for #general) can set **Who can start conversations** to **Only some people** and pick them. Everyone else sees a note instead of the message box, and can still reply in threads and react. Admins can always post. Scheduled messages follow the same rule.
- **@mentions:** type **@** and a few letters of a name to pick a colleague (arrow keys and Enter, or click). The person gets a notice with an **Open** button wherever they are in Workspace, the conversation gets an **@** badge until they read it, and mentions of you are highlighted. Works in threads too. **@ Mentions** at the top of the conversation list gathers the messages that mention you (by name, through a group you're in, or `@channel`) from every conversation you can see, newest first, with **new** on those you haven't read (its count says how many); click one to open it where it is. **🔔 Keyword alerts…** there takes words or short phrases of your own (*deploy*, *invoice*, *Project Falcon*, up to 20): a channel message with one of them as a whole word (any case; *deploy* isn't *deployed*) tells you as a mention would, with a notification, and shows under Mentions with the word. Not in direct messages (they reach you anyway), channels you've muted or left, or your own messages.
- **Group mentions:** the same **@** list offers the company's groups (from Admin → Groups, written as their name with dashes, e.g. `@field-sales`) and `@channel` (everyone in the conversation). Everyone in the group who is in the conversation gets the mention; people outside it aren't told. Not in direct messages.
- **Threads:** hover over a message and choose **Reply in thread** to answer it beside the conversation, so side discussions don't bury everything else. The message shows how many replies it has, who replied and when the last came; click that to open the thread. Replies in a thread only count as unread for people in it (who started it or replied). Deleting a message that has replies leaves *This message was deleted.* in its place, so the thread stays (reactions and pins go; it can't be edited); once its last reply is deleted too, it goes altogether. **💬 Threads** at the top of the conversation list gathers every thread you're in (you wrote the message or replied), from all your channels and direct messages, latest reply first: the message, where it is, how many replies, the last one, and how many are **new** to you. Its count says how many threads have new replies; click one to open it in its conversation. **Follow** at the top of an open thread puts you in a thread you haven't written in (its replies count as unread for you and it joins your Threads); **✓ Following** unfollows one, even your own, so a busy side discussion stops counting for you (replying again doesn't follow it again: click **Follow** for that).
- **Slash commands:** type **/** at the start of the message box for a list (↑ ↓ to move, Tab or Enter to pick, Escape to close it): **/meet** starts a video meeting and shares it here (as the camera button does), **/poll Lunch? | Pizza | Sushi** asks a question with options, **/task Book the venue** adds a task to your list, linked back to the conversation, and **/status 🍔 Lunch 1h** sets your status without posting anything (an emoji first if you like, then the words, then how long: *30m*, *2 hours*, *for 90 minutes*, *today* or *this week*; today when you don't say). **/status** on its own clears it. **/event Planning friday 3pm for 30m** opens it in Calendar the way the Calendar's quick add reads it (a day, time, length, repeat, *@people*, *in the Boardroom*), inviting everyone in a direct message or private channel and, in an open channel, only the people named with *@*; saving posts its card in the conversation. **/event** alone finds the first time you're all free, as 📅 does. **/dnd** pauses notifications for an hour (**/dnd 30m**, **/dnd 2 hours**, **/dnd tomorrow** until 9:00, up to a month), as the account menu's *Pause notifications* does, and **/dnd off** turns them back on. Anything else starting with / (*/shrug*) is sent as it is. They work in a thread's reply box too: the meeting link and the poll become replies in the thread, and the task links back to the thread itself.
- **Formatting:** type `*bold*`, `_italic_`, `~strike~`, `` `code` ``, a block between ```` ``` ```` lines, or start a line with `> ` to quote. Messages stay plain text (so search and notifications see the words), and ordinary text such as `2 * 3`, file_names and web addresses is left as it is.
- **Your status:** the account menu (your picture, top right) → **Set a status**: an emoji and a few words (or a suggestion such as 📅 In a meeting or 🌴 On holiday), cleared after 30 minutes, an hour, 4 hours, today, this week, or never. Colleagues see it at once next to your name in Chat (your direct messages list, and under your name at the top of a conversation with you) and in Contacts → Directory; your picture shows the emoji. **Clear status** removes it. Statuses you've typed yourself are kept under **Recent** (your last five, latest first, each with how soon you had it cleared), so setting *Lunch, 1 hour* again is one click; **×** forgets one.
- **Your photo:** Settings → **Add a photo** (any picture; it's cropped to a square in your browser). Colleagues then see it instead of your initials: in Chat, Contacts → Directory, Drive sharing and details, Tasks, Admin, the top bar, mail from you, and next to your name when you edit a file together. It changes everywhere at once. **Remove photo** brings your initials back. People in other companies you work with can see it too.
- **Muting:** 🔔 at the top of a channel or direct conversation mutes it for you: its messages still arrive and show their count in the list (greyed, with 🔕), but no longer add to Chat's unread count in the app bar. Mentions of you still do. Mute it **for 1 hour**, **for 8 hours**, **until tomorrow morning** (8:00), **for a week**, or **until I unmute**: a mute for a while shows until when (*🔕 until today at 3:00 PM*) and ends by itself. Click 🔕 to unmute sooner.
- **Links to messages:** hover over a message and choose 🔗 **Copy link to message** for its address (a reply in a thread opens with its thread). Pasted in a conversation, the link shows the message quoted under it: who wrote it, where (*#launch*, or just *a private channel* or *a direct message*), when, and the start of what it says; click it to go to the message. People who aren't in that conversation see *A message you can't see* instead, never what it says. The link also works pasted in an email, a document or a task.
- **Forwarding:** hover over a message and choose ↪ **Forward**, pick another conversation (a channel or a direct message) and add a note if you like. It arrives as a card showing who wrote it, in which channel (direct messages and private channels aren't named) and when, with its files, which everyone there can open. Forwarding a forward without a note passes on the original. Polls can't be forwarded.
- **Saved for later:** hover over a message and choose 🔖 to save it for yourself (only you see what you save). **🔖 Saved** at the top of the conversation list shows them, last saved first, with where each is; click one to jump to it (in its thread if it's a reply), or **×** to take it off. Messages in a conversation you've left drop out of the list.
- **Link previews:** a web link in a message gets a small card under it with the page's site, title and description, for everyone in the conversation (it arrives a moment after the message; editing the message updates it). The server looks the page up, only on the public internet (never addresses on its own network), reads just its title and description, and loads no pictures from it, so nobody's browser contacts the linked site until they click. Links to files in Workspace show as file cards instead.
- **Voice messages:** 🎙 beside the message box records one with your microphone (the time shows while recording, up to five minutes); ■ stops and adds it to the message, where you can listen to it before sending, and × throws it away. Everyone in the conversation plays it right there, and can skip through it. Like other files, it's kept in your **Chat files** folder in Drive.
- **Bookmarks:** **+ Bookmark** under a conversation's name adds a link to the bar there, for everyone in it: a document, sheet or folder (paste its Workspace address; it opens right there) or a website (opens in a new tab), with a name or one taken from the address. Anyone in the conversation can add one (up to 20) or remove one with ×; changes show for everyone at once. Archived channels keep theirs, read-only.
- **Files in a conversation:** **📎** at the top of a channel or direct message lists every file in it, newest first: what was attached (while it's still in Drive) and the documents, spreadsheets, presentations, folders and Drive files linked in messages (only those you can open), each once, with its size or kind, who shared it and when. **Find a file by name** narrows the list, and the buttons under it show one sort at a time: **Pictures**, **Documents** (documents, spreadsheets and presentations, and Word, Excel, PowerPoint, OpenDocument and text files), **PDFs**, **Audio & video** (voice messages too) or **Other**; clicking a name opens it (an attachment from its message, a document in Docs); **Show** jumps to the message it came in, in its thread if it was a reply. Deleted messages are left out.
- **Pinned messages:** hover over a message and choose 📌 to pin it to the conversation for everyone in it (anyone in the conversation can pin and unpin; thread replies can't be pinned). Pinned messages are marked, and **📌** at the top of the conversation lists them, most recently pinned first: click one to jump to it, or **Unpin** it there. Up to 50 per conversation.
- **Polls:** **📊** beside the message box asks a question with 2–10 options (tick *People can choose more than one* to allow several). Everyone in the conversation votes with a click, sees the counts and who chose what update live, and can change or take back their vote. Whoever asked can **Close poll** (no more voting) and reopen it. Polls can't be edited.
- **Scheduled messages:** write a message and choose **🕒** beside **Send** to send it later: tomorrow morning or afternoon, Monday morning, or a date and time you pick (within a year). It goes at that time as if you sent it then, even if Workspace isn't open. **🕒 N scheduled messages** under the box lists them, with **Send now** and **Delete**. If one can't be sent when it's due (the channel was archived, you left it, a file is gone), it stays in the list with the reason and you get a notification.
- **After their working hours:** in a direct message with a colleague who has set working hours (Calendar → Working hours) and whose day is over, a note above the message box says so with their local time (*🌙 It's 23:10 for Kim Park, outside their working hours*). **Send when their day starts (…)** schedules what you've written for then, as a scheduled message (it shows under *🕒 1 scheduled message*). Sending now still works as usual. Likewise, when their **Vacation reply** (Mail) is on today, the note says *🌴 Kim Park is away until 16 Nov (“Hiking in the Alps”)*. In a channel, @mentioning colleagues who are away or outside their working hours shows the same above the box as you write (*🌙 Kim Park: it's 23:10 for them, outside their working hours*), so you know before you send.
- **Remind me:** hover over a message and choose the clock to be reminded about it: in 20 minutes, in an hour, tomorrow morning or afternoon, Monday morning, or a time you pick. Then you get a notification that leads back to the message. The message shows ⏰ until then, with **Cancel**. Only you see your reminders. **⏰ Reminders** at the top of the conversation list (with how many) lists every reminder you've set, soonest first, with when and where; click one to open its message, or ✕ to cancel it (also at `/chat?reminders=1`, which Home's Chat reminders card opens). Only reminders in conversations you can still see are listed.
- **Cards keep up:** cards for notes, tasks, events and forms change by themselves while the conversation is open when someone ticks, answers, responds or edits them somewhere else (a form's response count, which only its owner and editors see, too).
- **Send in Chat from a document:** in a document, spreadsheet or presentation, **⋮ → Send in Chat…** puts its link in the message box of the conversation you pick and opens it there; it shows as a card. With this server's email on, the same menu has **Email link** (a new email with a link to it) and **Email as attachment…** (Mail asks whether to attach it as Word, Excel, PowerPoint, PDF… or put in a link; not offered to viewers who can't download it).
- **File cards:** a link to a Workspace document, spreadsheet, presentation, folder or uploaded file in a message (pasted from the address bar or Drive's **Copy link**, or just /docs/…) shows as a card with its name, kind, owner and what you can do with it. Click it to open the file. If it isn't shared with you, the card says so without showing the name and takes you to **Request access**.
- **Sharing what you link:** when you send a link to a Drive file that some people in the conversation can't open, a note says who. If the file is yours, **Share with them** lets them view it (in a company-wide channel, **Share with everyone at** your company); if not, ask its owner.
- **Webhooks:** in a channel's ⚙, whoever made it (or an admin) can **Add webhook** with a name (*Build server*, *Uptime monitor*). That gives a secret address, shown once, that posts into the channel: send it `{"text": "…"}` as JSON (the format most tools use) or plain text with POST, for example `curl -X POST -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"text":"Build passed"}' https://…/hooks/chat/…`. Its messages show the webhook's name with an **App** tag. Each takes up to 30 messages a minute; **Remove** stops its address working at once, and an archived channel takes none. A channel can have ten.
- **Keeping chat (admins):** Admin → Company settings → **Keeping chat** keeps messages for good (the default), 5 years, a year, 90 days or 30 days. Older messages, with their threads, reactions and polls, are deleted every hour, in the company's channels and in direct messages with its people (if two companies' settings differ, the shorter one applies). Files sent in chat stay in Drive. The change is in the activity log.
- **Moving from Slack (admins):** **Admin → Company → Keeping chat → Import from Slack…** takes the .zip a Slack workspace export gives (in Slack: *Workspace settings → Import/export data → Export*). First it shows what would come in (each channel with how many messages, which are renamed, and who isn't here) and asks before importing; untick the channels you don't want brought over. Each public channel comes in as a channel here (*name (Slack)* when the name is taken), with its topic, messages, dates, threads, pins, edits and reactions (from people who are here; the usual emoji such as 👍 🎉 🔥 ✅, while a workspace's own custom emoji can't come over); Slack's mentions and links become plain text, and attached files are named (their contents aren't in the export). People are matched to yours by email, so their messages are theirs; anyone who isn't here keeps their Slack name on what they wrote. Nobody gets the history as unread, and the import is in the activity log.
- **Pictures and files:** the paper clip attaches files (or drop or paste them into the message box). They're saved in a "Chat files" folder in your Drive, and everyone in the conversation can open them from the message, even without access to the folder. Pictures show in the conversation. Clicking a picture, PDF, text file, video, Word, Excel or PowerPoint file, or a zip opens it in a viewer right there (Office files read-only as Docs, Sheets or Slides would show them; a zip as a list of what's inside), with ← → (or the arrow keys) through the message's other files and **Download**. Other kinds download, and Shift+click downloads anything. **Save to Drive** (the folder button when you hover over a colleague's message with files, **Save** beside their attachments in **📎**, or the button in the viewer) puts your own copy in Drive: **Save to My Drive** at once, or **Save to a folder…** to pick one of your folders or a shared drive you can edit; **Show in Drive** then finds it; it counts towards your storage, keeps its name (numbered if taken) and stays when the original is deleted. A file its owner has since deleted or put in the trash shows struck through as *no longer available* in the message (it isn't offered to open or save; back from the trash, it's back). Open conversations and threads change by themselves as it happens, whether it was trashed in Drive or through the network drive.

## Notifications

The bell at the top right collects what needs you: being **@mentioned** in Chat or in a comment, a **task** given to you, a calendar **invitation** (or its cancellation), a file or folder **shared** with you, and someone **booking** time with you. New ones appear at once, with a count on the bell. **Bursts are grouped:** while one is unread and less than 10 minutes old, more of the same make it one notification counting them, at the top again (and your phone isn't buzzed again): files shared with you by one person (*Olivia Owner shared 3 files with you*, opening **Shared with me**), tasks one person gives you on one list (*gave you 3 tasks*, opening the list), comments one person leaves on one file, responses to one of your forms, and files sent through one file request by one person. **Show all 3** under a grouped one opens it up: each of them (the latest 20, newest first), each going to its own file, task or comment. Mentions are never grouped. Click one to go there (it's then marked read), or **Mark all read**. The latest 200 are kept.


**Notification settings** (⚙ in the bell's panel) turns each kind on or off for you: mentions in Chat and documents, mentions in file comments, tasks, calendar invitations and answers, shared files and access requests, changes to files you follow, chat reminders, and bookings. Kinds you turn off aren't kept, so they don't turn up later either.

**Pause notifications** (account menu, top right: for 30 minutes, 1 or 2 hours, or until 9:00 tomorrow) stops the pop-up notes and desktop notifications for new mail, mentions, event reminders and tasks until then. Everything still arrives in the bell and the apps. 🔕 by the bell shows it's on (click to resume), and colleagues see 🔕 by your name in Chat.

**Quiet hours** (Settings → Quiet hours) pause notifications by themselves outside your working hours (set in Calendar → Working hours): pop-up notes and desktop notifications wait until your next working time, colleagues see 🔕 by your name, and everything still arrives in the bell. The 🔕 by the bell then says *Quiet hours until …* and leads to Settings.

**Chat emails** (Settings → Chat emails, when this server has email): **Email me about unread direct messages, mentions and keywords** sends you an email when a direct message to you, a message mentioning you, or one with one of your Chat keywords (named in the email: *“invoice” in #general*) has waited half an hour unread (or, from **After it has waited** under it, 15 minutes, an hour or 3 hours). Several are gathered into one email ("3 unread chat messages for you") with who said what and where, and a link to Chat. Messages you read in the meantime are left out, and so are ones deleted meanwhile and ones in a conversation you can no longer see (a private channel you were taken out of, or one you left), and nothing is sent during your quiet hours or while notifications are paused (it comes once they're over). The email comes from your own mailbox to your inbox. Turning it off drops anything still waiting.

**Morning summary** (Settings → Morning summary, when this server has email): an email each day at the hour you choose (in your time zone) with today's events (repeating ones included; ones you declined left out, unanswered invitations marked), the tasks due today or overdue, how many chat messages are waiting, open comments for you (where someone @mentioned you, or assigned to you, as on Home; up to six), and what others changed in Drive in the last day (files you can open, each with what was done and by whom: *“Budget”: edited by Kim, Len; renamed by Kim*; up to eight, the rest on Drive → **Activity**). It comes from your own mailbox to your inbox. Under **What's in it**, untick the parts you don't want (*Today's events*, *Tasks due or overdue*, *Contacts' birthdays and dates* (whose birthday or anniversary it is today), *Unread chat messages*, *Comments for you*, *What others changed in Drive*); at least one stays. **Weekdays only** skips Saturdays and Sundays (in your time zone). **Only when there's something in it** skips days when the parts you chose are all empty (no events, tasks, unread chat, comments for you or Drive changes). It doesn't come on days you're on approved time off (a half day still gets one). Each part has a link to open it in Workspace (the calendar, My Day, Chat, each comment, each changed file), using the server's address (`PUBLIC_URL`). **Send me one now** shows it and sends it straight away.
## Branding

In **Admin → Company settings → Branding**, a company's admins can upload a **logo** (PNG, JPEG or WebP, up to 1 MB; SVG isn't accepted, as it can carry scripts) and pick a **colour**. Everyone in the company then sees the logo in the top bar and the colour on buttons, highlights and links. The sign-in page shows the logo and colour when Workspace is opened at an address on one of the company's domains, such as `workspace.acme.com` (set up the address in DNS and your reverse proxy); other addresses show the plain Workspace sign-in.

## Light and dark

Workspace follows your computer's or phone's light or dark setting. To choose for yourself, go to **Settings → Appearance** and pick **Light** or **Dark** (or **System** to follow the device again). The choice is kept on that device and applies from the moment a page opens.

## Keyboard shortcuts

Press **Ctrl+/** (**⌘/** on a Mac), or **?** outside a text field, for the list of keyboard shortcuts, with the ones for the app you're in first.

- **Go to an app:** anywhere outside a text field, **G** then a letter (within a second and a half) opens an app: **H** Home, **M** Mail, **D** Drive, **C** Calendar, **K** Contacts, **T** Tasks, **A** Chat, **N** Notes, **F** Forms, **S** Settings. In Mail, **G** then **C** goes to Calendar rather than starting a message.
- **Drive:** in the file list (rows or tiles), **↓** / **↑** (or **J** / **K**), **Home** and **End** move between the files, selecting each (the focus moves with them); **Enter** opens one, **Space** or **X** ticks it (to act on several), **S** stars or unstars it, **F2** renames it, **Delete** or **#** moves it to the trash (with **Undo**), and **U** goes up to the folder it's in. The arrows, Home, End, F2 and Delete keep working with single keys turned off.
- **Calendar:** **T** goes to today; **D**, **W**, **M**, **Y** and **A** switch to the day, week, month, year and schedule views; **J** or **N** goes to the next day, week or month (whichever you're looking at) and **K** or **P** to the previous one; **C** starts a new event. **←** / **→** still move between weeks and days in those views.
- **Contacts:** **J** / **K** go to the next or previous person in the list (the focus moves with them), **Enter** or **O** opens them (a contact to edit, or a colleague's card in the Directory), **S** stars or unstars a contact, **#** deletes it (after asking; it goes to Trash) and **C** starts a new contact.
- **Notes:** **J** / **K** go to the next or previous note (the focus moves with them), **Enter** opens it, **F** pins or unpins it, **E** archives it (or brings it back, in Archive), **#** moves it to the trash (with **Undo**; a note shared with you asks before it's removed from your notes) and **C** takes a new note.
- **Tasks:** in a list, **J** / **K** go to the next or previous task (the focus moves with them, so a screen reader reads each), **Enter** or **O** opens it, **X** ticks it off (the focus goes on to the next), **S** marks it important or not, **#** deletes it (with **Undo**) and **N** goes to **Add a task**.
- **Turning single keys off:** **Settings → Keyboard shortcuts** turns off every shortcut that's a single key (**G** then a letter, Mail's **C**, **J**/**K**, **R**, **E**, **#** and the rest, and **?** for the list), for people who press keys by accident or use speech input. Shortcuts with Ctrl or ⌘ (such as Ctrl+/ for the list) keep working. It's kept with your account, on every device. **/** (outside a text field) jumps to the search box at the top, except in Mail, where it searches mail.

## Backups

Back up `docker/workspace/data` and `docker/workspace/mailboxes`. (`mail-config` is regenerated by the app at every start.)

`docker/workspace/data` contains:

- `files/`: uploaded files
- `workspace.db`: companies, users, docs, sheets, calendar, contacts and chat
- `backups/`: a consistent database snapshot written daily (the last 7 are kept). It includes each domain's DKIM key and the webmail secret.

**Admin → System** (platform owners) lists the snapshots kept, with their dates and sizes, and has **Take a snapshot now** (before updating, say; today's is replaced) and **Download** for each, to keep a copy elsewhere. Taking and downloading snapshots are in the activity log. A snapshot holds password hashes and mail-signing keys, so keep downloaded copies somewhere safe.

**Restore…** next to a snapshot puts it back: type its date to confirm, and Workspace restarts with it (Docker starts it again by itself, thanks to `restart: unless-stopped`), so everything is as it was then: people, documents, calendars, contacts and chat. The page reloads when it's back, the platform owners get a notification, and it's in the activity log. The database as it was just before is kept among the snapshots (*workspace-…-before-restore-….db*), so a restore can itself be undone the same way. Uploaded files aren't in snapshots: ones added since stay in `data/files` but aren't listed, and ones deleted since can't come back (Hyper Backup covers those).

**Uploaded files** (Admin → System → **Check files**) compares `data/files` with what Workspace lists: files nothing refers to, neither the database nor any kept snapshot (so restoring one still works), are **unused**, left over after a restore or a crash, and **Remove them** deletes them (files written in the last hour are left alone, as an upload may be finishing; it's in the activity log). Files Workspace lists that aren't on the NAS are shown as **missing**, by name and owner, to copy back from a Hyper Backup or delete in Drive.

Point **Hyper Backup** at both folders, ideally to an off-site target. To restore, stop the project, copy the files back (replace `workspace.db` with a snapshot from `backups/` if needed) and start it again.

## Updating

Upgrading from v0.1 (before companies existed) is automatic. Existing users are moved into a company called "My company" with addresses like `alice@workspace.local`, and the first admin becomes the platform owner. Afterwards, add your real domain under Admin → All companies → Domains. Changing existing users' addresses isn't built in yet; it's on the roadmap.

To update, replace the files in `docker/workspace/app` with the new version. Then in Container Manager, go to Project → workspace → **Action → Build**. Your data folder is untouched. (Updating from a version without **Outgoing mail queue** on Admin → System: create the `workspace/mail-status` folder first; the new `docker-compose.yml` gives it to both containers.)

## Configuration (environment variables)

| Variable | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `PORT` | `8080` | HTTP port inside the container |
| `DATA_DIR` | `/data` | Where the database and files live |
| `MAX_UPLOAD_MB` | `4096` | Largest single upload |
| `KEEP_VERSIONS` | 50 | Earlier versions kept per file in Drive |
| `AUDIT_DAYS` | 365 | Days the admin activity log is kept (at least 7) |
| `MAIL_IMPORT_ALLOW_PRIVATE` | off | Set to `1` to let people import mail from a server on your own network (by default only internet mail servers are allowed) |
| `SECURE_COOKIES` | auto | Force `true` or `false`; auto means Secure when the request is HTTPS |
| `PUBLIC_URL` | the address the browser used | The web address people outside use (e.g. `https://workspace.example.com`), for links in invitation emails |
| `TRUST_PROXY` | `loopback, linklocal, uniquelocal` | Which proxies' `X-Forwarded-*` headers to trust |
| `MAIL_CONFIG_DIR` | unset (mail off) | Folder shared with the mail container; setting it turns email on |
| `MAIL_STATUS_DIR` | unset | Where the mail container reports its outgoing queue (`/mail-status` in docker-compose.yml); without it, Admin → System doesn't show the queue |
| `MAIL_HOSTNAME` | unset | Public name of the mail server (MX target, certificate name) |
| `MAIL_IMAP_HOST` | `mail` | Where the app reaches the mail container (IMAP 993, SMTP 465) |
| `AUTH_MAX_FAILURES` / `AUTH_WINDOW_MIN` / `AUTH_BAN_MIN` | `10` / `15` / `60` | Sign-in protection: failures allowed per address, time window, first block length |
| `AUTH_ACCOUNT_FAILURES` | `20` | Failures on one account (from any address) before its sign-ins are slowed down |
| `AUTH_ALLOWLIST` | empty | Extra never-block addresses or ranges, comma-separated (also editable in Admin → Security) |
| `AUTH_SOFT_NETS` | private ranges | Ranges that are only slowed down, never blocked; set it empty to make LAN addresses blockable too |
| `AUTH_POLICY_PORT` / `AUTH_POLICY_URL` | `8081` / `http://workspace:8081/` | Internal sign-in policy service, and how the mail container reaches it |
| `MEET_ICE_SERVERS` | none | Extra STUN/TURN servers for Meet, as JSON, e.g. `[{"urls":"turn:turn.example.com:3478","username":"u","credential":"p"}]` |
| `MAIL_DNS_SERVERS` | system | Comma-separated DNS servers for the "Check DNS" button and the blocklist check, e.g. `192.168.1.1` (your router) or `1.1.1.1,8.8.8.8`; Spamhaus refuses lookups through big public services such as those, so use your own resolver if you want its answer |

## Development

```bash
cd server && npm install && npm run dev        # API on :8080 (data in server/data)
cd web && npm install && npm run dev           # UI on :5173, proxies /api and /ws to :8080
cd server && npm test                          # API tests
cd web && npm test                             # formula engine tests
cd web && npm run lint                         # bugs a linter finds (hooks out of order, undefined names…); also run by both test suites
cd server && npm run lint
```

**Browser tests** (`e2e/`): about 700 scripts, each driving the real app in Chromium the way a person would (signing up, clicking, typing, dragging, checking what shows), several with accessibility checks (axe-core, light and dark). Each runs against its own fresh server and data:

```bash
cd web && npx vite build                                 # what the tests open
cd e2e && npm install && npx playwright install chromium
bash run.sh reqgroup.mjs                                 # one test: "reqgroup.mjs: 4 passed, 0 failed"
bash run.sh searchquick2.mjs --mail                      # one that needs the mail server (below)
JOBS=3 bash run-all.sh                                   # all of them, 3 at a time (mail ones one by one after)
```

Those listed in `e2e/mail-tests.txt` need this machine's mail server, as for the mail integration test below (`run-all.sh` passes `--mail` for them; `--mail` clears its mailboxes, using sudo). Screenshots, downloads, the server's log and each test's output go to `e2e/out/<test>/`. A test is a plain script: `step('what it checks', async () => { … })` lines print `PASS` or `FAIL`. A test that needs the server set up differently says so in its first lines: `// e2e-env: NAME=value` (such as a pretend DNS server's address), or `// e2e-restart` (the server is started again when it stops, as Docker does after a restore).

The mail integration test runs the app against a real Postfix, Dovecot and Rspamd. On Ubuntu 24.04, install `postfix dovecot-imapd dovecot-lmtpd dovecot-sieve rspamd`, apply the build steps from `mail/Dockerfile`, start `mail/entrypoint.sh` as root, then run:

```bash
cd server && MAIL_IT_CONFIG_DIR=/etc/nw npm run test:mail
```

## Roadmap

Everything planned so far is built.
